Four findings (`CR-001`-`CR-004`): `CR-001` (the `trips/{tripId}` Firestore rule has no field-level write restriction, so an Editor could in principle rewrite `ownerUid`/`editorEmails` via a raw write bypassing the app UI) and `CR-004` (`src/legacy-app.js`/`src/v4-app.js`, 142 lines, are confirmed fully dead code — unreachable from the app's real entry point, `src/main.js`, which only imports `src/v5-app.js`) are both recommended fixed before Beta, small and self-contained. `CR-002` (Smart Import's otherwise-solid, genuinely DNS-resolution-based SSRF defense re-resolves DNS a second time when it actually connects, leaving a narrow DNS-rebinding window) and `CR-003` (the daily Smart Import quota check is a non-atomic read-then-write, so near-simultaneous requests could overshoot the cap by a request or two) are both real but recommended left in the backlog — neither is a live security boundary. Everything else reviewed (`storage.rules`, both auth-verification helpers, the privileged account-deletion function, Trip-ownership reconciliation, Places API integration, and the app's `esc()`-escaping discipline across every sampled user/extracted-content interpolation) was found solid, no issues. **Awaiting the Product Owner's call** on whether to scope `CR-001`+`CR-004` as a standalone pre-Beta correction candidate.**Superseded (two further post-MVP backlog items added, `V139`, 2026-09-28):**

**Status:** **`0.7.8` (Transport Smart Import) closed and live in Production, 2026-09-27 (build `0.7.8-5010c543`).** This assistant independently verified closure directly against the actual repo (`git log`/`git show` on the full `0.7.8` commit sequence — `507024c` round-1 implementation, `ec71dd3` round-1 acceptance record (NO-GO), `f1dbb2e`/`d0b53ea` round-2 fix and record (NO-GO), `649f59d`/`f3e734f` round-3 fix and record (NO-GO), `5010c54`/`b03daad` round-4 fix and record (GO), `3b91498` GO decision, `0b06d63` Production promotion — and the complete text of `docs/versions/V7/0.7.8/EXECUTABLE_TEST_SESSION_V1.md`), not the relayed closure report alone. **This correction went four rounds, all on a single blocking finding (Test 4, rail misclassification), a genuinely hard root-cause chase, not a simple fix cycle:** round 1 found `TR-005` (a real Deutsche Bahn rail ticket) classified as Attraction, not Transport. Round 2's prompt-reweighting fix left the type field unchanged but surfaced a new self-contradiction warning ("this is a rail ticket, not an attraction ticket") the model itself generated but couldn't act on. Round 3 root-caused the structural disconnect (classification and extraction are separate, uncoupled model calls; the warning has no causal path to the type field) and raised classification's token budget/reasoning effort — the type field still didn't change, and the round-2 warning inexplicably vanished. Round 4, following this assistant's explicit instruction not to guess a third time and instead capture direct live-model evidence first, found the real cause via 47 repeated live-model samples: a genuine model bias in the crowded five-way classification prompt (`activity` was the plurality raw answer, ~36%, vs `car`'s ~30% — not sampling noise around a correct center, since more resampling of the same prompt converges on the wrong answer). Fixed with a separate, focused binary "is this Transport?" gate model call, run in parallel with the five-way classifier, validated with 8/8 correct trials on `TR-005` and 8/8 correct rejections on real Attraction/Insurance documents before shipping. **Final tally: 7 of 8 tests PASS** (`website`/`email` fields, manual/free-text classification heuristic, rental-car extraction, rail extraction as of round 4, ferry/boat extraction, transfer-exclusion with manual creation unaffected, and general regression); **1 test (`WC-001`, the mandatory wild-card) not independently verified but investigated and understood** — it produces no proposal at all on this composite photographed document, confirmed by the Development Agent as a genuine, pre-existing recognition gap never previously tested (not something `0.7.8` introduced or could fix), logged and left open for separate scheduling, not blocking this candidate. **One documentation-accuracy correction for this project's own historical record:** the `0.7.8` scope document and this assistant's own earlier framing both stated rental-car Smart Import extraction was "already live since `0.7.4`" — the Product Owner corrected this directly during acceptance testing: it was not actually available before `0.7.8`; Test 3 was a first-time test of new functionality, not a regression check. Noted here so this doesn't propagate uncorrected. Transfer remains out of scope for Smart Import extraction and fully usable manually, unaffected. Test 2 (`V7-F08`'s own "reset Trip data" action) FAILED outright in round 1 — the new `deleteAllAttachments()` helper's `listAll()` call had no matching Storage `list` rule, so both delete-style actions failed identically; round 1's rules fix (commit `9c2f67d`) did not resolve it, since `listAll()` is not recursive besides (files sit one path segment deeper than the prefix it queried) — **root-caused for real only in round 2** (commit `820c100`), which abandoned the `listAll()`-based prefix sweep entirely in favor of reading each source's already-known `storagePath` directly from Firestore, needing no Storage `list` permission at all; confirmed PASS on retest. Test 3 (Storage cleanup on full Trip deletion) initially showed leftover `sources/` files after round 2's fix — investigated directly against the code (round 2's fix confirmed correct and shared by both delete paths, no second bug found) and closed as **pre-existing debris**, not a defect: `deleteAllAttachments()` can only clean up a file with a surviving Firestore record pointing at it, and the leftover files predated this candidate's own fix, on the long-reused QA Test Trip — confirmed by a clean retest on a genuinely fresh Trip (round 3 build). Test 4 (cross-device deletion visibility) PASS, confirmed independently of Test 3's finding. Test 5 (offline upload fails clearly, not silently) needed three further rounds: round 3 (commit `14ce6de`) fixed a genuinely pre-existing unhandled-promise-rejection bug in `createLocalSource()` (neither `smartIngest()` nor `attachIngest()` caught its thrown error, a fully silent no-op click) but retested still FAIL, identically silent; round 4 investigation (commit `b625390`) found two further real causes — the app's own busy-spinner UI never rendered during the upload (no `render()` call between setting `ingestBusy=true` and the `await`), and the Storage SDK's own multi-minute upload-retry window could delay the eventual alert regardless — fixed both (`navigator.onLine` fail-fast plus an immediate `render()`); retested and the alert now appeared, but exposed a **new, adjacent bug**: a busy spinner left stuck on screen after the failure alert, since the null-source branch did a bare `return` instead of closing the modal like every other exit path; **root-caused and fixed in round 5** (commit `9f63dad`), retested and confirmed PASS. **A process incident occurred mid-session, unrelated to the product:** the actual QA Test Trip was accidentally permanently deleted during Test 3's first retest (a full-Trip delete run against it instead of a disposable Trip, contrary to the test's own instructions) — the Product Owner confirmed this and created a replacement; not a code defect, recorded for the record. **Promoted to Production, 2026-09-26** — the exact, already-`qa`-accepted artifact, no rebuild; Netlify reported "0 files/0 functions uploaded," confirmed byte-identical via a fresh fetch. Rollback backup preserved at `production-promotion-backup-20260926-090725/`. **A real design deviation occurred during round 2 and is worth recording explicitly, not just noting the fix worked:** the approved `DESIGN_V1.md` (and this assistant's own kickoff prompt) specified a Storage-side `listAll()` prefix sweep specifically because, in the design's own words, it "catches any Storage object under this Trip regardless of whether its owning source record still exists in Firestore" — a real advantage over a Firestore-record-driven approach. Round 2's fix abandoned that approach for a sound, well-documented reason (`listAll()` is genuinely not recursive at this path depth, and Firebase's own `list`-rule semantics couldn't safely be scoped here either way) and reverted to reading each source's known `storagePath` from Firestore instead — the same pattern `deleteTripCompletely` already used successfully before this candidate touched it. This is confirmed, by this assistant's own direct reading of both the code diff and the test session document, to be an accepted, self-aware tradeoff, not a silently reintroduced gap: Test 3's own investigation explicitly names the consequence (an orphan whose Firestore record is already lost can no longer be found and cleaned up by this mechanism) and confirms it matches exactly the debris this fix itself stops from accumulating going forward. `ARCHITECTURE_CURRENT_STATE.md` resynced to `V11` the same day (commit `00abd0e`) — this assistant read the complete file directly (not just the diff) and confirms it accurately reflects `0.7.6`'s actual outcome, including this same tradeoff, and synced this assistant's own project-store copy to match. `0.7.6` is closed. Wired into the admin panel as a "reactivate" button, independent of the 30-day deletion-eligibility window. **`V7-F12`'s real root cause differs from this assistant's own guess in the kickoff, as expected — the kickoff deliberately didn't lock in that guess.** Actual cause, confirmed by reading the diff directly: `listMyTrips`'s "shared with me" query is evaluated under `firestore.rules`' `hasTripAccess()`, which requires `isInvited()` (the calling account's own application-level invitation) regardless of whether `editorEmails` lists them — a Trip Owner adding an Editor via Trip Settings could silently create an Editor with zero Trip visibility, landing on a dead-end empty-Trip screen. Fixed at the point the gap is created: the add-editor handler now checks the added email's invitation status and warns the Trip Owner immediately if it's missing, no rules change, no new friction for the normal case. 7 new regression tests (`tests/v7-5-1-correction.mjs`) — this assistant re-ran them independently in its own sandbox and confirmed all 7 pass cleanly, no hang this time (unlike `0.7.5`'s `admin-delete-account.mjs` episode — this is pure client-side code, no `firebase-admin` import involved). `docs/versions/V7/0.7.5.1/EXECUTABLE_TEST_SESSION_V1.md` drafted, read in full — 4 tests, none run yet; Test 3 explicitly needs a genuinely fourth, never-before-invited account to reproduce `V7-F12`'s original trigger exactly. `V7-F13` confirmed untouched by this commit (its own regression test asserts so). **This assistant's own repo copy was again found stale during this verification:** the `V116` sync (folding `V7-F13` into `0.7.10`) had not been applied to the repo, which was still at `V115`. Full write-ups for all three real findings (`V7-F11`–`V7-F13`) are in `OPEN_GAPS_FOR_PLANNING_20260921.md`, authored by the QA/acceptance-session assistant that ran this round directly with the Product Owner; that document also notes `V7-F13` (family ticket) may plausibly share sequencing with `0.7.10`'s existing participant-system-extension work (`V7-F09`/`V7-F10`), since all three touch the same participant/ ticket model — not a sequencing decision, just context for whenever the Product Owner is ready to schedule these. **This assistant's own repo copy was found stale during this verification:** the `V112` sync (staged to the handoff folder, checkpointing the `qa` deployment and both bug fixes) had not actually been applied to the repo — the repo's `docs/roadmap/MVP_DELIVERY_PLAN_CURRENT_STATE.md` was still at `V111` when checked. This `V113` sync therefore carries both the unapplied `V112` content and this closure in one combined diff against the repo's actual `V111` state, so nothing is silently skipped a second time.**Superseded (`0.7.5` deployed to `qa`, live and healthy; acceptance testing starting, `V112`, 2026-09-21):**
**Status:** **`0.7.5` deployed to `qa` and live/healthy, build `0.7.5-8508e0cc` — after two real bugs found and fixed during the deploy itself, both now resolved.** This assistant independently verified as much of this as its own network access allows: the live `qa` hostname is not reachable from this assistant's verification sandbox (confirmed by direct attempt — blocked at the network layer, not something to route around), so the deployment's live behavior is taken on the Product Owner's and Development Agent's direct report; but this assistant independently checked the actual repo commit history before recording anything, rather than only the relayed summary, and found one fix that hadn't been mentioned. **Bug 1 (found by the Product Owner, not code): the `qa` build had a literally invalid placeholder API key baked in**, because `VITE_FIREBASE_API_KEY` had Netlify's "Contains secret values" flag set — masking the real value from any build process outside Netlify's own systems, including the Development Agent's local build and the Netlify CLI build it tried as a workaround. Not a `0.7.5` code defect. Fixed by the Product Owner deleting and re-adding the variable (and checking the other six `VITE_FIREBASE_*` variables for the same flag) without the secret flag — this key is meant to be public, embedded in every client bundle regardless, so marking it secret only ever broke tooling, never protected anything. **Bug 2 (found live during the `qa` deploy, real `0.7.5` code defect, commit `6c2b656`): `admin-delete-account.mjs` returned a `502` on every real invocation.** Root cause: `firebase-admin/auth` unconditionally imports `jwks-rsa` for token-verification machinery this function never used, and every published `jwks-rsa` 4.x version carries its own unfixed upstream bug — it tries to `require()` this project's own `jose` 6.x (already a real, working dependency elsewhere), which is ESM-only, crashing immediately under Netlify's bundled Function runtime specifically (not under plain local Node, which is exactly why neither the design review nor the local quality gate caught it before this). Fixed by replacing the one broken import with a direct, hand-signed REST call to Google's Identity Toolkit API instead of `firebase-admin/auth` — the same "plain REST over heavy SDK surface" pattern `0.7.2`'s own design already established elsewhere in this codebase, not a new approach invented for this fix. `firebase-admin/app`, `/firestore` and `/storage` confirmed unaffected. A regression-guard test was added asserting `firebase-admin/auth` is never imported by this file again. **This resolves the verification question this assistant flagged in `V111`.** The hang this assistant hit trying to import `firebase-admin/auth` in its own sandbox was very likely this exact same broken dependency chain — manifesting as a hang in that environment rather than the immediate crash Netlify's runtime produced, but pointing at the same real defect either way. Flagging it rather than silently accepting the original "quality gate passes" claim was the right call: it was pointing at something genuinely broken, which then surfaced for real during deployment and is now fixed and regression-guarded. The admin function now responds `401` (not `502`) to an unauthenticated request — correct behavior, confirmed by the Development Agent directly against the live endpoint. Sign-in on `qa` confirmed working. **Acceptance testing (`docs/versions/V7/0.7.5/EXECUTABLE_TEST_SESSION_V1.md`, drafted in commit `8508e0c`) is starting now, run directly by the Product Owner with the QA agent** — per the established responsibility split, this assistant returns for closure recording once that round (including any corrections) completes.**Superseded (`0.7.5` implemented and committed; one verification question open, `V111`, 2026-09-21):**
**Status:** **`0.7.5` implemented and committed (`68c050c`), not yet deployed to `qa` — one verification question open, plus two manual Product Owner actions required before deployment.** This assistant independently verified the commit against `DESIGN_V1.md` directly rather than recording the Development Agent's report at face value: the file list (`firestore.rules`, `storage.rules`, `src/cloud-store.js`, `src/operational-data.js`, `netlify/functions/_shared/smart-import-auth.mjs`, `netlify/functions/_shared/smart-import-quota.mjs`, new `netlify/functions/admin-delete-account.mjs`, `src/v5-app.js`, new `tests/v7-5-sharing-admin.mjs`) matches the design's own section structure, and `admin-delete-account.mjs`'s actual code (lazy `adminApp()` initialization, guarded by an explicit env-var check that throws `424 missing_server_configuration` rather than failing silently) matches what was reported. This part is confirmed. **One thing does not check out and is flagged back rather than silently accepted:** attempting to independently exercise `tests/v7-5-sharing-admin.mjs` in this assistant's own verification environment did not complete — it produced no output and had to be killed after 25 seconds. Isolating further: importing `admin-delete-account.mjs` alone hangs; isolating further still, `firebase-admin/auth`, `firebase-admin/firestore` and `firebase-admin/storage` each hang on bare import in this assistant's sandbox (`firebase-admin/app` alone imports fine). This may well be an artifact of this assistant's own network-restricted verification sandbox rather than a real defect in what shipped — but that is a guess, not a confirmed diagnosis, and `admin-delete-account.mjs` is this candidate's single highest-scrutiny piece per the design's own §9 risk assessment. `PROMPT_FOR_CLAUDE_CODE_0_7_5_TEST_SUITE_CLARIFICATION_V1.md` authored, asking the Development Agent to re-run the test suite now and report real output before the quality-gate claim is treated as confirmed. **Two manual Product Owner actions remain regardless of the above, unchanged from the Development Agent's own report:** (1) seed `invitations/eyalby@gmail.com` and `invitations/paolakohan@gmail.com` in the Firebase Console, with `isAdmin:true` on the Product Owner's own record, before the new rules can be published; (2) generate a Firebase service-account key and set it as the `FIREBASE_ADMIN_SERVICE_ACCOUNT` Netlify environment variable. Neither can be done by this assistant or the Development Agent — both are Console/credential actions reserved for the Product Owner, consistent with every prior secret this project has needed. Rules have deliberately not been published yet, since publishing before the `invitations` collection exists would lock out every user on the shared backend.

**Superseded (`0.7.3` design reviewed and approved; `0.7.2.1` correction and `0.7.3` implementation both authorized, V103):** **`0.7.3` design reviewed and approved; `0.7.2.1` correction and `0.7.3` implementation both authorized.** This assistant read `docs/versions/V7/0.7.3/DESIGN_V1.md` in full before recording this revision, not just the Development Agent's summary. The design verified `0.7.2`'s real current-state code directly (per the design-request's own instruction) and surfaced two urgent findings not asked for but reported anyway, per this project's standing discipline: **an offline edit made after the first can be silently and completely lost on reload** (`save()`'s serialization prevents any queued write past the first from ever reaching Firestore's own durable offline queue while offline — a violation of the frozen "no silent data loss" offline invariant, already live in Production), and **a live remote update can wipe an open, unsubmitted edit form** (`render()`'s unconditional full-DOM replacement fires on every remote snapshot, including while the user is mid-edit). The design's substantive recommendations: field-level `updateDoc()` merge writes for same-record/different-field concurrent edits (same-field-same-record stays last-write-wins with `updatedAt`/`updatedByUid` attribution, no interactive conflict UI — not disproportionate given this app's actual usage pattern); decoupling `save()`'s diff computation from server-ack waiting (Firestore's own persistence already durably queues a write the moment it's issued); buffering remote updates while a form is open; a concrete seven-test two-device acceptance protocol (Tests A–G); and confirmation the QA Test Trip convention remains sufficient for two-device testing, no separate Firebase project needed. **Three decisions made by the Product Owner, 2026-09-15, after this assistant cross-checked the offline-data-loss finding against `ARCHITECTURE_CURRENT_STATE.md`'s own frozen invariant language and `0.7.2`'s own `V7-F07` precedent (an offline regression treated as release-blocking) before asking:** (1) design approved as-is; (2) the offline-data-loss bug gets a fast, narrow `0.7.2.1` correction now, ahead of `0.7.3`'s own timeline, rather than folded into `0.7.3`'s implementation — the live, already-shipped exposure outweighs the design's own fair counter-argument that full validation wants the two-device testing `0.7.3` is building anyway; (3) sync-status visibility gets the minimal pending-sync indicator (the design document's own recommended default), not the status quo or a richer per-item surface. `PROMPT_FOR_CLAUDE_CODE_0_7_2_1_CORRECTION_V1.md` (narrow `save()` serialization fix only) and `PROMPT_FOR_CLAUDE_CODE_0_7_3_IMPLEMENTATION_KICKOFF_V1.md` (field-level merge, open-form buffering, sync-status indicator, two-device test session — sequenced to build on top of the `0.7.2.1` fix) both authored. No code changed by this revision — design-review and decision-recording pass only.

**Superseded (`0.7.2` re-scoped, Firestore confirmed, design requested, V97):** **`0.7.2` re-scoped — no server-side datastore exists yet; proceeding design-first with Firestore confirmed as the datastore.** The Development Agent stopped before implementing anything and reported a real architecture gap the scope document had missed: items 1–2 were written as if the hard infrastructure already existed and `0.7.2` just needed to activate access control on top of it, but every `state.*` mutation in the live app goes synchronously through `localStorage` — there is currently no way for two browsers, devices, or accounts to share a Trip at all. **Product intent:** FamilyTrips is built for the Product Owner's own family trips, but it must support sharing with friends who create and use their own independently-owned Trip in parallel — not a demo, not a stripped-down mode, a first-class Trip with the same sharing and sync contract. This is confirmed, non-negotiable MVP scope, scheduled in Alpha 0.7 (see "Shared Trips, roles and access" below); Alpha 0.6 (Smart Import) completes first.
**Scope:** Thailand trip only
**Purpose:** Define the remaining Alpha versions required to complete the MVP, followed by Beta and Release Candidate stages. This document holds the current, forward-looking plan and the live status of in-progress work only. Alpha 0.1 through Alpha 0.5.6 are fully delivered; their specifications, every embedded QA checkpoint, acceptance register and handoff narrative are preserved verbatim in `MVP_DELIVERY_HISTORY_V1.md` and are not repeated here.

| V112 | 2026-09-21 | `0.7.5` deployed to `qa`, live and healthy (`0.7.5-8508e0cc`) — two real bugs found and fixed during deployment | This assistant checked the actual repo commit history directly before recording anything and found a fix not mentioned in the relayed summary (commit `6c2b656`). Bug 1, not a code defect: `VITE_FIREBASE_API_KEY` had Netlify's "Contains secret values" flag set, masking the real value from any build outside Netlify's own systems — fixed by the Product Owner deleting and re-adding the variable without that flag (this key is meant to be public regardless). Bug 2, a real `0.7.5` defect found live during the `qa` deploy: `admin-delete-account.mjs` returned `502` on every invocation, because `firebase-admin/auth` transitively requires `jwks-rsa`, which has its own unfixed upstream bug requiring the ESM-only `jose` package the old CommonJS way — crashes only under Netlify's bundled Function runtime, not plain local Node, which is why neither the design review nor the local quality gate caught it earlier. Fixed by replacing that one import with a direct REST call to Google's Identity Toolkit API, the same pattern `0.7.2`'s own design already established elsewhere; a regression-guard test was added. This resolves the verification question this assistant raised in `V111` — the hang hit independently while checking `firebase-admin/auth` was very likely this same broken dependency chain, manifesting differently in a different runtime but pointing at the same real defect the whole time. Admin function now confirmed responding `401` (not `502`) to an unauthenticated request; sign-in on `qa` confirmed working. Acceptance testing is starting now, run directly by the Product Owner with the QA agent per the established responsibility split. No action pending from this assistant until that round completes. |

For `V6-F87`, the fix code is confirmed genuinely present and deployed, but its mechanism (a `notify()` toast, auto-dismissing after 2.6s, called AFTER `open(url,'_blank')` already shifts browser attention to a new tab/download) plausibly fires without ever being seen — a real UX-timing gap, not a wiring bug. **`0.6.7.4` re-fixes both, strengthened:** `V6-F86` — `insuranceSystemPrompt` now requires `evidence` for `provider` to be a literal, verbatim transcription of the printed company-name text (exact characters, unscript-preserved, not a translation/paraphrase) rather than a location description; an unproducible transcription is explicitly framed as proof the model lacks a grounded reading, directing it to `needs_review` instead of inventing a quote to satisfy the requirement — a concrete, harder-to-satisfy-by-hallucination mechanism than the `0.6.7.3` paragraph's more abstract caution (which remains, unchanged, alongside this addition). `V6-F87` — `openSource` now shows a blocking `alert()` (this codebase's own established pattern for a must-not-miss message) BEFORE `open()`/the download starts, not a toast after, eliminating the tab-focus race entirely rather than trying to out-time it. **Per this round's own strengthened closure bar (a passing test suite alone is not sufficient evidence of closure), NEITHER finding is reported `Fixed` this round:** this Development Agent has no `qa` access (the app requires Google sign-in restricted to authorized users, confirmed by navigating to the live site) and so could not perform the live re-upload (`V6-F86`) or live re-open (`V6-F87`) verification this package's own closure condition requires. Full quality gate passes: `node --check` syntax, 198/198 test assertions across 27 files (up from 197), clean production build. Committed to `version/V6`, not deployed — awaiting explicit Product Owner confirmation this is ready, and awaiting live verification of both findings, before any GO/NO-GO reconsideration. |
| V85 | 2026-09-10 | `0.6.7.2` acceptance session (Round 3) NO-GO; `0.6.7.3` correction package implemented | Product Owner ran a full 14-test acceptance session against build `0.6.7.2-6eb553d3` on `qa` (`docs/versions/V6/0.6.7/EXECUTABLE_TEST_SESSION_V3.md`): 13/14 PASS, 1 FAIL — the closest this candidate has come to a clean session. `V6-F84` and `V6-F85` both confirmed fixed on retest; `V6-F83` meaningfully improved. **NO-GO**, decided by the Product Owner: Test 6 (the mandatory `INS-006` wild-card) failed for a third consecutive round, each time one layer deeper — `V6-F78` (upload rejected) → `V6-F85` (extraction backend rejected the format) → now `V6-F86` (extraction succeeds end-to-end, but `provider` resolves to "Harel Insurance Company Ltd.", a completely wrong, unrelated company, with no `needs_review` flag; the source's own crystal-clear title bar reads "כלל חברה לביטוח בע"מ", Clal). Root-caused by direct investigation, not guessed: (1) the extraction pipeline is fully stateless per request — `callExtraction` builds a fresh input array per call with `store:false` and no shared context across sources, so literal cross-document leakage is not structurally possible; (2) the actual converted PNG (from the `V6-F85` fix) was regenerated and visually inspected specifically at the header/title-bar region — the "Clal Insurance Company Ltd." text is bold white-on-black, near-maximum contrast, unambiguously legible, ruling out TIFF→PNG conversion quality as the cause. This is a genuine model hallucination/pattern-completion failure (Harel is the provider for `INS-002`/`INS-005` elsewhere in this same benchmark — a plausible, real, commonly-seen name substituted for what this specific source actually shows), not a legibility or code-path bug. Fixed with a new `insuranceSystemPrompt` paragraph mirroring `activitySystemPrompt`'s own established anti-hallucination discipline: confidence is not evidence, a provider value must be traceable to literal visible text, and heightened scrutiny applies specifically to scanned/fax-quality sources — deliberately does not name "Harel" or "Clal" in the prompt itself, to avoid biasing the model away from ever correctly reporting Harel on `INS-002`/`INS-005`, the same overcorrection risk `V6-F84` already demonstrated on this exact code path. One more finding on the same test: `V6-F87` (High) — opening the item's attached `.tif` file downloads it instead of previewing inline, unlike every other supported type; root-caused to `openSource` (`src/v5-app.js`) treating every stored attachment identically, trusting the browser to render it inline, which works for PDF/PNG/JPEG/WebP but no browser renders TIFF natively. Fixed by detecting a TIFF source (MIME type or filename fallback) and surfacing an explicit `notify()` explaining the download, rather than duplicating the server-side TIFF decoder into the client bundle purely for this one narrow, offline-only local-file-viewing path — the download itself already worked and the file remains reachable, the bug was that it happened silently with no explanation. New test coverage in `tests/v6-7-insurance-import.mjs` (2 new named tests — `V6-F86` prompt-text source-inspection, including an explicit assertion that the prompt does NOT name "Harel"/"Clal"; `V6-F87` source-inspection of `openSource`'s new TIFF detection and notification). `package.json` bumped to `0.6.7.3`. Full quality gate passes: `node --check` syntax, 197/197 test assertions across 27 files (up from 195), clean production build. Committed to `version/V6`, not deployed — awaiting a new acceptance session before any GO/NO-GO reconsideration. |

## IMPORTANT — Production baseline was misdocumented; corrected 2026-08-30

Every prior revision of this plan (and the repo's own QA-package README) stated or assumed Production was at Alpha 0.5.6. **This was wrong.** Direct inspection of `origin/main` in the real repository showed it at `0.3.0-foundation`, 19 commits behind. Practically: Production has been running a much older build than anyone realized throughout this entire `0.6.x` engagement — none of V4, V5, or V6 (including all of Alpha 0.6.4, previously recorded above as "live in Production") had actually reached `main`. **This means the "Alpha 0.6.4 fully closed and live in Production" status recorded in every prior revision of this document needs re-verification** — it's now unclear whether that referred to a different deploy target than `main`, or whether that record was itself inaccurate. Flagging this explicitly rather than silently correcting it, since it affects how much to trust other "confirmed live in Production" statements in this document's history. **Follow-up needed:** now that the current push is confirmed live, verify directly (not from documentation) exactly what Production is serving and reconcile this document's Alpha 0.6.4 status accordingly.

- Today out-of-range fallback (`FL-008`) — Confirmed working exactly as specified. `0.7` backlog item logged for a smarter version.

## Decision made — Production Smart Import secrets and credentials

**Decided 2026-08-27: launch Flight Smart Import to Production now, Flight-only.** As of `V46`, this is understood to mean launching the entire V4/V5/V6 line at once (see baseline-correction note above), not just Flight in isolation — the Product Owner has been informed of this and explicitly authorized proceeding anyway.

**Production environment-variable status (updated 2026-08-27):**

| Variable | Needed by Smart Import? | Production status |
|---|---|---|
| `FAMILYTRIPS_ALLOWED_EMAILS` | Yes — allow-list gate on every call | **Set** (via CLI, dashboard UI would not persist it) — currently `eyalby@gmail.com,paolakohan@gmail.com`. Who else should be invited to Production remains an open Product Owner decision, not yet made. |
| `FAMILYTRIPS_GOOGLE_PLACES_API_KEY` | Yes — Hotel location verification only (non-fatal if unset) | **Set** (via CLI) |
| `FAMILYTRIPS_SMART_IMPORT_DAILY_QUOTA` | Yes — per-user daily cap (defaults to 10 if unset) | **Set** (via CLI) |
| `FAMILYTRIPS_FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID` | Yes, but falls back to `VITE_FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID`, already correctly set in Production | No action needed |
| `FAMILYTRIPS_V13_GOOGLE_PLACES_API_KEY` | No — confirmed unused anywhere in the codebase (stale) | Ignore |
| `OPENAI_API_KEY` | Yes — every extraction call | Not a site variable — provided by Netlify's account-level AI Gateway, already active for this site across all contexts |

**Known Netlify dashboard bug encountered:** editing an existing variable's Production value through the web UI and clicking Save silently failed. Worked around via `netlify env:set VAR "value" --context production` directly in a local terminal.

**Deploy mechanism (confirmed 2026-08-30):** the `familytripz` Netlify site auto-builds Production from `main` only. Promotion = `git push origin <commit>:main`.

**First Production deploy attempt failed, second succeeded (2026-08-30):** `94a32a2` failed to build — `npm install` hit a 404 on `@firebase/installations@0.6.33` (bad lockfile pin; the Node-version hypothesis first offered was wrong and was ruled out by getting the actual build log rather than guessing). Netlify's atomic deploys meant Production stayed on the old build throughout — no user-facing impact. Fix pushed as commit `a915a44`; deploy `6a9464b101a1690008db8823` succeeded, state `ready`, **live build identifier `0.6.5-a915a440`** (differs from `0.6.5-94a32a28` only because of the extra fix commit — no functional change, only `package-lock.json`).

**Uncommitted working-tree changes (2026-08-30) — resolved 2026-08-31:** the two renamed test fixture images under `tests/fixtures/flight_benchmark/` were committed as-is (`e0be719`) and pushed to `version/V6`. A separate, previously-undiscovered issue was also found and fixed while investigating: a stray file accidentally committed back in `d56482b` ("Add Alpha 0.3 Firebase and Vite foundation") — leftover `less`-pager text from a botched shell command, tracked in the repo under a garbled filename — was removed (`128eaba`) and pushed. Working tree is clean on `version/V6`.

Before ramping real Production traffic: (1) ~~live Production Hotel + Flight import test~~ — **CONFIRMED 2026-08-31**: Product Owner tested both separately against Production, both worked; (2) check AI Gateway billing/usage in Netlify (Team → Usage/Billing) — account currently shows `ai_usage_limit_enforced: false` with no cap, which is a configuration state, not a cost guarantee — **still open**; (3) decide who beyond the Product Owner and the QA test account should be added to the Production `FAMILYTRIPS_ALLOWED_EMAILS` allowlist — **still open, Product Owner decision, deliberately deferred for now.**

## 1. Delivery Principles

### Alpha
Alpha versions complete the full MVP functionality.

- Functionality is the priority.
- Architecture must remain stable.
- UI improvements are included only when required for usability or testing.
- Every release must be installable, testable, and deployable.
- Every release includes QA, acceptance tests, regression tests, release notes, and rollback instructions.

### Beta
Beta improves the product experience after MVP functionality is complete.

- Visual design
- HMI and interaction design
- Navigation refinement
- Mobile usability
- Accessibility
- Consistency
- Performance
- Final user-facing wording

### Release Candidate
Release Candidate is limited to:

- Bug fixes
- Data protection
- Reliability
- Offline verification
- Final Thailand-trip acceptance testing
- No new features unless a critical blocker is discovered

## 2. MVP Scope for Thailand

The MVP must support the real Thailand trip from planning through active use.

- Trip creation and management
- Multiple independently-owned Trips running in parallel — the Product Owner's family Trip and any number of friends' own Trips, each fully isolated from the others unless explicitly shared by its Owner. This is non-negotiable MVP scope, not a post-MVP nicety: FamilyTrips is built to be shared with friends who create and manage their own Trip alongside the Product Owner's.
- Private Trips and explicit shared-Trip access — on any Trip, whether the Product Owner's family Trip or a friend's own independently-owned Trip
- Two-user, two-device collaboration on any shared Trip — the same Owner/Editor sharing and synchronization contract applies identically whether the Trip belongs to the Product Owner or to a friend; there is no reduced or "friend-tier" version of sharing or sync
- Near-real-time online synchronization and offline-change synchronization after reconnect
- One or more Destination Countries, destination/route summary, and Trip dates
- Trip lifecycle
- Smart document intake
- Bulk Trip import from a fixed-template spreadsheet — create a whole Trip and its items in one pass from a Product Owner-designed column structure, deterministic parsing rather than AI extraction; approved MVP scope (Product Owner decision, 2026-09-06), sequenced to Alpha 0.7; restored to this document as of `V80` after being found missing from the repository's synced copy
- Manual item creation
- Dynamic forms by item type
- Item-owned documents plus a non-category Intake Inbox for unassigned sources; no standalone Documents category in Trip Center
- Direct camera/gallery/PDF/file attachment of documents and tickets to existing items, including items created from QR, without duplicate item creation
- Today
- Timeline
- Calendar and date selection
- Trip Center
- Games and travel activities
- Preparations and tasks
- Shopping places, scheduled shopping visits, and shopping lists
- Contextual website, booking, provider, document, and navigation actions attached to their owning items; no standalone Links category
- Item-owned locations and map/navigation actions; no standalone Locations category
- First-class Transport items for transfers, rail, ferry/boat, rental vehicles and other local transport — extended to include the same Smart Import extraction treatment (PDF/image/bounded URL) as Hotel, Flight, Attraction/Event and Insurance, approved as added MVP scope (Product Owner decision, 2026-09-21), sequenced to `0.7.9`; the item type itself (manual/QR entry only, no extraction) is `0.7.4`'s scope
- First-class Insurance items for travel/medical insurance policies — policy number, provider, coverage dates, emergency contact and claims information — with the same Smart Import extraction treatment (PDF/image/bounded URL) as Hotel, Flight and Attraction/Event, approved as MVP scope (Product Owner decision, 2026-08-31), sequenced to `0.6.7`; restored to this document as of `V80` after being found missing from the repository's synced copy
- Participants
- Participant-specific tickets and documents
- Packing lists
- Participant-specific packing
- Item-owned and Trip-level useful contacts; no standalone Contacts category in Trip Center
- Offline use
- Visible synchronization state and deterministic conflict handling
- Local persistence
- PWA installation
- Clear delete/reset behavior
- Interactive release testing

*Alpha 0.1 through Alpha 0.5.6 delivered this scope incrementally; see `MVP_DELIVERY_HISTORY_V1.md` for what shipped in each version. What follows is the current and future plan.*

# Alpha 0.6 — Smart Import Redesign

## Goal

Replace the failed browser-only heuristics for creating or enriching travel items from a URL, image/camera capture or PDF with one evidence-backed, source-preserving and review-first architecture. This release is dedicated to Smart Import and must not absorb the former broad MVP-completion backlog.

## Entry gate — Product Owner discovery session

**Entry gate: PASSED.** `SMART_IMPORT_REDESIGN_DISCOVERY_V22.md` freezes Decisions 1–7 for Hotel. `FLIGHT_SMART_IMPORT_DISCOVERY.md` freezes the Flight-specific type contract and benchmark, inheriting the generic decisions unchanged.

## Alpha 0.6.4 — correction package: QA-confirmed; Production-live status uncertain (see `V46` baseline-correction note)

`0.6.4` corrected the twelve findings assigned in `V14`, plus seven findings discovered during its own QA/post-promotion retesting (`V6-F18`–`V6-F24`), all Closed against QA. Prior revisions of this document stated this was "live in Production" — that claim is now uncertain given the `V46` discovery that `main` was still at `0.3.0-foundation`. Needs direct re-verification now that the current push is live.

## Alpha 0.6.5 — Flight Smart Import: GO in QA; Production live and verified

Approved 2026-08-24. Entry gate passed 2026-08-25. Seven fix passes sent across the engagement, plus one Production-only lockfile fix. **All 8 `FL-001`–`FL-008` benchmark cases pass; all findings (`V6-F25`–`V6-F44`) closed; `0.6.5` release gate PASSED against QA.** Product Owner decided to launch to Production now — this turned out to be the first-ever Production release of the whole V4/V5/V6 line (see `V46`), explicitly authorized. **Production is live**, build `0.6.5-a915a440`, deploy `6a9464b101a1690008db8823`. **The live Hotel + Flight import test against Production is confirmed complete (2026-08-31)** — the Product Owner tested both separately and both worked. `0.6.5` is fully closed. Full status: `EXECUTABLE_TEST_SESSION_V169.md`.

## Approved scope addition — Attraction/Event Smart Import + 0.6.5 Production findings (0.6.6) — discovery FROZEN, entry gate PASSED, implementation package authoring underway

`0.6.6` was originally approved as Restaurant on 2026-08-25/27. Retargeted to Attraction/Event on 2026-08-31 (`V49`) — see correction note below. Entry gate passed 2026-08-31 (`V50`, `ATTRACTION_EVENT_SMART_IMPORT_DISCOVERY.md` `V4`). Sequenced after `0.6.5` (Flight), to launch independently to Production when ready. **As of `V51`, scope also includes three findings from `0.6.5` Production monitoring** (`V6-F49`–`V6-F51`) — Product Owner decision to bundle a correction scope into this feature candidate rather than spin up a separate `0.6.6`-correction/`0.6.7`-feature split. See the `V51` change-history row above for the full finding list and the four items (`V6-F45`–`V6-F48`) requiring no `0.6.6` action.

### Attraction/Event Smart Import feature scope

- **Correction — the prior "Activity is blocked" claim was wrong, not just stale.** This document previously stated (`V16`) that Activities do not exist as a first-class Trip item type and are therefore blocked. Product Owner spotted a live `אטרקציה` (Attraction) Trip Center category tile in the running app; direct code inspection confirmed `ITEM_TYPES.activity` / `TYPE_FIELDS.activity` in `src/ingestion.js` are full peers of every other type, including `restaurant` — same generic dynamic-form system since `V4-F01` (Alpha 0.4.3). There was never a data-model blocker; what was actually missing is exactly what was missing for Restaurant: the Smart Import extraction contract and benchmark.
- **Why Attraction/Event instead of Restaurant:** brainstorming surfaced that real-world Restaurant sources are disproportionately phone/walk-in/informal-email — a thin match for a pipeline built to read PDF/image/URL documents. Attraction/Event sources are ticket-shaped (PDF, QR/barcode), which is architecturally closer to the boarding-pass case Flight already proved out (`FL-008`). Restaurant is shelved, not abandoned — `claude/RESTAURANT_SMART_IMPORT_DISCOVERY_V1.md` is preserved at `V2` as a real candidate for a later slice.
- **Discovery FROZEN 2026-08-31 (`ATTRACTION_EVENT_SMART_IMPORT_DISCOVERY.md` `V4`):** `claude/ATTRACTION_EVENT_SMART_IMPORT_DISCOVERY.md` defines the frozen Attraction/Event type contract — inheriting Decisions 1/2/3/5 unchanged, with Decision 4 (no-omission fields), Decision 6 (benchmark) and Decision 7 (delivery slice) all frozen.
- **Decision 4, frozen:** event/attraction name, date, time and venue/location are no-omission blockers; location must be accurate (property-first, same discipline as Hotel). Ticket/confirmation number is explicitly **not** a no-omission blocker — required-when-present, same treatment as Restaurant would have had, not Flight's PNR-blocker treatment (Product Owner decision, reversing the `V1` proposal).
- **Decision 6, frozen:** seven real, individually catalogued benchmark documents, `AE-001`–`AE-007`, each with SHA-256 identity and frozen field-by-field expected values — `AE-001` (FC Barcelona vs Girona FC, LaLiga) is the mandatory wild-card; `AE-003`/`AE-004` (Bloomfield Science Museum) is a real companion/duplicate-source pair; `AE-005` (Gamzu orienteering) is a real order-number-vs-ticket-number precision case; `AE-006` (WaterLand) is a 4-tickets-in-one-PDF family case with no per-person names; `AE-007` (a zoo ticket screenshot) closes the image-format gap and carries an explicit lower-confidence note on its barcode digits. Both PDF and image source formats are represented.
- **Decision 7, frozen:** ticket PDFs and images from global Add or existing-item attach, mirroring Hotel/Flight. Public URL acquisition is **in scope**, with an explicit `V4` sub-decision on how to treat it: official venue/museum/OTA URLs are the real target and are expected to work; ticket-resale/box-office platform URLs (Ticketmaster, vivenu/leaan.net, hapoelbc.com) are expected to land on a safe-failure state, and that is documented as expected behavior — backed by real evidence gathered before implementation (4 independently tested ticket-platform URLs, 4/4 blocked by `robots.txt`, a new named failure reason alongside Hotel's `access_required`/login pattern). **One explicitly accepted, non-blocking gap:** zero official venue/museum/OTA URLs have been tested end-to-end yet — the implementation package carries this as a named verification step, not an assumed pass.
- **Entry gate: PASSED (2026-08-31).**

### `0.6.5` Production findings folded into `0.6.6` (added `V51`)

| Finding | Status | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| `V6-F45` — corrupted lockfile blocked the first Production deploy attempt | Fixed, live | Already recorded in `V47`; registered here with a finding ID for register completeness only. No `0.6.6` action. |
| `V6-F46` — Product Owner locked out of Production Smart Import (`FAMILYTRIPS_ALLOWED_EMAILS` missing `eyalby@gmail.com`) | Fixed, live | Corrected same-day via the Functions-scoped env var and a redeploy. No `0.6.6` action. |
| `V6-F47` — Hotel PDF (Hyatt Regency Phuket Voucher) failed Smart Import 3 times in a row | Accepted, not a defect | Netlify function logs show three genuine OpenAI 5xx responses (distinct request IDs); a Flight import succeeded around the same time, confirming the AI Gateway path itself is healthy. No code change. Recommendation carried into support docs: retry; escalate to OpenAI with request IDs if the same document keeps failing while others succeed. |
| `V6-F48` — file picker reopened after selecting the very first upload on the account | Monitoring | One-time occurrence, has not recurred (including a second Flight upload). Reviewed code path already matches existing `V6-F05`/`V6-F11` regression coverage — no gap found. Left open as Monitoring, not assigned to any version, revisit only if it recurs. |
| `V6-F49` — needs-review warnings sourced from `draft.unresolved` free text never clear after a manual correction | **Fixed** (`0.6.6`, `version/V6`, not yet Production) | Root cause: `needsReviewFields`-sourced warnings were keyed by the raw model canonical key, which `NEEDS_REVIEW_FIELD_MAP` had no entries for at all, so nothing could ever match and clear them, regardless of source; `unresolved`-sourced warnings carry no field key by nature. Fixed: `NEEDS_REVIEW_FIELD_MAP` gained the missing Hotel/Activity-shaped entries and `smart-import-adapter.js`/`activity-import-adapter.js` now key by the resolved proposed-field name, so a structured needs-review warning genuinely clears on edit; an `unresolved`-sourced warning is wrapped `{message,dismissible:true}` with a manual dismiss control wired into the review screen (`v5-app.js`'s `isDismissibleWarning`/`dismiss-warning`); the tightened Hotel/Activity system prompts now instruct the model to resolve a conflict into a field from the start. Regression test: `tests/v6-49-51-correction.mjs` (both the structured-path clearing and the unresolved-path dismissibility). `src/ingestion.js:154-171`, `src/smart-import-adapter.js`, `src/v5-app.js`. |
| `V6-F50` — a date the model resolved correctly can silently never reach `startAt`/`endAt` | **Fixed** (`0.6.6`, `version/V6`, not yet Production) | Same root cause as `V6-F49`. Fixed: same prompt tightening as `V6-F49`, plus a regex fallback (`scanFreeTextForDate`, exported from `smart-import-adapter.js`, reused by `activity-import-adapter.js`) that recovers a labelled date from `unresolved`/`importantNotes` free text when the structured `fields` extraction found nothing, flagging the result `needs_review` rather than presenting it as confident. Regression test: `tests/v6-49-51-correction.mjs`, using the exact real Hyatt Regency free-text wording, plus a negative case proving the scan stays inert on unlabelled text. `src/smart-import-adapter.js`. |
| `V6-F51` — Smart Import review screen doesn't pre-fill blank dates with the Trip start date | **Fixed** (`0.6.6`, `version/V6`, not yet Production) | Low severity, UX consistency only. Fixed: new `suggestionReviewDefaults` (`src/ingestion.js`) mirrors `suggestionToItem`'s own trip-start fallback condition exactly (new-item suggestions only, respects schedule), applied in `suggestionView` before rendering `proposalFields`. Regression test: `tests/v6-49-51-correction.mjs`, covering range/single schedules, an already-dated suggestion (must not be overwritten), an update/attach-and-extract suggestion (must not get the new-item default), and no-trip-yet. `src/v5-app.js:145`, `src/ingestion.js:178-193`. |

Per `VERSION_PACKAGE_AUTHORING_STANDARD_V29.md`'s mandatory regression-test rule, `V6-F49`–`V6-F51` are recorded `Fixed` above because `tests/v6-49-51-correction.mjs` exists, is named for each finding ID, asserts the specific expected behavior recorded in this register (not merely that the code runs), and passes as part of the permanent `npm test` suite. "Fixed" here means fixed in the `0.6.6` candidate on `version/V6` — none of the three has reached Production, which remains on `0.6.5` until `0.6.6` completes its own acceptance and receives explicit Product Owner GO.

Implementation of the combined `0.6.6` scope (Attraction/Event feature + `V6-F49`–`V6-F51` corrections) is complete on `version/V6` and awaiting Product Owner install/acceptance testing — see the `V53` change-history row above.

### `0.6.6` QA acceptance findings (added `V54`)

Three findings discovered during Eyal's own `0.6.6` QA acceptance testing (build `0.6.6-3925bb20`), investigated and registered per the standard's mandatory finding register. All three were implemented on `version/V6`, each with a passing named regression test. **Update (`V62`):** Eyal has since retested all three — `V6-F52`/`V6-F53` are now **Fixed, verified**; `V6-F54` is **merged into `V6-F55`** (see below) rather than closed separately. See each row for full retest evidence.

`V6-F54`'s QA retest (build `0.6.6-9b5b1ed0`) surfaced a fourth finding, `V6-F55`, registered below — investigated and root-caused, not yet fixed. A second observation from the same retest (Panan Krabi) was investigated and assessed as very likely expected, by-design behavior, not a defect — see the note following the table; it is not registered as a finding.

Eyal's `AE-001` retest surfaced item 5 (per-passenger splitting) as not shown in the item detail UI. Investigated below and split into two findings, `V6-F56` and `V6-F57` — see the `V59` change-history row above for the full investigation narrative, including the explicit data-access limitation (no live Firestore/Firebase inspection tool available in this environment).

A follow-up investigation (`V60`) confirmed `V6-F57` extends uniformly to `AE-002`/`AE-004` (same unconditional code path, no case-specific behavior) and extends its scope to `details.amount`/`details.currency` (not shown in `detailView()` for any activity item, same "captured but never displayed" root cause) — `V6-F57`'s row below is updated to reflect this, not a new finding. The same follow-up also found a genuine test-coverage gap in the existing regression suite, registered as `V6-F58` below.

| Finding | Observed in | Test/evidence source | Observed vs. expected behavior | Severity | Assignment status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `V6-F56` — `ticketHolderSchema` has no field for a per-passenger national ID (DNI) and blocks one via `additionalProperties:false`, so DNI can never reach a saved item regardless of extraction quality | `0.6.6` QA, build `0.6.6-9b5b1ed0` | `AE-001` retest (FC Barcelona vs Girona FC ticket); direct code inspection of `netlify/functions/_shared/smart-import-schema.mjs` lines 87–90 (`ticketHolderSchema`) and 92–108 (`activityImportSchema`) | Observed: `AE-001`'s item detail shows no per-passenger DNI anywhere. Expected (frozen ground truth, `ATTRACTION_EVENT_SMART_IMPORT_DISCOVERY.md` `V4`, line 100–101): each of the two named passengers carries a DNI ("Eyal Ben Yitzchak Ben Yitzchak, DNI 40384410 …", "Amit Ben Yitzhak, DNI 40386943 …") as part of their evidence-linked per-person data. Root cause, confirmed by direct schema inspection (no live-item read needed for this half — the schema itself is the proof): `ticketHolderSchema` declares exactly `name`, `ticketNumber`, `seatOrSection`, `evidence`, `certainty` with `additionalProperties:false`; OpenAI's structured-output enforcement means any `dni`-shaped value the model tried to attach to a ticket holder is rejected/stripped, not merely dropped by app logic downstream. `activitySystemPrompt`'s `fields` key vocabulary (`location, meeting_point, duration, age_restrictions, included, not_included, amount, currency, confirmation_number, website, start_date, start_time`) also has no DNI/national-ID entry, so the model is never even instructed to look for one as a fallback generic field. This is a genuine data-capture gap in the schema/prompt design authored for `0.6.6`, not a rendering gap — DNI has no field to land in anywhere in the pipeline, so it cannot be "present but unshown." Distinct from `V6-F57` below, which concerns fields the schema does capture. | High — direct, provable failure against `AE-001`'s frozen ground truth, the mandatory wild-card case; requires a schema/prompt change (adding a `dni`/national-ID property to `ticketHolderSchema` and its vocabulary), not a UI fix | **Fixed, verified** (`V64`, target `0.6.6.1`) — Eyal's retest: a fresh delete-and-reimport of `AE-001` shows both ticket holders with correct DNI (`40384410`, `40386943`) |
| `V6-F57` — `details.ticketHolders`/`ticketNumbers`/`ticketQuantity`/`amount`/`currency` (per-passenger name, seat/section/gate/row, per-ticket number, and price) have zero rendering code anywhere in the app, on either the suggestion review screen or the saved item detail view — confirmed to apply uniformly to every Activity case (`AE-001`, `AE-002`, `AE-004`, `AE-005`, and now `AE-006`), not just `AE-001` | `0.6.6` QA, build `0.6.6-9b5b1ed0` | `AE-001` retest; direct code inspection of `src/activity-import-adapter.js` (capture) and repository-wide search of `src/v5-app.js` (rendering — zero matches for `ticketHolders`, `ticketNumbers`, or `ticketQuantity`; `detailView()`'s full render list, read directly, has no `amount`/`currency` either) | Observed: `AE-001`'s per-passenger seat/gate/row/ticket-code data (the `Acces`/`Gate`/`Boca`/`Row`/`Seat` vocabulary the discovery doc calls out as this case's defining wild-card trait) is not shown in the item detail view — only the source PDF is attached. `AE-005`'s follow-up investigation additionally found its ₪120 `amount` is likewise invisible on the saved item. Expected: this data, when the schema can capture it (unlike `V6-F56`'s DNI gap), should be visible. Root cause, confirmed by direct code inspection: `smartImportActivityResultToSuggestion` (`src/activity-import-adapter.js` lines 44–50, 115) correctly maps `draft.ticketHolders` (and `values.amount`/`values.currency`) into `proposed.details`, and the OpenAI schema slot exists for all of it — so this data is architecturally captured, not schema-blocked. But `proposalFields()` (`src/v5-app.js` line 217) only renders a single flat `participants` text input (names only) plus the fixed `TYPE_FIELDS.activity` list (which does not include `amount`/`currency` at all), `smartEvidencePanel()` (line 215) renders `details.smartImportFields`/`importantNotes`/place-validation only, and `detailView()` (line 150, read in full) renders only provider/confirmationNumber/location/startAt/endAt/notes/warnings/sources — none of it ever touches `ticketHolders`, `ticketNumbers`, `ticketQuantity`, `amount`, or `currency`. A follow-up investigation (`V60`) confirmed the capture-and-display code paths are unconditional — no branching by case or ticket count anywhere — so this applies identically to `AE-002` (Row 4/Seat 26/Gate B) and `AE-004` (3 assigned seats), not just `AE-001`; and it extends to `amount`/`currency` for every priced case (`AE-001`, `AE-004`, `AE-005`, `AE-007`), not only `AE-005`. A second follow-up (`V61`) confirmed `AE-006` (WaterLand, 4 tickets, no per-person names) as a fifth instance of the identical pattern — its `ticketNumbers` (`623000111`–`623000114`) are correctly captured (matching the passing `test_AE006_four_tickets_no_names_date_only` assertion that `ticketHolders.length===4`) but equally unrendered — this also confirms the `V53` implementation report's claim that `AE-006` "individually preserves all four physical tickets" is accurate at the adapter/capture level, just invisible in the UI like every other case, not a false claim. **Whether any specific item's actual saved data holds the full underlying values could still not be directly confirmed** — this environment has no Firebase/Firestore inspection tool, and the Netlify Blobs audit store does not retain the extracted draft (see `V59` row above); the existing regression suite (`tests/v6-6-attraction-event-import.mjs`) proves the adapter mapping is correct for the cases/fields it actually asserts (see `V6-F58`), but not for the live model's real output on any of Eyal's actual test documents. This determination rests on code-path tracing (capture path exists and is correctly wired for all these fields; display path does not exist anywhere for any of them) rather than a direct read of any live item, flagged explicitly per the investigation's own instruction not to assume. | Low if the underlying data is genuinely captured (a real, deferred-to-Beta-UI display gap, same class as other fields not yet in the current review/detail forms) — would need to be reassessed as Medium/data-loss if a later direct check of a saved item shows `ticketHolders`/`amount` themselves are empty or incomplete for reasons other than `V6-F56`'s DNI gap | **Fixed, verified** (`V64`, target `0.6.6.1`) — Eyal's retest across three fresh reimports: `AE-001` (seat/DNI/ticket-code table renders), `AE-005` (₪120 amount shows; the `ticketNumber` precision distinction — `2ZTN-G53R-GJ81P` vs. order number `2ZTN-G53R-GJ8` — is visible and correct), `AE-003` (three ticket holders correctly listed under Eyal Ben Yitzhak with distinct ticket numbers) |
| `V6-F58` — the existing "SHA-256-verified against the frozen benchmark" regression suite (`tests/v6-6-attraction-event-import.mjs`) does not actually assert several frozen-ground-truth fields it has fixture data for, so it would stay green even if the adapter mapping for those specific fields regressed | `0.6.6` QA acceptance, discovered while investigating `V6-F56`/`V6-F57`'s `AE-005` follow-up | Direct reading of `tests/v6-6-attraction-event-import.mjs`; suite executed directly (`node tests/v6-6-attraction-event-import.mjs`), currently **10/10 passing** | Observed: `test_AE005_order_vs_ticket_number_precision` (lines 166–188) builds its fixture with `f('amount','Price','120')` (matching `AE-005`'s frozen ₪120 ground truth) but never asserts `suggestion.proposed.details.amount` — unlike `test_AE004`/`test_AE007`, which do assert `amount`, and `test_AE002`/`test_AE003`/`test_AE006`, which assert it is correctly empty. `test_AE002_baseline_no_price_in_source` (lines 63–83) builds its fixture with `seatOrSection:'Row 4 · Seat 26 · Gate B'` but never asserts `suggestion.proposed.details.ticketHolders[0].seatOrSection`. `test_AE004_auditorium_companion_pair_becomes_two_related_items` (lines 139–157) builds its fixture with three distinct `seatOrSection` values (`Row 5 · Seat 11/10/9`) but never asserts any of them either. Expected: a suite whose own header comment (lines 9–17) claims to exercise "the adapter/mapping pipeline end-to-end against all seven real cases" per the frozen ground truth should assert every frozen field it has fixture data for, the same way `test_AE001` already does for `ticketHolders[0].seatOrSection`/`ticketNumbers`. Root cause: these three specific assertions were simply never written when each test was authored — not a mapping defect (the adapter code confirmed correct for these fields under `V6-F57`'s investigation) and not itself proof the live model's real extraction is correct either; it is a coverage gap in the test, independent of whether the underlying feature works. This does not by itself indicate `AE-002`/`AE-004`/`AE-005` are broken — `V6-F57` already establishes the capture-side mapping is uniform and correct for these fields — but it means the suite currently provides no regression protection for them and cannot be cited as verifying them. | Low-Medium — not a user-facing defect, but a real gap in the acceptance safety net for three frozen benchmark values (`AE-002`/`AE-004` seat data, `AE-005` amount); should be closed alongside any `V6-F57` fix so the corrected display code has actual regression coverage from day one | **Fixed, verified** (`V64`, target `0.6.6.1`, folded into `V6-F57`) — the three missing assertions were added directly to the named existing tests; `V6-F57`'s own retest evidence (`AE-005`'s amount/ticket-number precision now visibly correct, `AE-003`'s three distinct ticket numbers correctly listed) directly exercises the same fields these assertions now protect |
| `V6-F55` — a third category of warning (merge-conflict notices, place-not-validated notices, and every model-authored `draft.warnings` entry) has never been clearable, by any means, on a suggestion or an item, before or after `V6-F54` | `0.6.6` QA, build `0.6.6-9b5b1ed0` | Manual observation, `V6-F54` QA retest (`H-001`/Panvaree Resort, after attaching the companion document `Panvaree_Hotel_booking.png`) | Observed: after attaching a second document to the already-saved Panvaree Resort item, three new warnings appeared — two "ערך חדש התגלה עבור `startAt`/`endAt`: ... — הערך השמור נשמר; יש לבדוק ולעדכן ידנית במידת הצורך" (new value discovered, existing value kept, flagged for manual review) and one "המיקום נשמר כשם המלון בלבד; לא אומתו קואורדינטות" (location saved as hotel name only, coordinates unconfirmed). A manual edit to the item did not clear any of them — regardless of which field was actually edited, which matters here (see root cause). Expected: a warning describing a resolved-or-reviewed condition should be clearable, the same way `V6-F49`'s (`unresolved`-sourced, dismiss button) and `V6-F54`'s (`needsReviewFields`-sourced, edit-triggered) warnings now both are. Root cause, confirmed by direct code inspection: this project's warnings have always fallen into exactly two clearable shapes plus this third, uncovered one. (1) `unresolved`-sourced: wrapped `{message,dismissible:true}`, cleared only by the dismiss button (`V6-F49`). (2) `needsReviewFields`-sourced: plain string, but its originating entry is tracked in `details.needsReviewFields` and matched by field label, cleared only by `reconcileStaleNeedsReview` on a genuine field-value edit (pre-existing for suggestions; extended to items by `V6-F54`). (3) Everything else — `draft.warnings` (the model's own top-level array, spread in first in `smart-import-adapter.js`'s `proposed.warnings=[...(draft.warnings||[])...]`), the place-not-validated notice (same line, hardcoded string), and, confirmed as the actual source of this observation, `preserveTrustedFieldsOnMerge`'s merge-conflict notices (`src/smart-import-adapter.js`, `warnings.push(\`ערך חדש התגלה עבור ${key}...\`)`) — is a plain string that is neither object-wrapped nor registered in `needsReviewFields` at all, so it matches neither `isDismissibleWarning` nor any `reconcileStaleNeedsReview` label comparison, regardless of which field is edited or how many times. This category was never in scope for `V6-F49` or `V6-F54` — neither investigation identified it as a distinct shape, since the original Hyatt Regency case (`V6-F49`/`V6-F50`) happened to be entirely `unresolved`-sourced. It is not a regression of either fix and predates both: this gap has existed, unchanged, on the pre-approval suggestion review screen too (`updateSuggestion` calls the same `reconcileStaleNeedsReview`, with the same blind spot) since `preserveTrustedFieldsOnMerge` was first introduced (`V6-F15`). **`V6-F54` merged into this finding (`V62`):** Eyal's `V6-F54` retest found no dismiss control visible anywhere in the UI on either the Panvaree Resort or Panan Krabi item, plus two new, worse symptoms confirming this is the same root cause, not a separate one — (1) **Panvaree Resort:** after merging the companion PNG (wrong dates) and PDF (correct dates), the warning box still reads "new value discovered for `startAt`: 2027-01-17 — saved value kept, needs manual review," describing a conflict that has already resolved correctly (the correct date IS what's saved) — the warning is stale/misleading, not merely slow to clear; (2) **Panan Krabi:** Eyal actively edited both the check-in and check-out dates, which cleared most of the warning, but **one warning still remains** even after editing every field associated with it — worse than the previously-documented "edit clears it" fallback, since that fallback is now itself shown to be incomplete. **Product Owner's proposed fix direction (to guide, not dictate, implementation):** rather than silently keeping one value and showing a warning that may never clear, actively resolve the conflict with the user — either a picker asking which value is correct (reusing the existing `directionAmbiguous`/`directionCandidates` picker pattern rather than new UI), or a value proposed from the Trip's own date range requiring explicit user confirmation. Either approach replaces "silent-keep + indefinite warning" with a real decision point. **Implemented (`V63`):** `resolveMergeCandidate` (`src/ingestion.js`) and `mergeCandidatePanel` (`src/v5-app.js`) give an explicit keep/accept choice per conflicting field, reusing the `directionPickerPanel` radio pattern, wired into both `suggestionView()` and `detailView()`; `isDismissibleWarning` generalized so any plain-string warning not currently backed by a live `needsReviewFields` label is dismissible as a fallback, closing the third-category gap for `draft.warnings`/the place-not-validated notice without needing to wrap each generation site individually; `NEEDS_REVIEW_FIELD_MAP` gained a `location` entry and `smart-import-adapter.js`'s Hotel `FIELD_MAP` gained the address-related keys `place-validation.mjs` already recognized, closing a second, independent path (a location/address-shaped needs-review conflict with no target mapping) by which a warning could survive editing every associated field — a plausible, though not database-confirmed, explanation for the Panan Krabi "one warning survives full edit" symptom. | **High** (elevated from Medium at Product Owner request upon merging `V6-F54` in — a cross-cutting warning-lifecycle defect shared by Hotel and every other Smart Import vertical, not an Activity-specific or narrow cosmetic issue) | **Implemented** (`version/V6`, `V63`), awaiting Product Owner QA retest confirmation — not recorded `Fixed`; target `0.6.6.1`. Regression tests: `test_V6_F55_resolve_merge_candidate_keep_preserves_trusted_value`, `test_V6_F55_resolve_merge_candidate_accept_applies_new_value_and_reconciles_needs_review`, `test_V6_F55_resolve_merge_candidate_is_a_noop_for_an_unknown_key`, `test_V6_F55_resolve_merge_candidate_works_on_a_saved_item_shape`, `test_V6_F55_merge_candidate_panel_wired_into_both_screens`, `test_V6_F55_merge_conflict_warnings_excluded_from_generic_dismiss_list`, `test_V6_F55_plain_string_warning_dismissible_unless_backed_by_live_needs_review` (`tests/v6-55-correction.mjs`). **Core picker mechanism confirmed working (`V64`):** Eyal verified the explicit keep/accept decision point in two independent real scenarios — the `AE-003`/`AE-004` attach-merge (title/provider/location/date conflicts all presented as a choice, correctly saved once resolved) and a fresh Panvaree Resort re-merge (chose between differing PNG/PDF values). This is the Product Owner's own requested fix direction and it is genuinely working, generalized cleanly across Hotel and Activity — not marked `Fixed` because of the open sub-issue immediately below, discovered on the same Panvaree Resort re-merge, which must be understood before this finding can close. **Open sub-issue, investigated, not yet resolved either way:** after resolving the value-conflict picker, four other warnings remained even after saving and after directly editing the conflicting dates — a duplicate/unexplained second booking number, ambiguous check-in/check-out date labeling, RTL phone-formatting ambiguity, and a truncated address. Investigated by code-path classification (no live Firestore access, same limitation as every prior investigation this session): these four fall into two structurally distinct categories that require different answers, and without the exact stored warning text/`details.needsReviewFields` contents for this specific item, classifying each one with certainty isn't possible from here — (1) **RTL phone-formatting ambiguity and the truncated address** almost certainly originate from the model's own free-text `draft.warnings`/`importantNotes` commentary — there is no editable field concept in this app for "is this phone number's RTL rendering ambiguous" or "is this address truncated," so these were never resolvable by a field edit by design, only ever by the manual dismiss now available under `V6-F55`'s own fix (`isDismissibleWarning`'s new fallback). If Eyal saw no dismiss control on these two, that directly contradicts the fix and is a real bug; if a dismiss control was present but not used (only the dates were edited), these are working as intended and the open question is purely about UI clarity — should a permanent source-documentation note be styled differently from an actionable "needs review" warning, so it doesn't read as something an edit ought to clear. (2) **The duplicate booking number and the check-in/check-out date-labeling ambiguity** are plausibly `needsReviewFields`-shaped (Hotel's adapter can produce a `confirmationNumber`- or `startDate`/`endDate`-keyed entry for exactly this shape of conflict, and both keys are correctly mapped in `NEEDS_REVIEW_FIELD_MAP`) — if the booking-number one is confirmationNumber-keyed and Eyal only edited dates, its persistence is expected (unrelated field, correctly untouched), not a bug. The date-labeling one is the more concerning case: if it really is `startDate`/`endDate`-keyed and Eyal genuinely edited those fields, it should have cleared via `reconcileStaleNeedsReview` — persisting despite that is not explained by anything found in this investigation and needs either the live item's actual warning/needsReviewFields contents, or a real-device repro with the browser console open, to root-cause further. **Bottom line: cannot be fully resolved from code inspection alone this time** — reporting the classification hypothesis and exactly what evidence would settle it, rather than guessing a specific fix. **Root-caused and implemented (`0.6.6.2`, `V65`):** confirmed by code reading (not a live-repro against this specific item's exact stored `warnings`/`needsReviewFields` contents — that caveat still stands) that the actual mechanism was `isDismissibleWarning`'s label-TEXT-substring heuristic itself: it decided "resolvable by editing this field" by checking whether a warning's rendered text merely CONTAINED a currently-live field label's text, so a free-text warning that happened to mention a common label word (e.g. "טלפון") collided with an unrelated live field entry and was misclassified as field-backed though it never was — stuck both ways at once (not really field-backed, so editing did nothing; falsely appeared field-backed, so the generic dismiss refused it too). This plausibly explains all four warnings; the duplicate booking number and date-labeling ambiguity could equally be a genuine second `needsReviewFields` entry the edit didn't fully address — noted honestly rather than claimed with certainty either way. Fixed by replacing the substring heuristic with an explicit `needsReviewRef:{key,value}` a warning carries only when actually generated FROM a `needsReviewFields` entry (`smart-import-adapter.js`/`activity-import-adapter.js`); `isDismissibleWarning` (moved to `src/ingestion.js` for direct testability, imported into `v5-app.js`) now checks that reference by exact key+value match instead of text substring — anything without one (a plain string, or an object with neither marker) is unconditionally dismissible; `reconcileStaleNeedsReview` clears a `needsReviewRef`-tagged warning by exact key match on the matching field edit, keeping the old label-substring fallback only for a warning shape not yet migrated. Regression tests: `test_V6_F55_ext_needsReviewRef_warning_not_dismissible_while_live`, `test_V6_F55_ext_needsReviewRef_warning_dismissible_once_stale`, `test_V6_F55_ext_freetext_warning_dismissible_despite_label_word_collision`, `test_V6_F55_ext_reconcile_clears_needsReviewRef_warning_by_exact_key` (`tests/v6-55-correction.mjs`). **Fixed, verified** (`0.6.6.2`, `V66`). Eyal's QA retest: each of the four previously-permanently-stuck Panvaree Resort warnings (duplicate booking number, check-in/check-out date-labeling ambiguity, RTL phone-formatting note, truncated address) now shows a dismiss control, and dismissing it clears the warning. |

**Symptom B (Panan Krabi — a warning cleared only after a genuine value change, not by simply reopening/reconfirming the item) — assessed as very likely expected, by-design behavior, not registered as a finding:** Panan Krabi's date-label-conflict warning is the same wild-card source `V6-F13`'s history refers to; this project's own earlier investigation of it (this session, before `V6-F49` existed) found its warnings used the colon-prefixed "דורש בדיקה: " format specific to `unresolved`-sourced text at that time. Eyal's report that this retest's warning shows as structured `needs_review` fields is consistent with the tightened Hotel system prompt (`V6-F49`'s fix) working as intended this time — resolving the same kind of conflict into a proper field instead of free text. If so, this warning is `needsReviewFields`-shaped, not `unresolved`-shaped, which correctly predicts both halves of what Eyal saw: no dismiss button (dismiss only ever applied to the `unresolved` shape) and a requirement to make a genuine field-value change to clear it (`reconcileStaleNeedsReview`'s `changedFields` is a strict string-inequality comparison against whatever is currently stored at save time — opening the item and re-saving the same value produces an empty changed-field set and clears nothing; this is pre-existing, unchanged behavior already true for the suggestion review screen before `V6-F54`, not something `V6-F54` introduced). This reading could not be confirmed with full certainty — it would require either the exact warning text (the dash vs. colon prefix settles it definitively) or a direct read of the evidence panel's field-level certainty for this item, neither available from here. Flagged as an assessment, not a registered finding; happy to verify for certain later if wanted.

### First `0.6.6` acceptance session — remaining investigations closed out (added `V61`)

Four more investigation prompts from `docs/claude-code-prompts/0_6_6_1/` (Follow-up 3 of the `V6-F56`/`V6-F57` thread, plus three new AE-case reports) investigated and registered below, `V6-F59`–`V6-F65`. No fix implemented for any of them.

| Finding | Observed in | Test/evidence source | Observed vs. expected behavior | Severity | Assignment status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `V6-F59` — `AE-006`'s saved item title is "WaterLand" (the venue name) instead of the frozen "כניסה לפארק יום" (day park entry ticket-type label) | `0.6.6` QA, `AE-006` retest | Direct code inspection of `src/activity-import-adapter.js` line 101 (`title:activityName`) and the `activityName` paragraph of `activitySystemPrompt` (`netlify/functions/_shared/smart-import-schema.mjs`) | Observed: saved title is the venue's own name. Expected (frozen ground truth, discovery doc `V4`, which explicitly corrects an earlier draft that had mistakenly used a zoo-themed title): "כניסה לפארק יום". Root cause: `title:activityName` (line 101) passes the model's `draft.activityName` straight through with no fallback-to-provider logic anywhere in the adapter — ruled out as a code bug by direct inspection, there is no transformation in this codebase that could turn a correct ticket-type label into the venue name. The live model's own output must have been "WaterLand". The prompt's `activityName` paragraph explicitly permits "the venue/attraction **or** event name" as two alternative satisfying categories with no stated priority rule for a source (like `AE-006`) that shows both a prominent venue name and a smaller ticket-type label — unlike `location`'s explicit property-first discipline, there is no analogous priority rule for `activityName`.
