Notably the prompt's own worked example for the structurally identical `AE-003` shape ("כניסה למוזיאון", general museum entry) already demonstrates the desired pattern correctly, which argues this may be either an isolated live-extraction inconsistency on this one document, or a genuine ambiguity in the instruction that the example alone doesn't fully close — cannot be settled further without live model access. | Medium — fails one benchmark case's title check outright, but the adapter code itself is correct/verbatim and the prompt already has one correct worked example of this exact pattern; likely fixable by adding an explicit venue-vs-ticket-type-label priority rule to `activitySystemPrompt`, mirroring `location`'s property-first paragraph | **Reopened — root-caused, not fixed** (`V64`); target `0.6.6.1` correction resolved the original symptom but not the actual requirement, awaiting Product Owner decision on a follow-up package. Eyal's retest: the immediate bug (title = "WaterLand," the bare venue name) is gone, but the fix's replacement ("Entry Ticket," the bare ticket-type label alone) isn't what's wanted either — **new, more specific requirement from the Product Owner:** combine venue name and ticket type, e.g. "WaterLand - Day Entry" / "WaterLand - כניסה לפארק יום," not either alone. Root cause, confirmed by direct comparison against the shipped prompt text: the `V6-F59` fix added a strict *either/or* preference rule ("prefer the specific ticket-type/admission label as activityName, not the bare venue name... only fall back to the venue name itself when the source genuinely shows no more specific ticket-type/admission label at all") — this instruction was never written to produce a combined value, only to pick one of the two, so the model correctly followed it and picked the ticket-type label alone, exactly as instructed. Closing this requires rewriting the `activityName` paragraph's priority rule as a combination instruction (venue name + ticket type, in that order) rather than an either/or preference. **Implemented (`0.6.6.2`, `V65`):** `activitySystemPrompt`'s `activityName` paragraph rewritten from a strict either/or preference to a combination rule — when a source shows both a venue/brand name and a distinct ticket-type/admission label, report BOTH together as `activityName` ("<venue name> - <ticket type>", e.g. "WaterLand - Day Entry" / "WaterLand - כניסה לפארק יום"), falling back to whichever ONE the source actually shows only when the other is genuinely absent. Prompt-only change — the adapter's `title:activityName` passthrough is unchanged (re-verified by direct reading, not assumed). Regression test: `test_V6_F59_title_preserves_combined_venue_and_ticket_type` (`tests/v6-6-attraction-event-import.mjs`) — asserts the adapter preserves an already-combined `activityName` verbatim, guarding against a future adapter change that tries to split or cherry-pick one half. **Fixed, verified** (`0.6.6.2`, `V66`). Eyal's QA retest: a fresh `AE-006` reimport shows the title as a combination of venue name and ticket type ("WaterLand - Entry Ticket"), not either alone. |
| `V6-F60` — a date-only value (no time in the source) renders with a synthetic "12:00" that is visually indistinguishable anywhere in the app from a genuinely extracted time | `0.6.6` QA, `AE-006` retest ("24 באפר', 2024, 12:00" shown for a source with no time at all) | Direct code inspection: `src/activity-import-adapter.js` line 152 (`dateTime()`), `src/operational-data.js` line 158 (`parseTimeValue()`), `src/v5-app.js` line 37 (`fmt()`) and line 150 (`detailView()`'s `fmt(item.startAt)` call) | Observed: `AE-006`'s date/time display shows a specific 12:00 time. Expected per frozen ground truth: this source is explicitly a day-ticket with no time shown at all — no time should appear to read as extracted. **Confirmed by direct code trace, not model fabrication:** `dateTime(date,time)` (`activity-import-adapter.js:152`) computes `const clock=parseTimeValue(time)||'12:00'` — `parseTimeValue('')` (`operational-data.js:158-164`) provably returns `''` for empty input, so the `||'12:00'` fallback is a deliberate, code-level neutral default for a date-only value (exactly matching the existing regression test's own comment for this exact case: `tests/v6-6-attraction-event-import.mjs` line 223, `'T12:00 is the neutral compatibility time for a date-only value'`). The adapter does correctly track this distinction separately in `dateMeta.startPrecision:'date'` (line 126) — but `fmt()` (`v5-app.js:37`) and every call site of it, including `detailView()`'s `fmt(item.startAt)` (line 150, called with no second argument), never consult `dateMeta.startPrecision` at all — `fmt()` always renders the hour/minute portion unconditionally. This is a generic gap, not `activity`/`AE-006`-specific: every item type across the app that can produce a date-only `startAt` (Hotel, Flight, Activity) has this same synthetic-time-looks-real problem. | Low — not incorrect data, but a real, systemic display-trust gap: a user has no way to tell a real extracted time from a neutral placeholder anywhere in the app | **Fixed, verified** (`V64`, target `0.6.6.1`) — Eyal's retest: a fresh `AE-006` reimport shows the date with no time at all in the main detail view, no more misleading "12:00" |
| `V6-F61` — `AE-002`'s saved item shows "Purchaser: Purchaser: אייל בן יצחק" (the label doubled, and in English while every sibling label on the page is Hebrew) | `0.6.6` QA, `AE-002` retest | Repository-wide grep for the literal string "Purchaser" — **zero matches anywhere in application source**, ruling out a hardcoded template/default string; direct code inspection of `src/activity-import-adapter.js`'s `otherFields`/`noteLines` fallback (lines 55, 68, 87) | Observed: the label text appears twice before the name, in English. Expected: a single, Hebrew-localized label (or no such field at all if genuinely unmodeled). Root cause, the only mechanism in the codebase consistent with the exact observed string (confirmed nowhere else "Purchaser" is hardcoded): the "purchaser/orderer" concept has no canonical field key in `FIELD_TARGETS`/`activitySystemPrompt`'s field vocabulary at all — the same class of gap `V6-F56` found for DNI. An unmapped field the model itself labels and keys (e.g. `key:"purchaser"`, `label:"Purchaser"`) falls through to `else otherFields.push(field)` (line 68), and `otherFields.map(field=>`${field.label}: ${field.rawValue}`)` (line 87) then renders `${field.label}: ${field.rawValue}` into `item.notes` with no guard against `field.rawValue` itself already containing a copy of the printed label text (plausible if the model transcribed the ticket's own printed "Purchaser: <name>" string verbatim into `rawValue` while separately choosing `label:"Purchaser"` for the same field) — this exact mechanism reproduces the observed doubled string precisely. The English label is explained the same way: since "purchaser" has no canonical, pre-translated Hebrew UI label like every other officially-mapped field, whatever language the model freely chooses for its own ad hoc `label` leaks directly into the UI unfiltered. Could not be confirmed against the live model's actual raw output (no DB access), but no other code path in the repository could produce this exact string, and the "no canonical field" explanation is directly provable from the schema/vocabulary alone. | Low — cosmetic, notes-only, does not corrupt any structured field; fixable either by adding a canonical `purchaser`/orderer field with a proper Hebrew label, or by stripping a `field.label`-prefix from `field.rawValue` before concatenating in the generic fallback | **Reopened — root-caused, not fixed** (`V64`); target `0.6.6.1` correction was incomplete, awaiting Product Owner decision on a follow-up package. `AE-002`'s original purchaser instance is genuinely fixed — Eyal's retest confirms a single Hebrew-localized label there — but the identical symptom reappeared on a fresh `AE-005` reimport: "Payment status: Payment status: paid," "Booked by: Booked by: [name]," "Ticket number: Ticket number: 2ZTN-G53R-GJ81P," all doubled and in English. Root cause, confirmed by direct code inspection, broader than the original fix reached: (1) the fix (`normalizedGenericLabel`/`stripDuplicateLabelPrefix`) was applied only to `activity-import-adapter.js`'s `otherFields`→notes mapping — the byte-identical, entirely unfixed pattern (`otherFields.map(field=>\`${field.label}: ${field.rawValue}\`)`, no stripping, no translation) exists in `smart-import-adapter.js` (Hotel) too, confirmed by direct comparison — Hotel is equally vulnerable and was never touched; (2) both adapters' separate `importantNotes` mapping (`(draft.importantNotes||[]).map(note=>\`${note.title}: ${note.text}\`)`) has zero stripping/translation applied in either adapter — if the model reports an unmapped concept via `importantNotes` instead of `fields[]` (a real, plausible alternate path for the same "no canonical slot" root cause), it reproduces the identical doubled/English symptom completely unaffected by the existing fix; (3) the translation half of the fix relies on a small hardcoded synonym dictionary (`purchaser`/`buyer`/`orderer` only) that cannot scale to arbitrary field concepts a live model may invent ("Payment status," "Booked by" are not in it) — confirmed Flight is NOT affected by any of this, since `flight-import-adapter.js`'s `notes` field is always empty (`notes:''`), so this pattern never executes there at all. A genuinely centralized fix needs to apply generic label/value duplicate-stripping at every note-line construction site in both adapters (both the `otherFields` and `importantNotes` mappings), and likely stop relying on a hardcoded translation dictionary rather than trying to keep enumerating synonyms one at a time. **Implemented (`0.6.6.2`, `V65`):** `normalizedGenericLabel`/`stripDuplicateLabelPrefix` moved out of `activity-import-adapter.js` into `src/operational-data.js` as the ONE shared implementation (dictionary extended with `payment status`/`booked by`/`ticket number`, though full translation coverage was explicitly not the required fix — the dedup mechanism is), applied at all four note-line generation sites across both adapters: Hotel's `otherFields` mapping (previously unfixed, byte-identical to Activity's original bug), and both adapters' `importantNotes` mapping (previously untouched entirely — very likely the actual cause of the reopened `AE-005` evidence, which reads as `importantNotes` entries). Activity's `otherFields` site was already correct and is simply repointed at the shared import. Regression tests: `test_V6_F61_activity_important_notes_label_not_doubled` (`tests/v6-6-attraction-event-import.mjs`), `test_V6_F61_hotel_other_fields_label_not_doubled`, `test_V6_F61_hotel_important_notes_label_not_doubled` (`tests/v6-correction.mjs`). **Fixed, verified** (`0.6.6.2`, `V66`), for the scope this fix targeted. Eyal's QA retest: a fresh `AE-005` reimport shows no more literal doubled labels ("Payment status," "Booked by," "Ticket number" each appear once). A related but structurally distinct sub-case surfaced on the same retest (a translated canonical label followed by a differently-worded, same-meaning source-embedded prefix, which exact-string duplicate detection does not catch) — registered separately as `V6-F67` rather than reopening this finding; see that row. |
| `V6-F62` — `activity` item type has no attach-existing-document control at all, unlike Hotel/Flight | `0.6.6` QA, `AE-003`/`AE-004` retest | Direct code inspection: `src/v5-app.js` line 150, `detailView()`'s actions block | Observed (Eyal's own hypothesis, confirmed): `AE-003` and `AE-004` (the Bloomfield Science Museum companion/duplicate-source pair) landed as two fully independent items with no way to link them. Expected: per the frozen discovery doc, this pair "must land as two related items or one item with two attached sources." Root cause, confirmed directly from code: `detailView()`'s actions block reads `${['hotel','flight'].includes(item.type)?`<button ... data-action="attach-source" ...>הוספת מסמך</button>`:''}` — the attach-document button is hardcoded to exactly two item types. `activity` (and every other type — car, restaurant, insurance) has no attach control rendered at all, so there was never a way for Eyal to attach `AE-004` to the already-saved `AE-003` item even if he had wanted to, regardless of whatever duplicate-detection logic exists. This is a missing capability, not a duplicate-detection regression: `findPossibleDuplicates` (`src/ingestion.js` lines 88-111, wired into the live global-Add review flow via `src/v5-app.js`) is real, working code — proven by the passing `test_AE004_auditorium_companion_pair_becomes_two_related_items` regression test, which shows it correctly flags `AE-004` as a possible duplicate/companion of an already-approved `AE-003` item when given matching `provider`+date. Its exact-string matching rule (`sameType&&((sameProvider&&sameDate)||(sameTitle&&sameDate))`, `src/ingestion.js:109-110`, `normalized()` is only trim/lowercase/whitespace-collapse — no fuzzy matching) is precisely why it likely never fired on Eyal's *real* global-Add upload of both documents even independent of the missing attach control: see `V6-F63`, whose provider-extraction defect ("SmarTicket.co.il" vs. "SmarTicket" — not even identical to each other, let alone matching "Bloomfield Science Museum Jerusalem") would break the `sameProvider` precondition this dedup path depends on, while the titles are legitimately, correctly different by design (general entrance vs. specific show) so `sameTitle` was never going to catch it either. Both mechanisms (missing attach control, and the dedup path's dependency on a provider value `V6-F63` shows was wrong) plausibly contributed; the missing attach control is the more fundamental gap since Hotel's own precedent (Panvaree Resort, this same session) proves attach-after-the-fact is the intended, expected companion-source workflow. | Medium — blocks a core, frozen companion-source workflow for an entire item type, not an edge case (Bloomfield's general-entrance-plus-paid-show shape is a common real pattern for museums/venues) | **Fixed, verified** (`V64`, target `0.6.6.1`) — Eyal's retest: the attach control exists on activity items, and attaching `AE-004` to an existing `AE-003` item correctly became a second source on the same item, not a duplicate second item |
| `V6-F63` — `activity` extraction's `provider` field has no prompt guidance on venue-vs-ticketing-platform priority, producing inconsistent, platform-named results | `0.6.6` QA, `AE-003`/`AE-004` retest | Direct code inspection: `netlify/functions/_shared/smart-import-schema.mjs`, full text search of `activitySystemPrompt` for "provider" — zero paragraphs of guidance found, only the bare schema declaration (`provider:{type:'string'}`, line 96) | Observed: `AE-003`'s saved item shows `provider:"SmarTicket.co.il"`, `AE-004` shows `provider:"SmarTicket"` — two different strings for what should be the identical venue. Expected (frozen ground truth for both cases): `provider: Bloomfield Science Museum Jerusalem ... sold via SmarTicket.co.il` — ground truth treats the museum as `provider` and SmarTicket as a distinct "sold via" concept, not the same field (contrast `AE-002`, where ground truth explicitly makes the ticketing platform itself the `provider` — so this is a real, source-dependent distinction the prompt needs to make, not a fixed rule). Root cause: unlike `activityName` and `location`, both of which get dedicated explanatory paragraphs in `activitySystemPrompt` establishing exactly this kind of priority discipline, `provider` has zero prompt guidance at all beyond the one-line schema type declaration — the model has nothing telling it to prefer the venue over a ticketing platform when a source shows both, so it inconsistently picked the platform on both `AE-003` and `AE-004` (not even the same string between them, ruling out this being merely "the wrong but consistent choice"). This is also the direct explanation for why `V6-F62`'s dedup path never had a chance to catch the pair even via the global-Add route. | Medium — a required-when-present field per Decision 4, and the specific defect that breaks `V6-F62`'s dedup path; fixable by adding an explicit provider-priority paragraph to `activitySystemPrompt`, mirroring `location`'s | **Fixed, verified** (`V64`, target `0.6.6.1`) — Eyal's retest via the `AE-003`/`AE-004` attach-merge picker: provider shows "Bloomfield Science Museum Jerusalem" on both sides of the conflict, no "SmarTicket" anywhere |
| `V6-F64` — `AE-004`'s `location` shows only the sub-venue ("אודיטוריום") with the venue name dropped entirely | `0.6.6` QA, `AE-004` retest | Direct code inspection: the `location` paragraph of `activitySystemPrompt` (`netlify/functions/_shared/smart-import-schema.mjs`) | Observed: `AE-004`'s `מיקום:` field shows only "אודיטוריום" (Auditorium); `AE-003` (same venue, general entrance) correctly shows the full museum name for the same field. Expected (frozen ground truth): "Bloomfield Science Museum Jerusalem, Auditorium (אודיטוריום)" — both venue and sub-venue combined. Root cause: the `location` paragraph establishes the property-first discipline ("must identify the specific venue/attraction, never a general park, city, district or region as a stand-in for it") and separately instructs that a genuinely distinct meeting/transfer point belongs in its own `meeting_point` field — but gives no instruction at all for the case actually present here: a *sub-venue within* the main venue (an auditorium, a specific hall, a wing) that should be *combined with*, not substituted for, the venue name. This is a possible property-first location-accuracy issue per Decision 4 ("required when present, not [a no-omission] blocker" — but still graded ground truth), not merely cosmetic, since "Auditorium" alone doesn't identify which venue's auditorium — consistent with `AE-003`'s own correct result on the identical venue, confirming this is a source-dependent extraction gap for the sub-venue case specifically, not a systemic location failure. | **High** (elevated from an initial Medium proposal at Product Owner request, `V62`: location accuracy is one of Decision 4's explicit no-omission blockers for the `activity` item type, not a required-when-present field like `V6-F63`'s `provider` — this fails a hard project rule, not merely a nicety, since "Auditorium" alone doesn't identify which venue) — fixable by adding a sub-venue-combination instruction to `activitySystemPrompt`'s `location` paragraph | **Fixed, verified** (`V64`, target `0.6.6.1`) — Eyal's retest via the same picker: the location conflict correctly offered "Bloomfield Science Museum Jerusalem, Auditorium" as the new value |
| `V6-F65` — `AE-007`'s `provider`/`location` show a specific real-world venue name ("גן החיות התנ״כי ירושלים" / Jerusalem Biblical Zoo) that does not appear in any of the source's independently-verified legible plain text | `0.6.6` QA, `AE-007` retest | Direct visual inspection of the actual source file, `tests/fixtures/attraction_benchmark/AE-007_zoo_ticket_screenshot.png` (cropped, rotated and magnified via PowerShell `System.Drawing` for legibility, since the Read tool's default rendering was too small to fully verify); code inspection of `netlify/functions/_shared/place-validation.mjs` | Observed: both `provider` and `location` show the specific brand name. Frozen ground truth (discovery doc): the source only shows the generic label "גן החיות" (the zoo) — "venue's own precise name is not legible beyond this generic label in the source — frozen as a real location-precision limitation, not resolved by assumption." **Directly confirmed by reading the actual source image**: the ticket's main printed body text reads "כניסה לגן החיות 2020" (Entry to the Zoo 2020) — generic, no brand name; the PDF-viewer's own filename title bar, also visible in the screenshot, independently reads "...צתי גן חיות pdf(1)" — also generic, no brand name. Two independent, fully legible plain-text occurrences on the source, both consistent with the frozen ground truth's claim. The source also contains one small stylized decorative logo/wordmark graphic (bottom-right of the ticket, alongside an animal-silhouette pictogram) that could not be read with confidence despite repeated crop/rotate/magnify attempts at up to 10x — the image's native resolution (a screenshot of a screenshot, per the discovery doc's own description) does not support confident letter-by-letter reading of that stylized font, so genuine uncertainty remains about that one specific piece of the source, honestly flagged rather than guessed either way. **Ruled out as an alternative explanation, directly from code:** the place-validation mechanism (`validatePlace()`, `place-validation.mjs` line 24) sets `locationDisplayValue:name` — always echoing back the originally-extracted name, never the Google Places API's own canonical `candidate.displayName.text` — so a Places lookup could not have introduced a more specific name into `location`; and `provider` (`activity-import-adapter.js` line 102, `String(draft.provider||'').trim()`) is never touched by place validation at all. Both fields' specific values therefore trace directly back to the model's own raw extraction output, not to any code-level substitution. Combined, this is strong evidence — though not fully absolute, given the one unread logo — that this is a genuine violation of `activitySystemPrompt`'s own existing general instruction ("Do not fabricate, silently correct, infer from general knowledge... leave a string field empty... rather than guessing"), the exact failure mode this benchmark case was specifically designed to catch. This is a live-model compliance issue, not a missing code/schema guardrail (contrast `V6-F56`, where the guardrail itself doesn't exist) — the existing instruction is already correct and present; the model did not follow it on this real extraction. | High — per the investigation prompt's own instruction, given the balance of evidence; this is a direct, on-point failure of a benchmark case purpose-built to test against exactly this behavior, and a fabricated specific identity is materially worse than a merely-missing field. **Confirmed effectively-confirmed by Product Owner (`V62`):** Google Places lookup already ruled out as the source of the specific name; the one unresolved detail (the small stylized logo graphic that couldn't be read with confidence) is noted as a caveat but does not block treating this as confirmed. | **Reopened — root-caused, not fixed** (`V64`); target `0.6.6.1` correction reduced the surface symptom but did not fix the underlying behavior, awaiting Product Owner decision on a follow-up package. Eyal's retest: a fresh `AE-007` reimport correctly shows `location` as the generic "גן החיות" (that part of the fix worked), but `provider` now shows "החברה להגנת הטבע" (Society for the Protection of Nature) — a different specific organization, attributed by the item's own warning text to being "identified from the logo/text on the ticket." **Eyal confirmed directly against the real source that this text does not appear anywhere on the ticket.** So the known-brand-substitution failure mode the original fix targeted is gone, but a more subtle, arguably more dangerous version of the same underlying defect remains: when a source element (here, a small stylized logo) is visually present but not confidently legible, the model still outputs a specific plausible organization name rather than saying it can't be determined — and packages that guess with a citation ("identified from the logo") that makes it read as more credible, not less. Root cause, confirmed by direct schema inspection: `provider` and `activityName` are bare top-level strings in `activityImportSchema` (`type:'string'`, no paired certainty field), unlike every entry in `fields[]`, which carries its own `certainty:'exact'|'needs_review'|'unreadable'`. The `fields[]` key vocabulary also has no `provider`/organizer slot at all. So the model has no structured way anywhere in the schema to express "I can see something here but can't confirm what it says" for this specific field — it can only output a confident-looking string or leave it empty; there is no visible "unconfirmed" state available for it to choose, regardless of how the prompt is worded. The `V6-F65` anti-fabrication paragraph already shipped addresses "don't substitute a plausible KNOWN BRAND for a generic label" specifically, not this distinct "don't guess at all when a graphic can't be confirmed" case — closing this fully likely needs either a schema change (a certainty/hedge mechanism for `provider`/`activityName`) or an explicit instruction that an illegible graphic element must be treated as absent rather than a basis for a specific guess, not just a stronger negative example. **Implemented (`0.6.6.2`, `V65`):** `activityImportSchema` gained paired `activityNameCertainty`/`providerCertainty` enums (`exact`/`needs_review`/`unreadable`, both added to `required`), giving the model a structural way to say "visible but illegible" instead of guessing; `activitySystemPrompt` gained a paragraph explaining exactly when to use each value, restating the anti-fabrication rule concretely for this specific, previously-ungoverned case. `activity-import-adapter.js` adds defense in depth on top of the prompt fix: on `unreadable`, the value is force-blanked regardless of what the model actually returned (proven by a fixture that simulates the model still returning a guessed name despite declaring it unreadable) and a `needsReviewRef`-backed `needsReviewFields`/`fieldConfidence` entry is added; on `needs_review`, the value is kept but still flagged. Regression tests: `test_V6_F65_ext_unreadable_provider_certainty_blanks_value_and_flags_illegible`, `test_V6_F65_ext_exact_provider_certainty_unaffected`, `test_V6_F65_ext_unreadable_activityName_certainty_blanks_value_and_flags_illegible`, `test_V6_F65_ext_needs_review_certainty_keeps_value_and_flags` (`tests/v6-6-attraction-event-import.mjs`). **Fixed, verified** (`0.6.6.2`, `V66`), for the scope this fix targeted. Eyal's QA retest: a fresh `AE-007` reimport now returns a confident, specific provider name that Eyal confirmed against the actual source is genuinely correct — no longer an unverifiable/false "identified from the logo" claim. This exercised the normal `certainty:'exact'` path (the source turned out to be legible enough this time), not the new `unreadable` path — that mechanism's own behavior (blank + flag when genuinely illegible) is covered by the fixture-based regression tests, not by this specific live retest, since a live source that happens to be legible can't exercise it. An unrelated minor issue surfaced on the same item's ticket-holder name field — registered separately as `V6-F68`. |
| `V6-F66` — attaching a second per-person Activity ticket document to an existing item silently REPLACES the first document's ticket-holder/participant data instead of combining them, real data loss | `0.6.6.1` QA, mandatory wild-card retest (two real Hapoel Tel-Aviv vs. Beitar Jerusalem match tickets, one per person) | Manual observation: Eyal attached both documents to one `activity` item, expecting both people as ticket holders (the same shape as `AE-001`'s two-passenger case, just split across two source files); direct code inspection of `src/ingestion.js`'s `suggestionToItem()` and `src/smart-import-adapter.js`'s `preserveTrustedFieldsOnMerge()` | Observed: only the second-attached person (Paula Cohen) survived in the saved item; Eyal's own ticket (Gate 8, entrance 426, section 76B, row 35, seat 201) is completely absent, not merely unrendered — genuine data loss, not a `V6-F57`-style display gap. Expected: both per-person ticket-holder entries present, evidence-linked, the same "union, never silently replace" guarantee Flight's own multi-passenger merge already provides. Root cause, confirmed by direct code inspection: `suggestionToItem()` (`src/ingestion.js` line 131-133) merges `details` for every non-flight type with a plain object spread — `{...(existing.details||{}),...(p.details||{})}` — so when both the existing item's and the newly-merged suggestion's `details` contain a `ticketHolders` (and `ticketNumbers`) key, the second source's array wins outright; nothing unions the two arrays the way Flight's `mergeFlightDetails`/`mergeFlightPassengers` (matched by e-ticket number or normalized name) already does for exactly this class of problem on the Flight side. The same gap independently affects the top-level `participants` array too: `suggestionToItem`'s generic `value(key,fallback)` helper (line 116) picks the incoming suggestion's array outright whenever it is non-empty, never unioning it with the existing item's own non-empty array. `preserveTrustedFieldsOnMerge` (`src/smart-import-adapter.js`) offers no protection either — its `MERGE_FIELDS` list (`title/provider/confirmationNumber/location/website/phone/startAt/endAt`) covers only top-level scalars, never `participants` or any `details.*` array. This is not Activity-specific code with a bug in it — Activity has no merge-specific code for this at all; it inherits the same generic, array-unaware merge path every non-flight type has always had, which was harmless before `V6-F62` added the first realistic way to attach a second per-person Activity document to an existing item. | High — real, silent participant/ticket-holder data loss on a documented, no-omission-style requirement (mirrors `V6-F56`'s severity class: a benchmark-grade guarantee genuinely failing, not a display gap); discovered via the mandatory wild-card test specifically because it exercises a real multi-document merge the frozen `AE-00x` benchmark set never happened to test (every `AE-` case's ticket holders come from ONE source document, never split across two attach-and-extract merges) | **Implemented** (`version/V6`, `0.6.6.2`/`V65`): `mergeActivityTicketHolders`/`mergeActivityDetails` added to `src/activity-import-adapter.js`, mirroring Flight's already-solved `mergeFlightPassengers`/`mergeFlightDetails` — match by `ticketNumber` when present (most reliable, mirrors Flight's e-ticket-number priority) else normalized `name`, union all entries from both sides so no ticket holder from either side is ever dropped, existing non-blank field wins per matched entry with blanks filled from the incoming side; a small set of Activity detail scalars get the same "existing wins unless blank" rule, and `ticketNumbers` is deduped-unioned. `suggestionToItem` (`src/ingestion.js`) now branches on `type==='activity'` the same way it already does for `type==='flight'`, importing `mergeActivityDetails`; the top-level `participants` array is fixed the same way, but ONLY for `type==='activity'` — every other type's existing either/or `value('participants',...)` behavior is left untouched, to avoid any unintended behavior change for Hotel/Flight/manual items. Regression tests: `test_V6_F66_merge_activity_ticket_holders_preserves_both_people`, `test_V6_F66_merge_activity_participants_union_not_overwrite`, `test_V6_F66_merge_activity_ticket_holders_fills_blank_fields_from_either_side` (`tests/v6-6-attraction-event-import.mjs`) — the first two mirror Eyal's own wild-card repro (two real per-person tickets attached in two passes), the third proves a partial-overlap (same `ticketNumber`, complementary blank fields) merges rather than one side winning wholesale. **Fixed, verified** (`0.6.6.2`, `V66`). Eyal's QA retest: repeated the original wild-card two-document attach test (his own ticket, then Paula Cohen's, attached to the same item) — both people's ticket-holder entries are now present in the saved item, with both names in participants. |
| `V6-F67` — a translated canonical label followed by a differently-worded, same-meaning source-embedded prefix reads as a redundant compound line (e.g. "מזמין: הוזמן על ידי: אייל בן יצחק" — "Purchaser: Ordered by: Eyal Ben Yitzhak") | `0.6.6.2` QA retest, `V6-F61` verification pass | Manual observation, real item retest (Eyal) | Observed: the label "מזמין" (purchaser, via `normalizedGenericLabel`'s translation of the model's own canonical `label`, e.g. "Orderer") is immediately followed by the source's own embedded prefix "הוזמן על ידי:" ("ordered by:") inside the same value — a different phrasing of the same concept, not a literal repeat of "מזמין" itself. Expected: a single, non-redundant line. Root cause, confirmed by direct code inspection exactly as flagged: `stripDuplicateLabelPrefix` (`src/operational-data.js`) only stripped a value's own embedded prefix when it was an exact, case-insensitive literal match against `field.label` — no concept of two different strings meaning the same thing, so a canonical label translated to Hebrew (`normalizedGenericLabel`) and a source-language prefix already embedded in `rawValue` never matched textually even though a human reads them as saying the same thing twice. Specifically: the model's label ("Orderer") translates via `GENERIC_FIELD_LABELS` to "מזמין", but the source's own printed prefix was "הוזמן על ידי:" — which this same dictionary already stores as `booked by`'s own translation, just never checked against a DIFFERENT label's synonym before. | Low — cosmetic, notes-only, narrower than `V6-F61`'s original scope (that fix's own literal-duplicate detection is confirmed working; this is a distinct synonym/translation case it was never designed to catch) | **Implemented** (`version/V6`) — `operational-data.js` gained `LABEL_SYNONYM_GROUPS` (currently one group: `purchaser`/`buyer`/`orderer`/`purchased by`/`booked by`, the concept `V6-F67`'s real case involves), and `stripDuplicateLabelPrefix` now checks a value's embedded prefix against every phrasing in the current label's synonym group (each synonym itself AND its own Hebrew translation), not just the current label's own literal text — the original exact-literal-match check is still the FIRST candidate tried, so `V6-F61`'s already-fixed case is unaffected. `GENERIC_FIELD_LABELS`/`normalizedGenericLabel` themselves are unchanged. Regression tests: `test_V6_F67_strip_prefix_recognizes_synonymous_phrasing_not_just_literal_repeat` (direct unit test of the exported helper, also re-proves the `V6-F61` exact-duplicate case and the no-group/no-match fallback), `test_V6_F67_hotel_other_fields_synonymous_prefix_not_doubled` (end-to-end through the real adapter, reproducing the exact reported "מזמין: הוזמן על ידי: ..." string) — both in `tests/v6-correction.mjs`. Full quality gate passes (`node --check`, 122/122 test assertions across 19 files, production build). **Fixed, verified** (`V70`) — Eyal's retest on a fresh `AE-005` reimport confirms the targeted symptom is gone: the purchaser note no longer repeats a translated label immediately followed by its own synonym prefix, now reading as a single, non-redundant line. A different, low-severity residual issue was observed on the same item — a note line mixes a Hebrew translated label with an untranslated English source sentence in one line (e.g. "סטטוס תשלום: Payment status is paid.") — a distinct translation-coverage gap, not the synonym-duplicate pattern this fix targeted. **Product Owner explicitly declined further investigation or fix** ("still a minor bug — I would not spend additional effort on it"); accepted as-is, not registered as a separate finding, no further action planned. No target/package version needed — already fixed on `version/V6`. |
| `V6-F68` — a ticket holder's name renders with a one-character misread ("אילי" instead of "אייל"/Eyal) on a fresh `AE-007` reimport | `0.6.6.2` QA retest, `V6-F65` verification pass | Manual observation, real item retest (Eyal) | Observed: the saved item's ticket-holder table shows the name "בן יצחק אילי" where the correct name (matching every other benchmark case's use of the same real person) is "אייל בן יצחק" (Eyal Ben Yitzhak). Expected: the name transcribed correctly. Root cause, confirmed by tracing every step of the pipeline: **not a code defect — isolated live-model transcription inconsistency.** `smartImportActivityResultToSuggestion` (`src/activity-import-adapter.js`) maps a ticket holder's name with `name:String(holder.name||'').trim()` — no substring operation, regex replace, or character reordering of any kind. `mergeActivityTicketHolders` (`V6-F66`, `0.6.6.2`) only ever selects one side's WHOLE name string (`current.name||holder.name`), never touches individual characters; its `normalizedHolderName` match-key helper (`trim().toLowerCase().replace(/\s+/g,' ')`) is used only to build the merge lookup key, never to build the stored value, and does no reordering either. `participants` is a `Set`-deduped pass of the same untouched strings. Rendering (`activityTicketHoldersTable`, `src/v5-app.js`) reads `holder.name` directly with zero transformation. No code path anywhere in this application is capable of transposing two letters — the entire pipeline from raw model output to final render is proven to be a pure passthrough (aside from whitespace trimming). This conclusively rules out a genuine adapter/normalization defect; the one-character misread ("אילי" for "אייל") must have originated in the live model's own reading of that specific source image. | Low — a single-character name misread on one field, not a structural or data-loss issue like `V6-F56`/`V6-F66`; confirmed isolated rather than systemic, since the investigation proves no code mechanism exists that could produce this pattern repeatably | **Investigated — no code defect found, no fix implemented, none warranted.** There is nothing in this codebase to fix: the pipeline is proven to never transform a ticket holder's name beyond trimming. A protective regression test was added (`test_V6_F68_ticket_holder_name_is_never_transformed_by_the_pipeline`, `tests/v6-6-attraction-event-import.mjs`) proving this passthrough behavior directly, so a future change that accidentally introduces any name transformation would be caught here rather than silently producing a real, code-level version of this same symptom. Full quality gate passes. Disposition: **Closed — confirmed non-defect** (`V70`). Eyal's own retest: reimporting the same `AE-007` source produced the correct name ("אייל") this time, with no recurrence of the one-character misread — confirming the investigation's conclusion that this was an isolated live-model transcription inconsistency, not a systemic pattern. The protective regression test (`test_V6_F68_ticket_holder_name_is_never_transformed_by_the_pipeline`) stays in place as a guard against any future real, code-level version of this symptom. |
| `V6-F69` — the generic "Add by link" flow for a brand-new item (global Add, category not yet known) fails on every URL, not just source-specific ones — `analyzeSource()` never forwards the submitted URL to the server at all | Eyal's own use, a real Rivhit invoice PDF link (`https://api.rivhit.co.il/pdf/FileService.svc/GetDocument/580040541/522e0eee-b9ff-47c9-a57b-3dcc04f3b4f2/true/02_005248`), submitted via global Add → קישור (Link) → Analyze and propose | Direct code inspection: `src/smart-import-client.js` (`analyzeSource` line 13, `runSource` lines 30-49), `src/v5-app.js` (`runSmartAnalysis`'s `analyzeSource({trip:state.trip,source,file})` call), `netlify/functions/smart-import.mjs` (`analyze_source` branch, confirms `prepareSource(body.source,true)` — server already accepts a URL here); `tests/v6-5-flight-import.mjs` line 404 (only existing reference to `analyzeSource`, confirmed to assert only the v5-app.js call-site pattern, not `analyzeSource`'s own URL handling) | Observed: submitting the link produced the alert "יש לבחור PDF או תמונה.\nהמקור המקורי נשמר ולא נוצר פריט." (must choose a PDF or image; original source saved, no item created) — even though a URL, not a file, was exactly what was submitted, and the source was correctly saved as a `link`-kind source first. Expected: the same server-side URL-acquisition path already proven working for Hotel (`analyzeHotelSource`) and Activity (`analyzeActivitySource`) — fetch and classify/extract from the URL. Root cause, confirmed directly from code: `analyzeSource({trip,source,file})` has no `url` parameter in its signature at all and calls `runSource({operation:'analyze_source',trip,source,file,url:'',allowUrl:true})` with `url` hardcoded to `''`, unconditionally, regardless of `source.kind`/`source.url`. `runSource`'s branching (`if(url){submit as url}else{if(!file)throw new Error('יש לבחור PDF או תמונה.')...}`) always takes the `else` path for this call site; a link source has no `file` (`smartIngest` sets `file=kind==='link'?null:pendingSourceFile`), so the throw is immediate and unconditional — no network request is ever attempted, for any URL, regardless of its content-type, robots.txt, or reachability. This is a plain omitted-parameter bug, not a source-specific failure: `analyzeHotelSource({trip,source,file,url})` and `analyzeActivitySource({trip,source,file,url})`, the two sibling functions in the same file, both correctly destructure and forward `url`. Server-side, `analyze_source`'s handler already passes `allowUrl:true` to `prepareSource`, confirming the server was already built to accept and classify a URL-based source through this exact operation — the gap is entirely client-side. No regression test exercises this at all. | High — this is the primary, advertised entry point for adding a brand-new item by link before its category is known, and it is completely non-functional for every URL, not a narrow or content-specific edge case; it fails before ever reaching the network, so it isn't explainable as a `robots_disallowed`/`access_required`-style expected safe-failure the way ticket-platform URLs are for Activity | **Implemented** (`version/V6`, target `0.6.6.3`), awaiting Product Owner QA retest confirmation — not recorded `Fixed`. `analyzeSource` (`src/smart-import-client.js`) now accepts a `url` parameter and forwards it into `runSource`, mirroring `analyzeHotelSource`/`analyzeActivitySource` exactly; the one call site that used it (`runSmartAnalysis`, `src/v5-app.js`) now passes `url:source.url`. Server-side confirmed unchanged, not assumed: `analyze_source` already calls `prepareSource(body.source,true)` with `allowUrl` hardcoded to `true`, so the gap was entirely client-side as the root-cause analysis predicted. Additionally extracted the exact url-vs-file decision (`resolveSmartImportSubmissionMode`) into `src/operational-data.js`, dependency-free, specifically so it can be executed directly by a regression test — `smart-import-client.js` itself cannot be imported at all under this project's plain-Node test harness (confirmed directly: it transitively imports `firebase.js`, which touches `import.meta.env` and throws immediately outside a Vite build), which is exactly why the one prior reference to `analyzeSource` (`tests/v6-5-flight-import.mjs:404`) could only ever source-inspect the `v5-app.js` call-site text, never truly exercise this decision — the actual coverage gap that let the bug ship unnoticed. Regression tests (new file `tests/v6-69-correction.mjs`): `test_V6_F69_resolve_submission_mode_prefers_url_when_present_and_allowed`, `test_V6_F69_resolve_submission_mode_rejects_disallowed_url`, `test_V6_F69_resolve_submission_mode_throws_the_original_bug_message_with_neither_url_nor_file`, `test_V6_F69_resolve_submission_mode_falls_back_to_file_when_no_url` (all four execute the real decision logic directly), `test_V6_F69_analyze_source_accepts_and_forwards_url` (source-inspection of the actual fix wiring), `test_V6_F69_server_analyze_source_already_allows_url_unconditionally` (confirms, rather than assumes, the server-side claim above). The stale `tests/v6-5-flight-import.mjs:404` assertion was updated to match the new call-site signature. Full quality gate passes: `node --check` syntax, 128/128 test assertions across 20 files (up from 122), production build (`0.6.6.3-77ef08cc`). Also fixed in passing, unrelated process gaps left by the prior session: `package.json`'s version was still `0.6.6.2` (bumped to `0.6.6.3`, `package-lock.json` regenerated to match), and `VERSION_PACKAGE_AUTHORING_STANDARD_V30.md`'s filename had never been renamed to match its own internal `Document revision: V31` (renamed to `_V31.md`, per this project's own controlled-document rule). Committed to `version/V6`, deployed to `qa` (`0.6.6.3-3a5cf6cc`). **Fixed, verified** (`V73`) — Eyal's retest confirms the Rivhit link now reaches the server and returns a real content-type-specific rejection instead of the old instant client-side alert, and two genuinely HTML-serving URLs (`nofzuqim.co.il`, `secure.ezgo.co.il`) completed the full analysis-to-saved-item path. A distinct issue surfaced on the same retest, involving a different URL entirely (a ticket-platform link) — registered separately as `V6-F70`, not part of this finding's scope. |
| `V6-F70` — a real ticket-platform event URL (`tickets.leaan.net/ticket/...`) fails with `unsupported_source` ("not an HTML page") instead of the `robots_disallowed` safe-failure this exact platform was already expected to produce | `0.6.6.3` QA retest, `V6-F69` verification (Eyal's actual target use case: real event/ticket links, not general hotel/booking URLs) | Independent verbatim fetch of `tickets.leaan.net/robots.txt` (confirms `Disallow: /ticket/`, matching the URL's path exactly); manual trace of `isRobotsDisallowed`'s parsing logic (`netlify/functions/smart-import.mjs`) against this exact robots.txt content and pathname (correctly returns `true` on paper); no Netlify function log access from this environment, so the live server-side response could not be directly inspected | Observed: submitting the ticket URL via global Add → link → Analyze and propose produced the same alert as an unsupported file type ("The URL did not return an HTML page" / "המקור המקורי נשמר ולא נוצר פריט"). Expected, per the frozen `ATTRACTION_EVENT_SMART_IMPORT_DISCOVERY.md` `V4` decision (based on 4/4 ticket-platform URLs, `leaan.net` named specifically, tested `robots.txt`-blocked before implementation): this class of URL should safe-fail with the distinct `robots_disallowed` message ("האתר חוסם גישה אוטומטית לדף זה (robots.txt)..."), not the generic `unsupported_source` one — the two failure reasons exist specifically so this exact scenario is distinguishable from a genuinely wrong content type. Root cause not yet confirmed — the client-side half of the analysis is solid (this URL's robots.txt genuinely disallows `/ticket/`, and the matching code, traced by hand, correctly returns true for it), which means `assertRobotsAllowed`'s own request for `/robots.txt` most likely did not receive the same content this investigation did when made from Netlify's function runtime — most likely explanation, not confirmed: ticket-resale platforms commonly run bot-protection that treats requests from cloud/datacenter IP ranges (Netlify Functions runs on AWS Lambda) differently than other traffic, which could cause the robots.txt fetch itself to receive a non-200/blocked response there specifically, silently defaulting to "allow" via `assertRobotsAllowed`'s own `catch{return}`/`if(!robotsText)return` fallback, while the main page fetch immediately afterward receives whatever non-HTML response actually triggered `unsupported_source`. Two other real URLs tested in the same session (`nofzuqim.co.il`, `secure.ezgo.co.il`) completed successfully, so this is not a regression in `V6-F69`'s own fix — it is specific to how this one class of heavily bot-protected site responds. | Medium — not a regression and not blocking `V6-F69`'s own closure, but it means the Attraction/Event discovery doc's own explicitly-accepted safe-failure design (`robots_disallowed` as a distinguishable, documented outcome) is not actually reaching the user for the exact real-world case it was designed for; a user has no way to tell "this site blocks bots" from "this link points to the wrong kind of file" | **Investigated — root cause supported by evidence but not fully provable; observability fix implemented, no target version assigned.** Pulled real Netlify function logs directly (`netlify logs --source functions --function smart-import --url https://qa--familytripz.netlify.app --since 24h`), unlike Eyal's own investigation, which had no such access. The logs for the QA retest window show exactly one `robots_disallowed` error (16:11:58) and exactly one `unsupported_source` error (16:22:48), interleaved with several successful long-duration invocations (13-15s, consistent with a real extraction call) — but **the log format itself has no per-request URL/host field at all**, only a generic `Duration`/error-code summary, so neither error line could be definitively attributed to `tickets.leaan.net` specifically versus any other URL tested in the same session (a Ticketmaster/hapoelbc-style URL, or the `V6-F69` Rivhit PDF retest, which would independently and correctly also produce `unsupported_source` for an unrelated reason — a PDF is not `text/html`). This lack of correlation is itself the reason the investigation could not be taken further with certainty from log evidence alone. Independently fetched `tickets.leaan.net/robots.txt` and the exact reported ticket URL directly from this environment (a third vantage point, distinct from both Eyal's and Netlify's): both responded completely normally — `robots.txt` returned HTTP 200, `Content-Type: text/plain`, body containing `Disallow: /ticket/` under `User-Agent: *` exactly as Eyal found; the ticket URL itself returned HTTP 200, `Content-Type: text/html`, a genuine ticket page (no redirect to `vivenu.com` occurred, unlike what the discovery doc's own research speculated might be architecturally likely). **New, material evidence: both responses carry `Server: cloudflare`/`CF-RAY`/`cf-cache-status` headers, confirming this site runs behind Cloudflare** — independently corroborating the Product Owner's own leading hypothesis (a bot-protection layer that treats Netlify's AWS Lambda IP range and this function's non-browser User-Agent differently from a normal request), though the exact response Netlify's own runtime received could not be directly observed (the logging gap above is precisely why). Given a genuinely normal response from every other vantage point tested, and given the observed failure code (`unsupported_source`, meaning the response was HTTP-ok but non-HTML) is consistent with a differentiated Cloudflare block/challenge response served with 200 status and a non-`text/html` content-type specifically for bot-fingerprinted traffic, this remains the best-supported explanation but is flagged as not fully provable rather than confirmed. **Fix implemented (observability only, no behavior change):** added one bounded `console.info` log line at each of the three outcomes inside `assertRobotsAllowed` (fetch error; non-ok/empty robots.txt; a successfully parsed robots.txt, including its disallowed verdict and a 200-character body snippet) and one at the main page fetch in `fetchPublicPage` (host, HTTP status, content-type) — `netlify/functions/smart-import.mjs`. This closes the actual gap that blocked this investigation: the next time this happens, the function logs alone will show which host was involved and exactly what response it received, without needing an out-of-band manual fetch from a third vantage point. The `assertRobotsAllowed` fail-open default (treat an unreachable/empty robots.txt as "allowed") is explicitly UNCHANGED — whether it should fail closed instead (safer against exactly this silent-bypass shape, but riskier for a site whose robots.txt is merely slow or briefly unreachable) is a real design trade-off flagged back to the Product Owner, not decided unilaterally. Regression tests (new file `tests/v6-70-correction.mjs`): `test_V6_F70_public_page_fetch_logs_host_status_and_content_type`, `test_V6_F70_robots_check_logs_outcome_for_every_case`, `test_V6_F70_fail_open_behavior_is_unchanged` (the last one explicitly guards against a future change silently altering the fail-open policy as a side effect of a logging change). Full quality gate passes: `node --check` syntax, 131/131 test assertions across 22 files (up from 128), production build. **Product Owner decision (recorded):** keep the fail-open default, but make it honest — a robots.txt that can't be verified must never silently block a legitimate import, but if the subsequent page fetch also fails, the user must be told the real reason (couldn't confirm this site allows automated access), not the generic content-type message. **Implemented** (`version/V6`): `assertRobotsAllowed` (`netlify/functions/smart-import.mjs`) now returns a distinct outcome per host/step — `'verified'` (robots.txt fetched, parsed, did not disallow), `'unverifiable'` (fetch error, non-ok, or empty body), or throws `robots_disallowed` exactly as before for a confirmed block; the fail-open behavior itself (fetching is always attempted regardless of `'verified'`/`'unverifiable'`) is unchanged. `fetchPublicPage` now threads this outcome through: when the main page fetch subsequently fails (`!response.ok`) or returns non-HTML content, and the preceding robots.txt check was `'unverifiable'`, the new `robots_unverifiable` code and a distinct Hebrew message (`ROBOTS_UNVERIFIABLE_MESSAGE`, shared by both throw sites) are surfaced instead of the generic `protected_or_private_url`/`unsupported_source` ones — a verified-allowed site's later genuine failure (e.g. a real PDF-serving URL) still gets the original, unchanged messages, so a genuinely-wrong-content-type case is never misreported as a robots-verification problem it never had. **Mechanism deliberately deviates from the literal suggestion to thread this through `activityImportSchema`'s `acquisitionState` enum:** that enum describes what the MODEL itself reports about content it successfully read, a different layer from the HTTP-level error codes (`robots_disallowed`/`unsupported_source`/etc.) that `assertRobotsAllowed`/`fetchPublicPage` actually throw before the model is ever invoked — the new `robots_unverifiable` code is added alongside those existing codes instead, the correct layer for this mechanism. **Real, separate discovery made in the course of this fix, flagged rather than silently fixed:** the client's `errorMessage()` Hebrew lookup dictionary (`src/smart-import-client.js`) has been effectively dead code for every existing entry, including the already-present `robots_disallowed` one — `request()` always prefers the server's own `payload.message` verbatim, and every `httpError` call site in `smart-import.mjs` supplies one (in English), so the Hebrew dictionary is only ever reached if the response body fails to parse as JSON entirely. The new `robots_unverifiable` message is therefore set directly as the server's own `httpError` message text (in Hebrew, unlike every other existing message in that file) so it actually reaches the user; a `robots_unverifiable` entry was also added to the client dictionary as the explicitly-asked-for secondary defense. The pre-existing dead-code issue for `robots_disallowed` and the other entries was NOT fixed, since that is broader than what was asked — worth its own decision later. Regression tests (`tests/v6-70-correction.mjs`, extended): `test_V6_F70_verified_allowed_keeps_original_generic_messages_on_later_failure`, `test_V6_F70_robots_disallowed_still_thrown_unchanged`, `test_V6_F70_unverifiable_then_failed_surfaces_honest_message`, `test_V6_F70_client_error_message_dict_has_robots_unverifiable_entry` (plus the two pre-existing observability tests updated for the new `robotsCheck` return value/log field). Full quality gate passes: `node --check` syntax, 135/135 test assertions across 22 files (up from 131), production build. Committed to `version/V6`, not deployed. **Target assigned: `0.6.6.3`** (Product Owner decision) — same package as `V6-F69`/`V6-F71`. Deviation from the literal suggestion (threading through `activityImportSchema`'s `acquisitionState` enum) confirmed approved: that enum belongs to a later layer (what the model reports about content it successfully read), and the HTTP-level throw codes this fix actually uses are the correct place for it. **Fixed, verified** (`V79`) — Product Owner retest against QA build `0.6.6.3-8f6c1b95` confirms a robots.txt-blocked import (tested against the `leaan.net` ticket URL) now shows the correct, specific Hebrew message, not the generic `unsupported_source` one. |
| `V6-F71` — every Smart Import failure message a user has ever seen has actually been in English, not the Hebrew the client's `errorMessage()` dictionary implies is shown — including `robots_disallowed`, cited in `V6-F70`'s own investigation as "already working" based on the dictionary entry's mere existence | Discovered while implementing `V6-F70`'s `robots_unverifiable` fix; confirmed by the Product Owner's own review of that report | Direct code trace of `src/smart-import-client.js`'s `request()`: `if(!response.ok)throw new Error(payload.message\|\|errorMessage(payload.error,response.status));` — `payload.message` is `safeMessage(error)` server-side, which for any 4xx/5xx error returns `String(error.message)` verbatim (`netlify/functions/_shared/smart-import-auth.mjs`'s `httpError(status,code,message){const error=new Error(message);...}` sets `error.message` to exactly the English string every call site supplies); since every `httpError` call site in `netlify/functions/` supplies a non-empty message, `payload.message` is always truthy, so `errorMessage()`'s Hebrew dictionary is never reached in normal operation — only if `response.json()` fails to parse the body at all does `payload={}` and the dictionary get consulted. Audited every `httpError(...)` call site across `netlify/functions/*.mjs` and `_shared/*.mjs` (12 distinct codes) against the dictionary's 7 (now 8, post-`V6-F70`) entries. | Observed: the exact reported `V6-F70` alert text was English ("The URL did not return an HTML page") plus a Hebrew suffix `v5-app.js` appends on catch — never the fully-Hebrew `robots_disallowed` message the dictionary defines, which was assumed (not verified) to be what "already worked." Expected: every error code with a Hebrew dictionary entry should actually display that Hebrew text to the user, not the server's raw English message. Root cause: a backwards precedence in `request()` — the server's own message is checked first (`payload.message||...`), so it always wins whenever present, making the dictionary an unreachable fallback for every entry it has, not just the new one `V6-F70` added. | High — this is not a cosmetic wording issue; it means a project-wide, Hebrew-first user-facing guarantee has been silently false for every Smart Import error since the dictionary was first introduced, discovered only because `V6-F70`'s own new fix happened to need the dictionary path to actually work | **Fixed** (`version/V6`, target `0.6.6.3`, same package as `V6-F69`/`V6-F70`). `request()`'s precedence reversed: `resolveSmartImportErrorMessage(payload.error,response.status,payload.message)` now checks the dictionary FIRST by code, falling back to the server's own raw message only for a code with no entry (audited: `invalid_trip`/`invalid_operation`/`invalid_url`/`authentication_required`/`source_too_large` have none, correctly still show the server's English message, unaffected — no translation was asked for these lower-severity/rare paths). The dictionary itself (`SMART_IMPORT_ERROR_MESSAGES`) and its resolver were extracted from `src/smart-import-client.js` into the dependency-free `src/operational-data.js` (mirroring the `V6-F69` `resolveSmartImportSubmissionMode` pattern) specifically so a regression test could genuinely EXECUTE it and assert on the displayed text — `smart-import-client.js` itself cannot be imported at all under this project's plain-Node test harness (transitively imports `firebase.js`, which touches `import.meta.env` and throws outside a Vite build). `V6-F70`'s server-side `ROBOTS_UNVERIFIABLE_MESSAGE` workaround (Hebrew text set directly as the `httpError` message, to route around the then-broken dictionary) was reverted to a normal English message matching every other `httpError` call in that file, since the dictionary path is now genuinely reached. Regression tests (new file `tests/v6-71-correction.mjs`): `test_V6_F71_robots_disallowed_displays_hebrew_not_the_english_server_message`, `test_V6_F71_robots_unverifiable_displays_hebrew_not_the_english_server_message`, `test_V6_F71_unsupported_source_displays_hebrew_not_the_english_server_message` (all three assert on the actual resolved/displayed text, not merely that a dictionary key exists — exactly the gap that let this go unnoticed the first time), `test_V6_F71_a_code_with_no_dictionary_entry_falls_back_to_the_servers_own_message`, `test_V6_F71_neither_dictionary_nor_server_message_falls_back_to_generic_hebrew`, `test_V6_F71_request_uses_the_fixed_precedence_function` (source-inspection of the call-site wiring, since `smart-import-client.js` can't be executed). Two pre-existing `V6-F70` tests updated where the moved dictionary/reverted-to-English constant made their old assertions stale. Full quality gate passes: `node --check` syntax, 141/141 test assertions across 23 files (up from 135), clean production build. Committed to `version/V6`, not deployed. **Fixed, verified** (`V79`) — Product Owner retest against QA build `0.6.6.3-8f6c1b95` confirms Smart Import error messages, this one included, now actually render in Hebrew, not English. |
| `V6-F72` — `FL-003` flight direction extracted backwards on both legs (confirmed by the Product Owner from firsthand trip knowledge), the identical RTL-misread failure mode as `FL-001`, with no review flag raised either time | `0.6.6.3` QA, Product Owner confirmation session | Manual observation/confirmation (Eyal); `FLIGHT_SMART_IMPORT_DISCOVERY.md` `V2`'s own prior `FL-001` correction and its explicitly-flagged-as-pending `FL-003` risk | Observed: leg 1 (LY87, first itinerary card) extracted as HKT→TLV, actually TLV→HKT; leg 2 (LY84, second card) extracted as TLV→BKK, actually BKK→TLV — both directions exactly backwards, both confidently presented with no `directionAmbiguous`/needs-review flag at all. Expected, per the frozen source-relative-completeness contract: a source this misread should surface as needs-review, not a confident wrong answer — exactly the risk `V2` named as pending for this case after `FL-001`'s own RTL-misread was confirmed and corrected there. `FL-001`'s `V2` fix only ever corrected the benchmark document's own recorded ground truth; nothing was root-caused or changed in the actual extraction behavior, so the identical misread reproduced here fully undetected. Root cause: a live-model visual-parsing ceiling on right-to-left combined/summary itinerary layouts — `flightSystemPrompt` already contained an explicit, detailed warning against exactly this failure mode (added for `V6-F32`/`V6-F34`) and it still recurred, meaning wording alone is not sufficient, the same ceiling already documented for `V6-F53` (direction non-determinism) and `V6-F65` (fabrication). Confirmed this is not something the existing `V6-F53` dual-call reconciliation mechanism (`reconcileFlightSegmentDirections`) can catch either: that mechanism flags `directionAmbiguous` only when two independent model calls DISAGREE with each other — a systematic misread has both calls read the layout the same wrong way and agree, which is exactly why `FL-003`'s own two calls never disagreed and the picker never triggered for this. While investigating, also identified that `FL-002` (same LY087 flight number, catalogued `HKT→TLV`) was never independently Product-Owner-confirmed the way `FL-001` was, and `FL-003`'s own confirmation of LY87 as `TLV→HKT` makes `FL-002`'s catalogued direction for the same flight number suspect by the identical signature — corrected in the discovery document, flagged as inferred pending Eyal's own direct confirmation for that case specifically. | High — confirmed data-accuracy defect on live-model-extracted flight direction, the second live occurrence of the identical failure mode with zero review-flag protection either time, directly contradicting the frozen source-relative-completeness contract's needs-review guarantee | **Fixed** (`version/V6`, target `0.6.6.3`). Two-part fix, since prompt wording alone already failed once for this exact class: (1) `flightSystemPrompt` (`netlify/functions/_shared/smart-import-schema.mjs`) gains a new paragraph with a concrete, positive worked example of reading direction from a combined RTL two-leg itinerary view (the exact `FL-003` shape: two flight-number-headed row groups, each read independently by its own icon/label direction, explicitly warning against letting one leg's already-determined direction set a default/expectation for reading the other). (2) A new, fully deterministic, code-level safety net independent of live-model behavior: `flagReversedFlightDirection(suggestion,items)` (`src/ingestion.js`) compares a newly extracted flight segment's departure/arrival airports against any already-saved item sharing the same (normalized) flight number, and flags needs-review whenever they are the EXACT REVERSE — the precise, unambiguous signature of this failure mode, regardless of whether repeated live-model calls happen to agree with each other (closing the specific gap `V6-F53`'s own agreement-based mechanism cannot address). Wired into `runSmartAnalysis` (`src/v5-app.js`) for every flight-category suggestion, compared against the live `state.items`. Explicitly does not help a flight number's very first extraction (nothing yet to compare against) — the prompt-level fix remains necessary for that case. `FLIGHT_SMART_IMPORT_DISCOVERY.md` bumped to `V3`: `FL-003`'s row corrected to the confirmed directions (LY87 TLV→HKT, LY84 BKK→TLV); `FL-002`'s row corrected to the inferred TLV→HKT direction (departure TLV 22:20 on 7 Jan 2027, arrival HKT 14:30 on 8 Jan 2027), explicitly flagged as inferred from `FL-003`'s confirmation and not yet independently confirmed by Eyal for `FL-002` itself. Regression tests (new file `tests/v6-72-correction.mjs`, 8 tests): both `FL-003` legs' correct-vs-reversed directions, `FL-002`'s corrected direction under the same signature, a correctly-matching reading never flagged, a flight number with no prior saved item never flagged (documents the known first-extraction limitation), a non-flight suggestion left untouched, source-inspection confirming the `runSmartAnalysis` wiring, and source-inspection confirming the new worked-example paragraph exists in the prompt. The test file's own comments explain why `V6-F53`'s existing coverage (`tests/v6-52-53-correction.mjs`) could not have caught this recurrence: its own test explicitly constructs a DISAGREEMENT between two calls to prove that mechanism, whereas a systematic misread produces AGREEMENT between calls — a case that mechanism was never designed to catch. Full quality gate passes: `node --check` syntax, 154/154 test assertions across 25 files (up from 141), clean production build. Committed to `version/V6`, not deployed. **`FL-002` update:** the Product Owner has now independently confirmed `FL-002` (LY087) directly — TLV→HKT, exactly matching the inferred correction — no longer pending; `FLIGHT_SMART_IMPORT_DISCOVERY.md` bumped to `V4` to record this as confirmed ground truth on the same footing as `FL-001`/`FL-003`, no code change involved. **Fixed, verified** (`V79`) — Product Owner retest against QA build `0.6.6.3-8f6c1b95` confirms a fresh `FL-003` re-import now produces the correct directions on both legs (LY87 TLV→HKT, LY84 BKK→TLV). |
| `V6-F73` — Panvaree Resort: a date-conflict resolution the Product Owner explicitly selected and saved silently reverts, and the same conflict-resolution prompt reappears on the item's detail/view page — confirmed data loss | `0.6.6.3` QA, Product Owner repro session (Panvaree Resort item, PNG source already attached, second PDF source attached with conflicting dates) | Manual observation/repro (Eyal); direct code trace of `approveSuggestion`'s `mode==='merge'` branch (`src/v5-app.js`) | Observed: attaching the second, date-conflicting PDF source correctly surfaced the merge-conflict picker; the Product Owner selected the correct values and saved; the edit page reported success, but navigating to the item's detail/view page showed the OLD, unresolved conflicting values again, with the same conflict-resolution prompt reappearing. Expected: once a conflict resolution is selected and saved, that is the item's real persisted state — the detail page must reflect the chosen values, and a resolved conflict must never reappear. Root cause, confirmed by direct code trace, not inference: `preserveTrustedFieldsOnMerge` (`src/smart-import-adapter.js`) is correctly called ONCE, at suggestion-creation time (`runSmartAnalysis`, when the second source is attached and extracted), which is what surfaces the conflict as a reviewable `mergeCandidates` entry in the first place. `approveSuggestion`'s `mode==='merge'` branch then called it AGAIN, unconditionally, at approval time — before the target item is assigned the suggestion's values (`Object.assign` happens two lines later). At that moment the suggestion's value is whatever the Product Owner just resolved it to, but the target item's own field is still its old, untouched value, so `preserveTrustedFieldsOnMerge` reads this as a BRAND NEW conflict: it silently reverts the suggestion's resolved value back to the item's old one and recreates a fresh `mergeCandidates` entry — on the very item being saved. This exactly explains both symptoms: the detail page (reading the persisted item) shows the old value, and the same prompt reappears, because the item itself now carries a fresh, unresolved conflict. This shares its root-cause SHAPE with `V6-F66`'s array-merge data loss and the standing rule (`V6-F55`) that a field's needs-review/conflict state must reflect actual persisted state, not be silently re-derived and shown stale — though the specific mechanism here (a redundant second protective call, not a merge-shape defect) is distinct from both. | High — confirmed data loss: a Product Owner's deliberate, explicitly-saved conflict-resolution choice is silently discarded, and the item is left in a state that actively re-prompts for a decision already made, exactly as instructed to register this at | **Fixed** (`version/V6`, target `0.6.6.3`). `approveSuggestion`'s `mode==='merge'` branch (`src/v5-app.js`) now only calls `preserveTrustedFieldsOnMerge` a second time when the suggestion has NOT already been through it once: `suggestion.targetItemId?suggestion:preserveTrustedFieldsOnMerge(suggestion,target)`. A suggestion carrying `targetItemId` (the attach-and-extract path — exactly the Panvaree scenario) was already protected once at creation and is used as-is, so an already-resolved conflict survives unchanged into the item. A suggestion with no `targetItemId` (the general duplicate-merge-dialog path, whose target is chosen only at approval time) has never been protected before and still gets it applied for the first time here, unaffected by this fix. Regression tests (new file `tests/v6-73-correction.mjs`, 5 tests, using the already-exported `preserveTrustedFieldsOnMerge`/`resolveMergeCandidate`/`suggestionToItem` directly since `v5-app.js` itself cannot be imported under this project's plain-Node harness): reproduces the exact two-source `startAt`/`endAt` conflict and confirms it is correctly surfaced at attach time; directly exercises the OLD double-invocation logic and confirms it reproduces exactly the reported bug (silent revert + reappearing prompt); directly exercises the FIXED single-invocation logic and confirms the resolved values persist onto the item with `mergeCandidates` fully empty (no re-prompt); confirms the general duplicate-merge-dialog path (no `targetItemId`) is unaffected, still gets first-time protection; source-inspection confirming `v5-app.js`'s actual code uses the fixed conditional form and the old unconditional call is gone. One pre-existing stale source-inspection assertion in `tests/v6-5-flight-import.mjs` (`test_V6_F33_merge_preserves_existing_trusted_values_on_conflict`, which literally matched the old unconditional call text) updated to match the new conditional form — the underlying behavior it guards (the general duplicate-merge path still gets trusted-field protection) is unchanged, only the source text's shape changed. Full quality gate passes: `node --check` syntax, 154/154 test assertions across 25 files (up from 141), clean production build. Committed to `version/V6`, not deployed. **Fixed, verified** (`V79`) — Product Owner retest against QA build `0.6.6.3-8f6c1b95` confirms Panvaree Resort's two-source date-conflict resolution now saves and persists correctly: reloading the detail page shows the resolved values with no revert and no re-prompt. **One related, non-blocking gap surfaced during this retest, explicitly scoped out of this closure:** the ambiguous-date-labeling warning on the same item now correctly clears when the field is actively edited, but there is still no way to dismiss/confirm it as reviewed-and-correct without changing the value — the general dismiss mechanism `V6-F55` (extended) was meant to bring to this warning type still doesn't reach it. Product Owner decision: not a `0.6.6.4` patch item; registered as a new backlog finding, `V6-F74`, targeted `0.6.7` (see below). |
| `V6-F74` — Panvaree Resort's ambiguous-date-labeling warning has no way to be dismissed/confirmed as reviewed-and-correct without changing the underlying value | `0.6.6.3` QA, `V6-F73` retest (Panvaree Resort item, same session that confirmed `V6-F73`'s fix) | Manual observation during `V6-F73` retest (Product Owner) | Observed: the warning now correctly clears when the flagged date field is actively edited to a different value (progress since `V6-F55`'s original warning-lifecycle work) — but there is still no way to mark it as reviewed/confirmed-correct when the existing value is already right and no edit is needed; the only way to make the warning disappear is to change the value, which is not always the correct action. Expected: `V6-F55` (extended)'s general dismiss mechanism — an explicit keep/accept-style decision point, or a plain dismiss for a non-field-backed warning — was meant to reach every warning type on a saved item; this ambiguous-date-labeling warning is a type it still does not reach. | Low/Medium — a review-workflow friction and warning-lifecycle completeness gap, not data loss; explicitly not urgent enough to justify its own `0.6.6.4` patch | **Fixed, verified** (`version/V6`). Root cause, confirmed by direct code inspection of `isDismissibleWarning`/`reconcileStaleNeedsReview` (`src/ingestion.js`): a `needsReviewFields`-sourced warning (the shape this exact warning is — see `smart-import-schema.mjs`'s Hotel prompt, which explicitly covers "check-in/check-out dates printed inconsistently... report your best-supported reading... with certainty `needs_review`") has exactly ONE clearing mechanism, `reconcileStaleNeedsReview`, which fires only when the mapped field's VALUE actually changes. Unlike `mergeCandidates` (which `V6-F55` gave an explicit keep/accept picker via `resolveMergeCandidate`), `needsReviewFields` never received an equivalent "confirm the current value, no change needed" action — `isDismissibleWarning` correctly withholds a plain dismiss for a live `needsReviewRef` (hiding a genuinely unresolved reading would be worse), but nothing was ever put in its place. This is a real, general gap in `V6-F55`'s own mechanism, not specific to dates — the date-ambiguity case is simply the one that surfaced it, since a model's own best-supported guess on an ambiguous reading is often already correct. Fixed: new `confirmNeedsReviewField(target,key,value)` (`src/ingestion.js`), mirroring `resolveMergeCandidate`'s `'keep'` choice — removes the matching `needsReviewFields` entry (exact key+value match, the same match `isDismissibleWarning` itself already uses to decide "still live") and its warning, without touching the field's actual value; a no-op for a stale/non-matching key+value, and leaves any other, unrelated `needsReviewFields` entry on the same item untouched. Wired into `src/v5-app.js`: `warningRow` now renders a "אישור כנכון" (confirm as correct) button precisely when `isDismissibleWarning` withholds a plain dismiss (i.e. exactly the live-`needsReviewRef` case), and a new `confirm-needs-review` click action calls `confirmNeedsReviewField`, mirroring `resolve-merge-candidate`'s existing scope/id convention (works identically on a pending suggestion's `proposed` object or an already-saved item). Regression tests (new file `tests/v6-74-correction.mjs`, 8 tests): confirms a live-`needsReviewRef` warning correctly has no plain-dismiss path (documents the gap, not a regression); confirming removes both the `needsReviewFields` entry and its warning; confirming never touches the field's actual value; a safe no-op for a non-matching key or value; confirming one entry leaves a different, unrelated one on the same item untouched; works identically on a suggestion's `proposed` object; source-inspection confirming the `warningRow`/click-handler wiring for both scopes. One pre-existing stale source-inspection assertion in `tests/v6-54-correction.mjs` (which matched `warningRow`'s exact old function body) updated to a narrower regex matching only the dismissible-branch fragment it actually cares about, so it survives this and future structural changes to the non-dismissible branch. Full quality gate passes: `node --check` syntax, 162/162 test assertions across 26 files (up from 154), clean production build. Committed to `version/V6`. **QA retest confirmed 2026-09-10** by the Product Owner directly on Production (build `0.6.7.4-4fe16e5c`), reproducing the original Panvaree Resort item: the property-address warning showed "אישור כנכון" with no plain dismiss, distinctly from an adjacent ordinary dismissible warning on the same item (confirming the fix's live-`needsReviewRef`-only scoping) — clicking it cleared the warning without changing the field's value. PASS. |
| `V6-F75` — `INS-001` provider resolves to "IDI Insurance Company Ltd." instead of the frozen "ביטוח ישיר," silently, with no `needs_review` flag | `0.6.7` acceptance session, Test 1 | `EXECUTABLE_TEST_SESSION_V1.md` Test 1, direct Product Owner observation against the deployed `qa` build | Observed: `provider` resolved to "IDI Insurance Company Ltd." — the legal underwriting entity — with the "ביטוח ישיר" customer-facing brand name dropped entirely and no `needs_review` flag, unlike the correctly-handled ambiguous cases elsewhere in the same session (Tests 3, 7, 8, all flagged honestly). `provider` is a Decision-4 no-omission blocker, so a silent wrong/incomplete value here is more severe than an honestly-flagged one. Expected: either the correct value, or an honest `needs_review` flag if genuinely uncertain — never a silent substitution. | Medium-High | **Fixed, confirmed by Product Owner retest** (`0.6.7.1` acceptance session Round 2, `docs/versions/V6/0.6.7/EXECUTABLE_TEST_SESSION_V2.md`, 2026-09-10).
