Root cause: this is the same underlying pattern as `V6-F81` below — a source names both a customer-facing brand and a distinct legal underwriting entity for the ONE same insurer, and `insuranceSystemPrompt` had no guidance at all for that specific shape, only for the DIFFERENT case of a genuinely separate selling agent/broker. Fixed with a new prompt paragraph instructing the model to report `provider` as the combination `"<brand name> / <legal entity name>"` when a source shows both together for one insurer, mirroring the discovery document's own catalogued format for exactly this pattern (`INS-003`'s "PassportCard / DavidShield Insurance Company Ltd."), and explicitly warning against silently dropping one name or flagging `needs_review` about one when the source shows both with no genuine ambiguity. This is prompt-only (live-model behavior, no adapter-level parsing of "which of two names is which" is possible or appropriate) — regression coverage is source-inspection of the prompt text (`test_V6_F75_V6_F81_insuranceSystemPrompt_instructs_combining_brand_and_legal_entity`, `tests/v6-7-insurance-import.mjs`) plus the `INS-001` fixture updated to the corrected combined-string reading, proving it flows through the adapter cleanly end-to-end. Cannot be verified against the live model from this environment (no API access) — the next acceptance session must confirm the actual live extraction now produces the combined form. |
| `V6-F76` — Insurer website extracted correctly but lands only in `notes`, not a structured field | `0.6.7` acceptance session, Tests 1, 2, 4 (recurring, three separate real documents) | `EXECUTABLE_TEST_SESSION_V1.md` Tests 1/2/4 | Observed: the website value (`www.555.co.il`, `www.harel-group.co.il`, `WWW.FNX.CO.IL`) was present and correct in the extracted notes text on three separate real documents, but never populated a structured field. Expected: a website value should land in the item's own structured field, the same as Hotel/Activity/Restaurant already do. | Low-Medium | **Fixed, confirmed by Product Owner retest** (`0.6.7.1` acceptance session Round 2, `docs/versions/V6/0.6.7/EXECUTABLE_TEST_SESSION_V2.md`, 2026-09-10). Root cause, confirmed by direct code trace (same single root cause as `V6-F77` below): `website` was never added to `TYPE_FIELDS.insurance`, and `src/insurance-import-adapter.js`'s `FIELD_TARGETS` never mapped it, so any extracted website value fell through to the generic `otherFields`→notes fallback with nowhere structured to land. Fixed: `website` added to `TYPE_FIELDS.insurance` (`src/ingestion.js`) and mapped in `FIELD_TARGETS` (`src/insurance-import-adapter.js`). Regression tests: `test_V6_F76_website_lands_in_structured_field_not_only_notes` plus updated `INS-001`/`INS-002`/`INS-004`/`INS-008` fixtures (`tests/v6-7-insurance-import.mjs`), each asserting `proposed.website` directly (`INS-008`'s claims URL now correctly resolves there too, since it's a website-shaped value). |
| `V6-F77` — Website/link field absent from the edit form at initial proposal review, appears only after save and reopen | `0.6.7` acceptance session, Test 2 | `EXECUTABLE_TEST_SESSION_V1.md` Test 2 | Observed: on first proposal review (before save), the edit form showed no קישור (link) field at all; after saving the item and reopening it for editing, the field appeared. Expected: the field should behave consistently whether shown before or after save. | Low-Medium | **Fixed, confirmed by Product Owner retest** (`0.6.7.1` acceptance session Round 2, `docs/versions/V6/0.6.7/EXECUTABLE_TEST_SESSION_V2.md`, 2026-09-10). Root cause, confirmed by direct code trace, the exact same single root cause as `V6-F76`: `proposalFields()` (`src/v5-app.js`) only renders a `website` field when `TYPE_FIELDS[type]` includes it OR `p.website` is already truthy — since insurance had neither (the adapter never populated `proposed.website`, per `V6-F76`), the field never appeared on the suggestion-review screen. `editModal()` alone calls `proposalFields(item,{alwaysWebsite:true})`, which unconditionally forces the field regardless of `TYPE_FIELDS`/`p.website` — the one path that made it appear, but only after save+reopen, exactly matching the observed symptom. `V6-F76`'s single fix (mapping `website` into `TYPE_FIELDS.insurance`) closes this too, since the field is now a genuine `TYPE_FIELDS` member rendered on the very first suggestion-review screen, no longer dependent on the `alwaysWebsite` special case. Regression test: `test_V6_F77_website_field_is_a_permanent_TYPE_FIELDS_member_not_only_alwaysWebsite` (`tests/v6-7-insurance-import.mjs`), confirming both that `editModal`'s `alwaysWebsite:true` still exists (unchanged, still needed for every other type) and that insurance no longer depends solely on it. |
| `V6-F78` — `.tif` upload rejected entirely at both entry points, blocking the mandatory wild-card test — **hard blocker** | `0.6.7` acceptance session, Test 6 (`INS-006`, the mandatory wild-card) | `EXECUTABLE_TEST_SESSION_V1.md` Test 6 | Observed: uploading `INS-006_TorenInsurance.tif` — via both global Add → Document and an existing Insurance item's Attach-and-extract — failed immediately with the client-side alert "יש לבחור קובץ PDF או תמונה" (must choose a PDF or image), even though the `.tif` file was genuinely selected. Confirmed systemic, not entry-point-specific. This directly contradicts Decision 7's confirmed scope ("PDF and image, including scanned/fax-quality images and screenshots, per `INS-006`/`INS-007`") and makes the mandatory wild-card test, required by the standard's own wild-card rule, impossible to evaluate at all. Expected: `.tif`/`.tiff` accepted the same as PNG/JPEG/WebP, per Decision 7's confirmed scope. | High/blocking | **Fixed at the upload layer, confirmed by Product Owner retest** (`0.6.7.1` acceptance session Round 2, `docs/versions/V6/0.6.7/EXECUTABLE_TEST_SESSION_V2.md`, 2026-09-10) — `.tif` now passes upload at both entry points with no client-side alert, exactly as this fix intended. **But Test 6 (the same mandatory wild-card) still FAILED that same retest**, one layer deeper: the extraction backend itself rejects the raw TIFF bytes (the vision API does not accept TIFF natively), a distinct, new finding (`V6-F85`, see its own register row below) that this fix's own residual-risk note below had already flagged as unverifiable from this environment. Root cause of the upload-layer fix itself, confirmed by direct code trace: a MIME/extension allowlist that never included TIFF, at three separate layers that all had to be fixed together: (1) the client-side `accept` attributes on both file inputs (global Add's `smartImportPanel`, the existing-item `attachmentModal`) — cosmetic (some browsers/OS allow selecting a file outside `accept` anyway) but still corrected; (2) the actual client-side `validateSmartSource` (`src/v5-app.js`) `isImage` regex, `/^image\/(?:png|jpeg|webp)$/` — this is the literal source of the reported alert; (3) the server-side `prepareSource` MIME check (`netlify/functions/smart-import.mjs`), which would have rejected the upload even if the client-side check were fixed alone. Also fixed in passing, the same root-cause class: `inferMime` (`src/smart-import-client.js`) had no TIFF branch at all and would have mislabeled a `.tif` file with an empty `file.type` (TIFF MIME-type detection is less universal across browsers/OS than PNG/JPEG) as `image/jpeg`, corrupting the actual bytes sent to the server/model even after the upload itself was no longer rejected. `validateSmartSource`'s `isImage` now also falls back to a `.tif`/`.tiff` filename check, mirroring `isPdf`'s own existing MIME-or-filename fallback. Regression tests (`tests/v6-7-insurance-import.mjs`): `test_V6_F78_client_validation_accepts_tif`, `test_V6_F78_file_inputs_accept_tif_for_both_entry_points` (both confirmed-broken entry points, individually), `test_V6_F78_server_side_prepareSource_accepts_tiff_mime`, `test_V6_F78_inferMime_recognizes_tiff_filenames`. **Residual risk, honestly flagged, not verifiable from this environment:** these fixes unblock the upload/pipeline; whether OpenAI's vision endpoint itself natively supports a raw TIFF byte stream in an `input_image` content part cannot be confirmed without a live API call — the next acceptance session must verify the actual `INS-006` extraction succeeds, not just that the upload is no longer rejected. |

Root cause: `insuranceSystemPrompt`'s `V6-F75`/`V6-F81` paragraph had no way to distinguish a genuine second entity name (company/organization identity, answering "which company") from a plan/product/coverage-tier/edition name (answering "which package under this one insurer," never "which company") — both are "a second name printed near provider," and the existing instruction to combine applied indiscriminately to either. | `V6-F85` — `.tif` upload now succeeds (per the `V6-F78` fix), but the extraction backend rejects the raw TIFF format one layer deeper — **hard blocker, second consecutive failure of the same mandatory wild-card test** | `0.6.7.1` acceptance session (Round 2), Test 6 (`INS-006`, the mandatory wild-card) | `EXECUTABLE_TEST_SESSION_V2.md` Test 6 | Observed: uploading `INS-006_ToreinInsurance.tif` — via both global Add → Document and an existing Insurance item's Attach-and-extract — now passes the upload step cleanly (the `V6-F78` fix works as intended), but fails one step later, during actual extraction, with `400 The image data you provided does not represent a valid image. Please check your input and try again with one of the supported image formats: ['image/jpeg', 'image/png', 'image/gif', 'image/webp']`. This directly contradicts Decision 7's confirmed scope and makes the mandatory wild-card test impossible to evaluate a second time, one layer deeper than before. Expected: a real completed extraction result for `INS-006` (even if some fields are `needs_review`, given this is a poor-quality 2010 scan), not merely an accepted upload. | High/blocking | **Fixed, confirmed by Product Owner retest** (`0.6.7.2` acceptance session Round 3, `docs/versions/V6/0.6.7/EXECUTABLE_TEST_SESSION_V3.md`, 2026-09-10) — `INS-006` now extracts and converts end-to-end (policy number, policyholder, ID, coverage dates, premium, coverage scope and health declaration all correctly extracted from the converted image), exactly as this fix intended. **But Test 6 (the same mandatory wild-card) still FAILED that same retest**, one layer deeper again: `provider` resolved to a completely wrong, unrelated company with no honesty flag, a distinct, new finding (`V6-F86`, see its own register row below) discovered underneath this fix, a third consecutive failure on the same mandatory test. Root cause of this fix itself, confirmed by direct investigation: the raw TIFF bytes were being passed straight through to the vision/extraction API in `prepareSource` (`netlify/functions/smart-import.mjs`), which does not accept TIFF natively — only jpeg/png/gif/webp, per its own error message; the `V6-F78` fix only ever addressed the upload-time allowlist, a necessary but not sufficient layer. Fixed by inserting a server-side TIFF→PNG conversion (`convertTiffToPng`, exported from `netlify/functions/smart-import.mjs`) into `prepareSource`, before the extraction call, for any `image/tiff` source. Uses pure-JS decode (`utif2`, confirmed to support CCITT Group 3/4 fax compression — the exact compression the real `INS-006` fixture uses) and pure-JS encode (`pngjs`) — deliberately not `sharp`, which ships a platform-specific native binary, a real risk inside an esbuild-bundled Netlify Function with no container image to pre-match the deploy target. **Verification, per this correction's own explicit bar** (a second attempt at the same mandatory test; `V6-F78`'s own narrower test passed while this failure mode still existed underneath it) **— a real completed conversion was verified against the actual fixture, not a mock and not merely the absence of an error:** `convertTiffToPng` run directly (both manually and via the committed regression test) against the real `tests/fixtures/insurance_benchmark/INS-006_TorenInsurance.tif` bytes decodes cleanly into a valid PNG matching the fixture's own real pixel dimensions (1654×2339, independently confirmed via direct TIFF-header inspection: little-endian, CCITT Group 4, bi-level, PhotometricInterpretation=WhiteIsZero), independently re-decoded with `pngjs` itself to confirm round-trip integrity, and visually inspected — the converted image is fully legible (policy number `220008858030`, policyholder Eyal Ben-Yitzhak, ID `043119213`, coverage `31/03/2010`–`03/04/2010` all readable), matching the discovery document's own catalogued ground truth. Regression coverage (`tests/v6-7-insurance-import.mjs`): `test_V6_F85_prepareSource_is_wired_to_convertTiffToPng` (confirms the wiring, source-inspection) and `test_V6_F85_tiff_conversion_produces_real_decodable_png_from_actual_fixture` (runs the real conversion against the real fixture end-to-end, asserting actual output dimensions, not a stubbed format check). **Honestly-flagged residual, not verifiable from this environment:** whether the OpenAI vision endpoint itself successfully extracts real field values from the converted PNG cannot be confirmed without a live API call — the next acceptance session must verify the actual `INS-006` extraction produces a real completed proposal, not just that no error is thrown at either the upload or conversion layer. |

But `provider` resolved to "Harel Insurance Company Ltd." — not a brand-vs-legal-entity nuance like `V6-F75`/`V6-F81`/`V6-F84`, but a completely different, unrelated company that appears nowhere in this document. The source's own header states "ביטוח נסיעות לחו"ל - כלל חברה לביטוח בע"מ" ("Travel insurance abroad - Clal Insurance Company Ltd."); Clal is the underwriting insurer, Toren (the letterhead's own agency name) is the selling agent. No `needs_review` flag was raised. Expected: the correct provider (Clal), or an honest `needs_review` flag if genuinely uncertain — never a confidently wrong, unrelated company. | High/blocking | **Fixed, verified** — confirmed by direct Product Owner retest against `qa` build `0.6.7.4-4fe16e5c` (`0.6.7.4` acceptance session Round 5, 2026-09-10), the live re-upload of the real `INS-006` fixture this finding's own closure condition required, and which this Development Agent could not perform itself (no `qa` access). This closes the disconnect the `0.6.7.4` correction package had explicitly left open. **History below, preserved for the audit trail:** `0.6.7.3` attempt reported `Fixed` but confirmed NOT actually fixed on live retest (`0.6.7.3` acceptance session Round 4, `docs/versions/V6/0.6.7/EXECUTABLE_TEST_SESSION_V4.md`, 2026-09-10) — `provider` still resolved to the exact same wrong "Harel Insurance Company Ltd." value, no `needs_review` flag, identically to before the `0.6.7.3` fix, a fourth consecutive failure of the same mandatory test. Investigated directly (not re-guessed): the `0.6.7.3` prompt paragraph is genuinely present, correctly wired, and was correctly deployed (commit/wiring/deploy all confirmed) — the actual gap was that `0.6.7.3`'s own regression test was pure source-inspection of the prompt string, never invoking the real model call, so it was never capable of catching "the model still doesn't comply." **`0.6.7.4` attempt (target `0.6.7.4`), status: Implemented, live verification required — NOT recorded `Fixed`, per this round's own strengthened closure bar.** Strengthened `insuranceSystemPrompt` further: `evidence` for `provider` must now be a literal, verbatim transcription of the printed company-name text (exact characters, script-preserved, not translated/paraphrased) — a concrete, harder-to-fabricate mechanism than the `0.6.7.3` paragraph's abstract caution (which remains, unchanged, immediately above this new paragraph). Regression coverage: `test_V6_F86_insuranceSystemPrompt_requires_literal_quoted_evidence_for_provider` (`tests/v6-7-insurance-import.mjs`), alongside the retained `0.6.7.3` test. **Cannot be verified against the live model from this environment — this Development Agent has no `qa` access (the app requires Google sign-in restricted to authorized users) and could not perform the live re-upload of `INS-006` this closure condition explicitly requires.** The next acceptance session (or any session with `qa` access) must actually re-upload the real `INS-006` fixture and confirm the returned provider value before this can be marked `Fixed` — a passing test suite is explicitly not sufficient evidence this round. **`0.6.7.3` attempt's own original root-cause record** (preserved for the audit trail; its "Fixed" claim is now known incorrect, per above): root-caused by direct investigation, not guessed, per the correction prompt's own three specific hypotheses: (1) cross-document leakage ruled out — `netlify/functions/smart-import.mjs`'s `callExtraction` builds a fresh `input` array per call (`store:false`, no `previous_response_id`, no shared context across sources); literal leakage from a different document (Harel is the provider for `INS-002`/`INS-005` elsewhere in this same benchmark) is not structurally possible in this stateless-per-request pipeline. (2) TIFF→PNG conversion quality ruled out — the actual converted PNG (from the `V6-F85` fix) was regenerated from the real fixture and visually inspected specifically at the header/title-bar region: the "כלל חברה לביטוח בע"מ" text is bold white-on-black, near-maximum contrast, unambiguously legible — not a plausible source of a misread. (3) Confirmed as a genuine model hallucination/pattern-completion failure, not a legibility or code-path bug — a plausible, real, commonly-seen insurer name (one the model has "seen" as correct elsewhere in this same review context) substituted for what this specific source actually, literally shows. That investigation's conclusion (hallucination, not a code bug) is still believed correct; what was wrong was believing the prompt-only fix had actually closed it without live verification. |
| `V6-F87` — `.tif` attachment downloads instead of previewing inline | `0.6.7.2` acceptance session (Round 3), Test 6 (`INS-006`, the mandatory wild-card) | `EXECUTABLE_TEST_SESSION_V3.md` Test 6 | Observed: opening the item's attached `.tif` file (from the item detail view, wherever attachments are normally previewed) downloads the file instead of displaying it inline, unlike every other supported file type (PDF, JPG, PNG). Expected: an inline preview, or at minimum an explained reason the file downloaded instead. | High | **Fixed, verified** — confirmed by direct Product Owner retest against `qa` build `0.6.7.4-4fe16e5c` (`0.6.7.4` acceptance session Round 5, 2026-09-10), the live re-open of the actual `.tif` attachment this finding's own closure condition asked for, closing the gap this Development Agent's own code-level verification (deployed-bundle inspection, `window.open()` focus-shift experiment) could not fully substitute for. **History below, preserved for the audit trail:** `0.6.7.3` attempt reported `Fixed` but confirmed NOT actually noticeable on live retest (`0.6.7.3` acceptance session Round 4, `docs/versions/V6/0.6.7/EXECUTABLE_TEST_SESSION_V4.md`, 2026-09-10) — the `.tif` attachment still downloaded silently with no explanatory message, identically to before the `0.6.7.3` fix, a fourth consecutive failure of the same mandatory test. Investigated directly: fetched the actual live `0.6.7.3` `qa` bundle's own JS chunk and confirmed the `0.6.7.3` fix code (the TIFF-detection logic and its `notify()` call) was genuinely present and correctly deployed — this was not a deployment or wiring bug. The likely real gap: `notify()`'s toast auto-dismisses after 2.6 seconds (`notify()`, `src/v5-app.js`), called AFTER `open(url,'_blank','noopener')` already shifts the browser's attention to a new tab and starts the (unavoidable, since no browser renders TIFF natively) download — a plausible, concrete explanation for the message technically firing but never actually being seen, distinct from `V6-F86`'s "the fix didn't reach the model at all" class of gap. **`0.6.7.4` attempt (target `0.6.7.4`), status: Implemented, live verification recommended but not required to close (this fix is deterministic, not LLM-dependent) — recorded `Fixed` only in the sense of "the code path is now structurally immune to the specific timing race identified," not re-confirmed live.** Replaced the after-the-fact `notify()` toast with a blocking `alert()` (this codebase's own established pattern for a message that must not be missed — see e.g. `validateSmartSource`'s own `alert(error.message)`), shown BEFORE `open()`/the download starts rather than after, removing the tab-focus race entirely rather than trying to out-time it. Regression test rewritten: `test_V6_F87_openSource_explains_tiff_download_instead_of_silent_inconsistency` (`tests/v6-7-insurance-import.mjs`) now asserts the `alert()` call AND that it occurs before `open()` in source order, not merely that a message string exists somewhere in the function (the `0.6.7.3` test's exact blind spot — it asserted the message existed, which was true, but never asserted it would actually be seen). **This Development Agent could not perform a live click-through of the actual app to confirm the alert dialog is genuinely seen in practice** — no `qa` access (Google sign-in restricted to authorized users) — so while the underlying `window.open()`-triggers-focus-shift mechanism was independently verified (a direct browser experiment confirmed `open()` on a non-renderable blob: URL does not return a usable window handle, consistent with immediate download/focus-shift behavior), the exact real-browser, real-app experience remains unconfirmed from this environment. |
| `V6-F82` — an item linked to two insurances only displays the first in its own reverse-lookup view | `0.6.7.4` acceptance session (Round 5) | Product-promotion handoff summary (full observed notes not separately transcribed to the Development Agent — see the Product Owner's own acceptance-session write-up, `claude/FAMILY_TRIPS_0_6_7_4_ACCEPTANCE_SESSION_RESULT_V1.md`, for the complete record) | Observed: when an item is linked from more than one Insurance item (`relatedItemIds`), its own detail-page reverse-lookup ("🛡️ מבוטח באמצעות...") only shows the first linked insurance, not all of them. Expected: every insurance item covering this item should be discoverable from its own detail page, not just the first one found. A related enhancement idea — an icon marking items that already carry linked insurance, for discoverability in list views — was raised in the same discussion and explicitly deferred to Beta, not this vertical. | Low | **Assigned — target `Alpha 0.7`** (`0.6.7.4` acceptance session Round 5, `V87`; target decision `V89`), explicitly not blocking `0.6.7.4`'s promotion, per the Product Owner's own framing (display bug, Low severity, real-world case is rare — most items are covered by at most one insurance policy). Not yet implemented, no regression test yet. Root cause not yet investigated by the Development Agent (out of scope for the `0.6.7.4` correction package, which was scoped to `V6-F86`–`V6-F87` only) — likely `insuranceCoveringItem` (`src/v5-app.js`) returning only the first match from `state.items` rather than every insurance item whose `relatedItemIds` includes this item's id, based on the function's name/singular framing, but this has not been directly confirmed by code inspection and should not be assumed correct without doing so first. **2026-09-10: Product Owner decision — Assigned, target Alpha 0.7** (folded into that phase's scope rather than a dedicated `0.6.7.5` patch or Beta 0.8; see the Alpha 0.7 section below). **2026-09-10 (`V91`): formally slotted to `0.7.2`**, alongside that candidate's Shared-Trip/multi-owner item-relationships work. |

| Finding | Observed in | Test/evidence source | Observed vs. expected behavior | Severity | Assignment status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `V6-F52` — sign-in screen shows a stale hardcoded version | `0.6.6` QA, build `0.6.6-3925bb20` | Manual observation, `0.6.6` QA acceptance session | Observed: the pre-authentication sign-in screen displays "Alpha 0.6.5" under the logo; the in-app About/landing banner correctly show `0.6.6-3925bb20 · Alpha 0.6.6` for the same build. Expected: every user-visible version label derives from the same build-injected source and can never drift independently — this is the exact class of defect `V6-F31` fixed for the landing banner, and (per Eyal) the same class as `V5-F05` (Alpha 0.5 era), which predates this project's mandatory-regression-test rule and almost certainly never received one. Root cause confirmed: `src/main.js`'s `renderLogin()` hardcoded `<p class="auth-kicker">Alpha 0.6.5</p>` as a plain literal — unlike `v5-app.js`'s `appVersionLabel()` (read `document.title`) and `buildVersionLabel()` (reads `<meta name="build-version">`), `main.js` never imported or called either helper; it was a fully disconnected hand-maintained string in a different file. **Implemented:** extracted `appVersionLabel()` into its own shared module, `src/app-version.js`, imported by both `src/main.js` and `src/v5-app.js` — the two screens now structurally cannot diverge again, since there is only one function that knows how to read the version. Regression test: `test_V6_F52_signin_version_matches_about` (`tests/v6-52-53-correction.mjs`), source-inspection style (asserts no hardcoded `Alpha 0\.\d` literal remains, and both files import the one shared source) — the same pattern this codebase already uses for DOM/Firebase-dependent UI code that can't run in the plain-Node test harness. Two pre-existing tests that had literally encoded the old hardcoded string as expected behavior were also corrected (`tests/v5-correction.mjs`, `tests/v6-5-flight-import.mjs`'s `V6-F31` test) — worth noting as its own small instance of the exact defect class this finding is about. **Eyal's retest (`V62`):** sign-in screen, browser tab title, and About panel all now consistently read "Alpha 0.6.6" (About panel additionally shows build hash `0.6.6-9b5b1ed0`). | Low (cosmetic, pre-authentication only) but a direct recurrence of a known defect class | **Fixed, verified** (`version/V6`) |
| `V6-F53` — FL-003 flight direction/segment order inconsistent across identical immediate re-imports, no disambiguation shown | `0.6.6` QA, build `0.6.6-3925bb20` | Manual observation during Test 13 (short regression) of `EXECUTABLE_TEST_SESSION_V184.md` | Observed: importing `FL-003_ELAL_itinerary_summary_both_legs.png` twice in immediate succession, file unchanged, produced the wrong segment direction/order on attempt 1 and the correct order on attempt 2; the `directionAmbiguous`/`directionCandidates` picker (built specifically for this class of case — `V6-F32`/`V6-F34`, commit `716f950`, 2026-08-27) was never shown on either attempt. Expected per the frozen `flightSystemPrompt`: either a stable correct read every time, or — when the source is genuinely undeterminable — `directionAmbiguous:true` with both candidates surfaced for explicit Product Owner selection; never a silent, possibly-wrong guess with no visible uncertainty. Root cause: confirmed via git history (commit `716f950`) that FL-003 was already identified at the time as exhibiting genuine run-to-run non-determinism in the raw model extraction (same unchanged source producing correct, confidently-wrong, and refused reads across separate attempts during the original investigation) — not a deterministic code defect, and explicitly not fixable by further prompt-tuning per that commit's own conclusion. The `directionAmbiguous` escape valve built in response is itself correct and unit-tested at the adapter level, but depends entirely on the model correctly self-diagnosing "this is genuinely ambiguous" on a given call — a decision with no determinism control at all (confirmed by direct code inspection: `netlify/functions/smart-import.mjs`'s `client.responses.create()` calls set only `reasoning:{effort:'low',context:'current_turn'}` — no `temperature`, no `seed`). Eyal's observation (two different confident guesses, escape valve never triggered) is consistent with this same previously-documented non-determinism resurfacing in the one place the fix could never make deterministic — not a regression in the picker/adapter mechanism itself, which is unchanged and still passes every existing test. Two referenced documents could not be located anywhere in the repository or the broader project directory tree and could not be used as evidence: `FLIGHT_SMART_IMPORT_DISCOVERY.md` (no file by that name exists anywhere), and `EXECUTABLE_TEST_SESSION_V169.md` (referenced repeatedly in this very document as the "full record" of `0.6.5`'s QA/Production acceptance, but the file itself was apparently never created) — whether FL-003's direction was explicitly reconfirmed before the `0.6.5` GO could not be determined from any available record. **Implemented (Product Owner-approved approach — detect via repetition):** for a multi-segment flight source, `runExtraction` (`netlify/functions/smart-import.mjs`) now makes a second independent model call and cross-checks the two readings per segment (matched by flight number); a disagreement synthesizes `directionAmbiguous:true` with both readings as `directionCandidates`, routing through the existing picker mechanism unchanged — no new UI. Scoped to multi-segment sources only (`draft.segments.length>1`, detected from the first call's own result, since segment count isn't knowable beforehand) — Hotel/Activity extraction and single-segment Flight sources are unaffected, matching the accepted 2x cost/latency tradeoff for this shape only. Regression tests: `tests/v6-52-53-correction.mjs`, testing the exported, pure `reconcileFlightSegmentDirections` function directly with hand-constructed "first call"/"second call" segment pairs (disagreement → picker; agreement → unchanged; already-self-flagged → left alone; no matching flight number → fails open) plus a source-inspection check that the second call is genuinely gated to multi-segment sources — no live model call is mocked or invoked, matching this suite's existing pattern of testing deterministic logic directly rather than the live extraction itself. **Eyal's retest (`V62`):** re-imported `FL-003` — the two internal extraction calls genuinely disagreed on direction this time, the `directionAmbiguous`/`directionCandidates` picker correctly appeared, and Eyal resolved it to the correct direction — a real exercise of the disagreement-detection mechanism, not a lucky single-call pass. | Medium — data-accuracy risk (a wrong-but-confident direction could be approved unnoticed, no warning shown), self-correcting on retry in this observed instance | **Fixed, verified** (`version/V6`) |

`V6-F52` and `V6-F53` both now have passing named regression tests (`tests/v6-52-53-correction.mjs`) and are implemented on `version/V6` — see each entry above for detail. Neither is recorded `Fixed`; both await Eyal's QA retest per the standard's responsibility split.

A third finding, `V6-F54`, was registered from the same QA acceptance session (Eyal saved the `H-001` benchmark case, Panvaree Resort, and separately attached a companion document to it) — investigated and root-caused, not yet fixed:

| Finding | Observed in | Test/evidence source | Observed vs. expected behavior | Severity | Assignment status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `V6-F54` — a warning on a saved item can never be cleared, by any means, once the item exists | `0.6.6` QA, build `0.6.6-3925bb20` | Manual observation, `0.6.6` QA acceptance session (`H-001`/Panvaree Resort Hotel item) | Observed: the saved, approved Panvaree Resort item still showed two needs-review date warnings ("Arrival date: 2027-01-17", "Departure date: 2027-01-18", both correctly matching `H-001`'s frozen dates — the values themselves are right) after saving, not just during pre-approval review. Separately, attaching a companion document to the same saved item produced further warnings (phone/address/location review flags, plus three "ערך חדש התגלה עבור X" merge-conflict notices for phone/`startAt`/`endAt`) that were equally impossible to clear. Expected: a warning whose underlying issue has been reviewed, accepted, or resolved should be dismissible or should clear, the same way it already can on the pre-approval suggestion review screen (`V6-F49`'s dismiss control for `unresolved`-sourced warnings; `reconcileStaleNeedsReview`'s edit-triggered clearing for `needsReviewFields`-sourced ones). Root cause, confirmed by direct code inspection, not by inference: `detailView()` (`src/v5-app.js`) renders `item.warnings` as plain, non-interactive text — `${item.warnings?.length?...map(warning=>\`<div>⚠️ ${esc(warningText(warning))}</div>\`)...}` — no index tracking, no `isDismissibleWarning` check, no dismiss button at all. The item-edit save handler (`form.id==='edit-item-form'` in the same file) does `Object.assign(state.items.find(...), values)` and nothing else — it never calls `reconcileStaleNeedsReview` or touches `item.warnings` in any way. A repository-wide search for every `.warnings=` assignment confirms this precisely: every one of them touches only `suggestion.warnings`; none ever touches an item. Once `suggestionToItem()` copies `warnings` onto a newly created or merged item, that array is fixed for the remaining lifetime of the item — this predates `V6-F49`/`V6-F50` entirely (it is not a regression they introduced) and applies uniformly to every warning shape (`needsReviewFields`-sourced, `unresolved`-sourced/dismissible, and the plain-string merge-conflict warnings from `preserveTrustedFieldsOnMerge`) and every item type (Hotel, Flight, Activity) — this single gap fully explains both symptoms Eyal observed, which is why they are registered as one finding rather than split. Two related, separately-investigated points, both concluded **not** to be defects: (1) no website was saved for Panvaree Resort — confirmed correct against `SMART_IMPORT_REDESIGN_DISCOVERY_V22.md`'s frozen `H-001` ground truth, which explicitly lists "official website" among the source's explicitly-absent fields; (2) the merge-conflict behavior itself (existing value kept, new value flagged, no one-click "accept new value" control) is the frozen contract working as designed ("Duplicate detection must preserve non-empty trusted values... flagged for review", confirmed `V6-F33`/`V6-F39`) — a one-click accept action would be a legitimate future UX enhancement, not a contract violation, so it is not registered as its own finding here. One more referenced document could not be located anywhere in the repository or project tree, consistent with the pattern already reported for `V6-F53`: `SMART_IMPORT_HOTEL_BENCHMARK_CASE_H001_V3.md` (cited by `SMART_IMPORT_REDESIGN_DISCOVERY_V22.md` as `H-001`'s ground-truth document) — no standalone document by that name survives even in the separate Project knowledge store; only the inline `H-001` summary already embedded in `SMART_IMPORT_REDESIGN_DISCOVERY_V22.md` itself exists, and it is sufficient — the existing `H-001` test fixtures/regression tests already match it, confirmed independently during this investigation. Flagged and accepted as a permanent gap, not chased further. **Implemented:** a dismiss control added to `detailView()` for `isDismissibleWarning`-shaped entries, mirroring `suggestionView()`'s own (new `dismiss-item-warning` action, scoped to `state.items`); the item-edit save handler now computes a `changedFields` set the same way `updateSuggestion` already does and calls `reconcileStaleNeedsReview` on the item before saving. Regression tests: `tests/v6-54-correction.mjs` — `reconcileStaleNeedsReview` proven to work identically on a real saved-item shape (not just a suggestion's `proposed` object), plus source-inspection confirming both the edit-handler wiring and the dismiss-control rendering/action. Two pre-existing tests updated where they encoded the old rendering exactly (`tests/v6-5-flight-import.mjs`'s `V6-F33` test). **Eyal's retest (`V62`) found the fix incomplete on both cases** (no dismiss control visible anywhere in the UI on either item; Panvaree Resort's warning is now additionally stale/misleading, describing a conflict that already resolved correctly; Panan Krabi retains one warning even after editing every associated field) — this is the same underlying problem `V6-F55` already opened. **`V6-F54` is merged into `V6-F55` as of `V62` rather than closed separately; see `V6-F55`'s entry below for the combined finding, elevated severity, and Product Owner's proposed fix direction.** | High — a warning is meant to be a temporary review prompt, not a permanent, unremovable fixture; as implemented it degrades the item detail page's trustworthiness over time as attachments/edits accumulate warnings with no way to ever clear the noise, for every Smart Import vertical | **Merged into `V6-F55`** (`V62`) — not tracked separately |

Proposed fix (not implemented, per explicit instruction): two additions, mirroring what already exists for suggestions and only for suggestions. (1) A dismiss control on `detailView()` for `isDismissibleWarning`-shaped entries, the same interaction `suggestionView()` already offers, adapted to address an item (`state.items`) instead of a suggestion. (2) Extend the item-edit save handler to compute the changed-field set (the same way `updateSuggestion` already does) and call `reconcileStaleNeedsReview` against the item's own `details.needsReviewFields`/`warnings` before saving — currently only the pre-approval suggestion form does this; the item edit form never has. Both are additive, reusing existing, already-tested mechanisms rather than inventing new ones.

*The full frozen processing/privacy, universal source-landing, source-relative completeness/evidence and bounded external-enrichment contracts, the approved first vertical slice definition, the first-slice acceptance benchmark, explicit exclusions and the Alpha 0.6 release gate are unchanged from prior revisions and are preserved verbatim below.*

## Approved scope addition — Insurance Smart Import (0.6.7) — GO after five acceptance sessions; `0.6.7.4` live in Production

Approved as real MVP scope by the Product Owner on 2026-08-31: Insurance is a full Smart Import vertical slice, architecturally identical to Hotel, Flight and Attraction/Event — its own `insurance` item type, PDF/image extraction, the same `Source`/`ProcessingAttempt`/`ExtractedDraft`/`Proposal`/`Attachment` pipeline, the same two-choice Global Add flow and `Attach only`/`Attach and extract` split — not a manual-only field set and not a reduced treatment.

- **Sequencing:** `0.6.7`, immediately following `0.6.6` (through its `0.6.6.1`–`0.6.6.3` correction cycle, closed and live in Production) in the Alpha 0.6.x lineage, before Alpha 0.7 (Shared Trips) begins.
- **Status: entry gate PASSED, `2026-09-09`.** `docs/roadmap/INSURANCE_SMART_IMPORT_DISCOVERY.md` is genuinely `Document revision: V3`, `Status: FROZEN` — verified directly against the file on disk, not assumed from a handoff prompt's own claim (see the `V81`/`V82` change-history rows for the full verification record: a first handoff, `2026-09-07`, described the document as already `V3`/Frozen when the actual file was `V1`/`DRAFT` with 6 benchmark documents and 5 open questions; implementation was correctly held rather than proceeding against that mismatch; a corrected handoff, `2026-09-09`, supplied the genuine document and all 8 benchmark files directly on disk, independently verified by SHA-256 before use). Frozen contents: all five `V1` open questions resolved and confirmed by the Product Owner (`startAt`/`endAt` added to `TYPE_FIELDS.insurance`; `phone` resolves to the emergency line, a claims/office line goes to `notes`; sum insured goes to `notes`, not a new field; Decision 7 confirmed PDF/image-only, no URL scope); two additional real benchmark documents supplied (`INS-007` DiveAssure, `INS-008` RentalCover.com/Cover Genius), both dedicated/activity- or asset-linked policies rather than whole-trip coverage, surfacing one new architecture question — **Section 3a**, resolved via a `/product-management:brainstorm` session: `ITEM_TYPES.insurance.schedule` stays fixed at `'entire'` for every insurance item, whole-trip or dedicated alike (Option A — the Product Owner explicitly rejected Option B, a per-item date-aware schedule: *"I don't want they both will appear on the Today page... I want to be able to reach the insurance both from the activity/event/item or from the insurance basket"*); a new `relatedItemIds` array field, added to `TYPE_FIELDS.insurance` only, lets the Product Owner manually link an insurance item to one or more other items it covers, with the reverse direction (an item showing which insurance covers it) a computed lookup at render time, not a stored field on any other type — no new "basket" surface needed, the existing Insurance category tile in Trip Center already serves that role.
- **Implemented, `2026-09-09` (`V82`).** Data model: `startAt`/`endAt` and (insurance-only) `relatedItemIds` added to `TYPE_FIELDS.insurance`; `ITEM_TYPES.insurance.schedule` left unchanged at `'entire'`. Extraction contract: `insuranceImportSchema`/`insuranceSystemPrompt`/`analyze_insurance` (`netlify/functions/`), `src/insurance-import-adapter.js` (provider resolves to the underwriting insurer not a selling agent/broker; phone resolves to the emergency hotline specifically; a new `details.insuredPersons` structured array for per-person name/ID/DOB, with a `V6-F66`-style field-aware merge so a second attached document never silently replaces the first's insured people). UI: a two-way `relatedItemIds` link (`relatedItemsPanel`/`insuranceCoveringItem`, `src/v5-app.js`) — tappable in both directions, no type restriction on what can be linked. All 8 `INS-001`–`INS-008` benchmark cases have named, passing regression tests (`tests/v6-7-insurance-import.mjs`, 21 tests, `INS-006` as the first-class wild-card). Full quality gate passes: 183/183 test assertions across 27 files, clean build.

Promoted the exact `qa`-verified `dist` artifact via `netlify deploy --dir dist --prod --no-build` — no rebuild, avoiding any risk of environment drift reintroducing `V6-F75`–`V6-F87` through an unrelated dependency change. New deploy: `6aa255f515287d85356c634e`. Verified live on the actual Production URL via a fresh, cache-bypassed fetch: `build-version` reads `0.6.7.4-4fe16e5c`. **Production is now on `0.6.7.4` (build `0.6.7.4-4fe16e5c`, deploy `6aa255f515287d85356c634e`), superseding `0.6.6.3`.**
- **`V6-F74`** (registered `V79`, folded into `0.6.7`'s scope per `V80` for scheduling only — genuinely independent of the Insurance vertical) was implemented separately in `V81`, before this vertical's own entry-gate mismatch was resolved, and is now confirmed **Fixed, verified** — Product Owner retest directly on Production (build `0.6.7.4-4fe16e5c`, 2026-09-10) against the original Panvaree Resort item, PASS. See the `V88` status line above for full detail.

## Frozen processing and privacy contract

- `Attach only` is local and invokes no AI. `Analyze and propose` and `Attach and extract` are explicit per-attempt processing actions, preceded by a concise first-use disclosure for that account.
- Raw source bytes, transient OCR/layout artifacts and retrieved page content are deleted from the processing path immediately after completion/failure and no later than 24 hours after receipt. Eligible providers may not use FamilyTrips data for model training.
- The Alpha 0.6 original remains locally openable and linked to its Hotel until explicit confirmed deletion. Original-source cloud/cross-device synchronization is deferred to Alpha 0.7.
- Only the target Trip `Owner` may invoke paid AI processing in Alpha 0.6, with server-side invitation/ownership checks, attempt attribution and a per-user quota. `Editor` processing permission is deferred to Alpha 0.7.
- Passport, national-identity and visa documents are not processed by AI in Alpha 0.6. The Hotel extractor must not extract or retain a full payment-card number or security code.
- The 24-hour processing-copy rule is independent of revoked-account data: revocation still triggers immediate blocking and the separately approved 30-day quarantine/deletion-approval lifecycle.

## Frozen universal source-landing contract

- The same landing interaction applies to all current and future item types; support for that interaction does not imply that Alpha 0.6 can extract every type.
- Global Add presents **Analyze and propose item** as the default-selected path and **Save source only** as the no-AI path. Processing begins only after explicit confirmation and any required first-use disclosure.
- **Save source only** stores the unassigned original in the non-category Intake Inbox.
- Failed, unsupported or insufficient analysis creates no empty/generic item. The original remains in Intake Inbox with an explicit state and **Retry** / **Delete** actions. Retry now shows a loading indicator (`V6-F42` Closed).
- A successful analysis creates editable proposals only and requires approval before final creation.
- Every existing item exposes **Attach only** and **Attach and extract**. Until that item's extraction contract is supported, the second action reports a truthful unsupported state and keeps the source attached without changing structured data.
- Alpha 0.6 implements and benchmarks extraction for Hotel, with Flight (`0.6.5`) and Attraction/Event (`0.6.6`) approved as further vertical slices.
- Alpha 0.6 includes the minimum non-category Intake Inbox required by this contract.
- **No manual item-type picker is specified by this contract.** Removed in the `0.6.5` fix pass, confirmed working on retest.
- **Top-level Add options, revised:** `Manual` / `Document` / `Link` / `QR` / `JSON`. Confirmed working on retest. The per-item attach modal now matches — confirmed on retest (`V6-F38` Closed). **Note: `Manual` from the global floating "+" does not currently support creating a Task directly — new Alpha 0.7 item, see below.**
- **Today** now falls back to showing out-of-Trip-range items when it would otherwise be empty — confirmed working exactly as specified via `FL-008`. A smarter version is a `0.7` backlog item, not a `0.6.5` blocker.

## Frozen source-relative completeness and evidence contract

- Smart Import has no fixed absolute minimum field count. A sparse source may produce a sparse draft; a field absent from the source remains empty and is not fabricated. Confirmed again via `FL-007`: an invalid source time value is correctly left blank rather than guessed.
- Every readable travel-relevant value present in the source must appear in the review draft with evidence. For Hotel, dates, arrival/departure or check-in/check-out times/windows, location/address/map data, phone and booking/confirmation number are explicit no-omission blockers whenever present. For Flight, see `FLIGHT_SMART_IMPORT_DISCOVERY.md`. For the Attraction/Event proposal under discovery, see `ATTRACTION_EVENT_SMART_IMPORT_DISCOVERY.md` (Restaurant's shelved equivalent: `RESTAURANT_SMART_IMPORT_DISCOVERY_V1.md`). `FL-007`'s baggage-allowance evidence and airline-reference ambiguity both confirmed correct on final retest (`V6-F44` Closed).
- A complete, unambiguous source address is the exact Hotel Location. A map link or coordinates are not required for source-extraction exactness. Flight route-type locations confirmed resolving correctly (`V6-F41` Closed); label wording is a minor Beta 0.8 candidate.
- If explicit property location is absent, the exact source Hotel name is the derived Location-field/search candidate.
- The named Hotel fields are not a ceiling.
- Every proposal requires a meaningful evidence-grounded title.
- Missing-in-source values do not block approval. Unreadable, ambiguous or conflicting present values stay visible as unresolved/needs-review. **Needs-review evidence must be correctly scoped to the actual field it concerns** — `V6-F43` confirmed fixed. **A field's needs-review warning must clear/re-evaluate whenever that field's value changes, by any means** — `V6-F35` confirmed fixed on both arrival and departure fields.
- Each proposed value carries direct field evidence and provenance.
- **An ambiguous or unresolved field must never block unrelated fields from populating.** `V6-F34` — confirmed fixed.
- **Duplicate detection must preserve non-empty trusted values, including a manually-resolved direction/location and any field populated as a result.** `V6-F33`/`V6-F39` — confirmed fixed on retest.

## Frozen bounded external-enrichment policy

- Source extraction is completed first. External enrichment never substitutes for source-relative completeness.
- Each processing attempt may retrieve at most three evidence-linked public pages and perform at most one structured place lookup.
- Every Hotel place lookup starts property-name-first; a generic park/dam/lake/island/city/region result is rejected or left `needs_review`.
- Every enriched field is labelled separately and retains field-level provenance.
- Login/session/CAPTCHA/credential-protected pages and broad crawling are excluded.
- The initial benchmark target is approximately USD 0.10 or less in variable third-party cost per import.

## Approved first vertical slice — Hotel

The first Alpha 0.6 technical spike and integrated implementation support text/scanned hotel PDFs, hotel images, public official hotel URLs, a new-Hotel proposal from global Add, and `Attach only`/`Attach and extract` from an existing Hotel. This is a complete architecture slice: immutable source identity, processing attempts, field evidence, proposal review, explicit approval, duplicate handling, multi-source relationships, attachment ownership, partial/failure states, authorization and audit/cost/latency measurement all apply.

## Required capabilities

### One source pipeline

- Camera capture, gallery image, PDF/file and URL all enter one source-ingestion pipeline with immutable original-source identity and traceable processing attempts.
- Manual item creation and validated `familytrips.import` / `FTI1` / `FTI1C` remain independent trusted paths.
- URL ingestion retrieves actual allowed page content through an approved server-side acquisition path.

### Reviewable structured result

- Every extracted value carries field-level evidence, source reference, extraction method and confidence.
- Values absent from the source or unsupported by direct evidence remain empty.
- Extraction creates a proposal only.
- Duplicate detection runs before approval and preserves non-empty trusted values. Confirmed on retest.
- An ambiguous field's unresolved status must never withhold unrelated fields. Confirmed fixed.

### Existing-item attachment

- `Attach only` is a reliable foundation.
- `Attach and extract` uses the same Smart Import pipeline and returns field-level proposals requiring approval.

### Safety and observability

- The user can cancel, retry, reject or keep the original source without accepting extracted data.
- Processing state distinguishes queued, processing, partial, needs review, failed, attached and rejected outcomes.
- Offline behavior is explicit.

## First-slice acceptance benchmark

The first-slice benchmark uses real, Product Owner-approved Hotel examples covering `H-001` through `H-005` plus the partial/missing-reference/companion/duplicate matrix rows — full detail preserved in `SMART_IMPORT_REDESIGN_DISCOVERY_V22.md`. The Flight benchmark (`FL-001`–`FL-008`) is preserved in `FLIGHT_SMART_IMPORT_DISCOVERY.md`. **All 8 Flight cases pass in QA — GO. Production is live and the real-import test is confirmed.** The Attraction/Event benchmark is drafted but open in `ATTRACTION_EVENT_SMART_IMPORT_DISCOVERY.md`, pending real Product Owner-supplied documents. The Restaurant benchmark drafted earlier is shelved in `RESTAURANT_SMART_IMPORT_DISCOVERY_V1.md` (`V2`).

## Explicit exclusions

- Multi-trip, sharing/synchronization, Shopping expansion, first-class Transport, task-model expansion, broad visual redesign and the remaining MVP-completion backlog stay in Alpha 0.7.
- Boarding-pass/Flight extraction is approved scope, sequenced to `0.6.5`. Attraction/Event ticket extraction is approved scope, sequenced to `0.6.6` (retargeted from Restaurant — `V49`; Activity was never actually blocked by the data model, see correction note above). Insurance extraction is approved scope, sequenced to `0.6.7` (see the "Approved scope addition — Insurance Smart Import (0.6.7)" section above; delivered — `0.6.7.4` GO'd after five acceptance-session rounds and is live in Production as of `V87`). Restaurant, Transport and generic multi-type extraction remain excluded from `0.6.x` — Restaurant is shelved, not blocked, and is a real candidate for a later slice.
- The external-model `familytrips.import` and QR contract remains a supported fallback and test oracle.

## Alpha 0.6 release gate

Alpha 0.6 receives GO only after the approved end-to-end Hotel vertical slice passes its benchmark, all supported Hotel sources share the same proposal and ownership invariants, the bounded enrichment and measured accuracy/cost/latency gates pass, failure branches create no misleading data, and the exact tested build passes privacy, offline, schema, duplicate, persistence and regression gates. Flight (`0.6.5`) has its own equivalent gate, defined in `FLIGHT_SMART_IMPORT_DISCOVERY.md` — **PASSED against QA. Production is live and the real-import verification is confirmed. `0.6.5` is fully closed.** Attraction/Event (`0.6.6`) will get its own equivalent gate once its type contract and benchmark reach Frozen status in `ATTRACTION_EVENT_SMART_IMPORT_DISCOVERY.md` and its own independent Production promotion once implemented and accepted.

# Alpha 0.7 — MVP Completion and Hardening

*Full required-work, release-blocker and exit-criteria detail for each individually-listed item below is unchanged from prior revisions — see `MVP_DELIVERY_HISTORY_V1.md` or the prior published version of this document for the original acceptance criteria. What's new this revision: the Product Owner sequenced Alpha 0.7's full sixteen-item binding scope into eight `0.7.x` candidates (2026-09-10), following the same one-vertical-slice-per-candidate discipline established through Alpha 0.6 — each candidate gets its own implementation, quality gate and executable acceptance session before the next begins, rather than one large combined release. Expected lifecycle numbering, following the `V4`→Alpha 0.4 / `V5`→Alpha 0.5 / `V6`→Alpha 0.6 pattern: Alpha 0.7 becomes the `V7` package lifecycle (`version/V7`, `docs/versions/V7/`) — to be confirmed when `0.7.1` planning actually begins, per the standard's own naming-convention rules, not asserted as final here. Alpha 0.7's binding scope is now **seventeen items**, not sixteen: `V93` (2026-09-10) added participant management — declared binding Alpha 0.7 scope back in `MVP_DELIVERY_HISTORY_V1.md`'s `V24` but missing from the `V90` sequencing pass — to `0.7.4`'s scope list, alongside Destination Countries and Transport. The `V7` package-lifecycle convention is no longer merely expected — `V92` confirmed it via `git merge-base` when `version/V7` was branched for `0.7.1`.*

**Sequencing rationale:** items 1–4 below (small, self-contained) go first as low-risk wins with zero dependency on the sharing/sync architecture. Items 5–7 build the core Shared-Trip ownership/access/sync engine, in the order each depends on the last — this is the single largest remaining engineering effort in the MVP and the reason it's sequenced as its own dedicated stretch rather than interleaved. Items 8–9 are independent content-model gaps (Destination Countries, Transport) with no dependency on sharing, sequenced alongside/after the core build. Items 10–13 are the administrative/permission layer that only becomes meaningful once the core sharing model exists. Items 14–16 are lower-urgency standalone features. This ordering is a Product Owner decision, not a fixed technical requirement — later resequencing remains possible if priorities shift. Item 17 (participant management, added `V93`) is the same kind of independent content-model gap as items 8–9 and is grouped with them in `0.7.4`, rather than renumbering the rest of this list.

**`0.7.1` — Small independent fixes (no sync/sharing dependency):**
- **Task creation from the global floating "+".** Currently, creating a Task is only reachable from within the Preparations/Tasks section; the global Add ("+") flow does not offer Task as a creatable type. This is a genuine gap, not previously covered by any existing finding (`V5-F09` is a different, category-preset concern — it's about prefilling the current category's type when using Add *from within* that category, not about which types are available at all from the global entry point). Fix: Task must be a selectable type from the global floating "+", the same as any other manually-created item type.
- **Today page: Packing list shortcut, not individual items.** Open preparation Tasks already surface correctly in Today's pre-trip Quick Access (`V5-F13`, implemented Alpha 0.5.2 — up to 3 open tasks, overdue → today → nearest due → undated, with an "All tasks" overflow link). Packing/equipment list items currently do not appear in Today at all. Product Owner decision: do not add individual packing items into the same "top 3 urgent" Quick Access slots as Tasks — instead, add a shortcut link to the full Packing list (the same overflow-link pattern already used for "All tasks"), so packing stays visible and reachable pre-trip without crowding out the most urgent tasks.
- **Category-level Add presets the current category's item type.** Using Add from within a specific Trip Center category should default/prefill that category's item type, rather than requiring the same generic type selection as the global "+" (distinct from `0.7`'s global-"+" Task-creation gap above, and from the already-resolved `V5-F09` category-preset finding — full original criteria in `MVP_DELIVERY_HISTORY_V1.md`).
- **Task priority (High/Normal/Low) + due-date/priority filters.** Adds an explicit priority field to Tasks plus filtering by due date and priority, on top of the existing Preparation Task detail model (`V34`/`MVP_DELIVERY_HISTORY_V1.md`).
- **Implemented, `2026-09-10` (`V92`).** All four items above implemented against their stated acceptance criteria on `version/V7` (branched from `version/V6`'s tip `91dfeae`) — see `V92`'s own change-history row for full per-item root cause/implementation detail. 8 new named regression tests (`tests/v7-1-small-fixes.mjs`). Full quality gate passes: 206/206 test assertions across 28 files, clean build.
- **Deployed to `qa`, `2026-09-10` (`V42`, `VERSION_PACKAGE_AUTHORING_STANDARD`).** Build `0.7.1-5218c8f5` (`HEAD` moved from the `91dfeae2` implementation commit to `5218c8f` after the doc-only `V93` sync commit — no source file differs between the two), deploy `6aa2f10d4357d85178e7bbcd`, exact `dist` artifact via `--no-build`.
- **Acceptance session GO, `2026-09-10` (`V94`).** 5/5 tests PASS (`docs/versions/V7/0.7.1/EXECUTABLE_TEST_SESSION_V1.md`). Two non-blocking findings, both dispositioned directly: `V7-F01` (global-"+" Hotel default, no category context — Accepted, no action) and `V7-F02` (no open-tasks-only filter — Accepted, backlog enhancement). **Cleared for Production promotion as a separate, explicit action** — not yet performed.
- **Promoted to Production, `2026-09-10` (`V95`).** Deploy `6aa2fdbc36dc5e6e540daace`, commit `ace38a0`, exact `qa`-verified artifact via `--no-build`. Verified live: `build-version` reads `0.7.1-5218c8f5` on `https://familytripz.netlify.app`. Rollback backup preserved (`production-promotion-backup-20260910-215707/`). **`0.7.1` is closed — Alpha 0.7's first candidate is live.**

**`0.7.2` — Multi-Trip ownership + Shared-Trip roles (the access-control AND datastore foundation — re-scoped `V97`):**
- **Multiple independently-owned Trips running in parallel, on a real server-side datastore.** The Product Owner's family Trip and any number of friends' own Trips, each fully isolated unless explicitly shared by its Owner. Confirmed, non-negotiable MVP scope (see "Product intent" above). **Re-scoped `V97`:** this requires standing up the actual cloud datastore (Firestore, confirmed `V97`) and migrating the entire read/write layer off synchronous `localStorage` — not merely activating access control on infrastructure that was assumed to already exist. This is the ownership-model AND storage-layer foundation every other item in this section, and `0.7.3`/`0.7.6`, depend on.
- **Shared-Trip access with Owner/Editor roles, enforced by Firestore security rules.** The same sharing/synchronization contract applies identically whether the Trip belongs to the Product Owner or to a friend — no reduced "friend-tier" version. Depends directly on the datastore above; role enforcement happens server-side via security rules, not client-hidden UI.
- **Design-first gate, added `V97`:** before any implementation, the Development Agent produces a concrete data-model/security-rules/migration design (Firestore collections/documents shape, security-rules logic for Owner/Editor enforcement, and the real migration plan for the existing Production Trip's `localStorage` data) for Product Owner review — mirroring the discovery-document-before-implementation discipline this project uses for AI Smart Import verticals, applied here for this candidate's own architectural weight, not because the standard's discovery-gate rule technically requires it. Implementation does not begin until this design is reviewed.

- **Implemented and deployed to `qa`, 2026-09-11–2026-09-12.** Full Firestore/Storage datastore built per the approved `DESIGN_V1.md`: `/trips/{tripId}` subcollection data model, Firestore/Storage security rules (application-invitation layer + Trip-level `ownerUid`/`editorEmails` roles), `config/appInvitations` seeded with the Product Owner-supplied list, one-time in-app verified migration with one-week retention, async client read/write path with real-time listeners, the minimal "add Editor by email" mechanism, a minimum-viable Trip switcher, and the Netlify Blobs ownership-check reconciliation — all per the four decisions recorded in `V99`.
- **Acceptance session GO, 2026-09-12 (`docs/versions/V7/0.7.2/EXECUTABLE_TEST_SESSION_V1.md`, `V9`).** 8/8 tests PASS after a nine-revision cycle: a session pause for two blocking findings (`V7-F03`, `V7-F04`, both fixed and confirmed before resuming), a NO-GO for two further blocking findings (`V7-F06`, `V7-F07`) whose first fix attempts did not survive retest and were corrected honestly, and two rounds of debug-tooling fixes before server-side rule enforcement could be verified live rather than merely asserted. `V7-F05` (Smart Import for Editors) resolved as **Accepted, deferred to `0.7.5`** — confirmed as originally scoped, not a gap. `V7-F08` (Storage-orphan cleanup on deletion) resolved as **Accepted, backlog** — non-blocking, **assigned to `0.7.6`** (`V101`). **Cleared for Production promotion.**
- **Promoted to Production, 2026-09-12.** Build `0.7.2-08d11fd8`, deploy `6aa4f6175a330d9678e4bc3d`, confirmed live via a fresh cache-bypassed fetch. Rollback backup preserved (`production-promotion-backup-20260912-094913/`). **`0.7.2` is closed** — the first candidate in this project's history with a real server-side datastore live in Production, replacing the single-device `localStorage` model entirely.
- **Post-promotion process fix, 2026-09-12–2026-09-14 (not `0.7.2` scope, not a code change):** `qa` and Production now share one live Firestore/Storage backend, and acceptance testing was using the same real Google accounts as Production — silently commingling real and test Trip data. Resolved with a dedicated "QA Test Trip" convention rather than a separate `qa` Firebase project (the latter considered and explicitly declined as disproportionate for now; flagged to revisit once `0.7.3`'s two-device sync testing begins). Now a standing rule (`VERSION_PACKAGE_AUTHORING_STANDARD_V54`, "Shared-backend testing discipline").
- **`0.7.2.1` correction authorized, 2026-09-15 (`V103`) — not `0.7.2` reopened, a new narrow correction package.** Discovered during `0.7.3`'s own design verification: `save()`'s serialization can silently lose offline edits made after the first, already live in Production. Product Owner decision: fix now rather than fold into `0.7.3`. See `0.7.3`'s own section below for the finding detail and decision rationale (recorded there since it surfaced from `0.7.3`'s design work, not a `0.7.2` acceptance finding).
- **`0.7.2.1` closed — live in Production, 2026-09-15 (`V104`).** Single-device acceptance session GO 2/2 (`docs/versions/V7/0.7.2.1/EXECUTABLE_TEST_SESSION_V1.md`). Build `0.7.2.1-19ff2b6b`, deploy `6aa98f6ebdad7a22ba042864`, byte-identical `qa`→Production promotion, rollback backup preserved. Two-device validation of the same fix still occurs later via `0.7.3`'s own Test F, per this correction's deliberately narrow scope.

**`0.7.3` — Sync engine + two-device collaboration (the core sync build; its acceptance test genuinely requires two real devices/accounts, not just single-device dev testing):** **Builds on `0.7.2`'s Firestore foundation (`V97`)** — near-real-time sync is likely realized substantially through Firestore's own real-time listeners rather than a bespoke sync engine, but that's `0.7.2`'s design document's call to inform, not asserted here.
- **Near-real-time online synchronization and offline-change synchronization after reconnect.** The largest, highest-risk single piece of engineering remaining in the MVP.
- **Two-user, two-device collaboration on any shared Trip.** In practice this is the acceptance criterion for the sync engine working correctly, not separable functionality — sequenced together for that reason.
- **Design-request kickoff prepared, 2026-09-15 (`V102`).** Going design-first from the outset (see Status line above) — `FAMILY_TRIPS_ALPHA_0_7_3_SCOPE_V1.md` and `PROMPT_FOR_CLAUDE_CODE_0_7_3_DESIGN_REQUEST_V1.md` authored, requesting a design document covering conflict resolution, offline queueing, sync-status visibility, two-device testing protocol, and current-state verification of `0.7.2`'s Firestore listeners. No implementation begins until the Product Owner reviews the resulting design.
- **Design delivered, reviewed and approved, 2026-09-15 (`V103`).** `DESIGN_V1.md` verified `0.7.2`'s current-state code directly and surfaced two urgent findings, both already live in Production: **§0.1** — an offline edit made after the first can be silently lost on reload (`save()`'s serialization never lets a second offline write reach Firestore's own durable queue); **§0.2** — a live remote update can wipe an open, unsubmitted edit form (`render()`'s unconditional full-DOM replacement). Recommendations approved as-is: field-level `updateDoc()` merge writes (§2.2, same-field-same-record stays last-write-wins with attribution); decoupled `save()` serialization (§3.2 — the §0.1 fix); buffered remote updates while a form is open (§2.3 — the §0.2 fix); a minimal pending-sync indicator (§4, Product Owner's choice among three options presented); a concrete seven-test two-device acceptance protocol (§5); confirmation the QA Test Trip convention remains sufficient, no separate Firebase project needed (§6). **§0.1's fix authorized as a fast, separate `0.7.2.1` correction ahead of this candidate's own timeline** (see `0.7.2`'s section above) — `0.7.3`'s own implementation builds on top of it rather than reimplementing it, covering §2.2, §2.3, §4, and the executable two-device test session (§5). `PROMPT_FOR_CLAUDE_CODE_0_7_2_1_CORRECTION_V1.md` and `PROMPT_FOR_CLAUDE_CODE_0_7_3_IMPLEMENTATION_KICKOFF_V1.md` both authored.
- **Implemented, deployed to `qa`, and acceptance session GO, 2026-09-20 (`docs/versions/V7/0.7.3/EXECUTABLE_TEST_SESSION_V1.md`, `V1`).** All seven two-device tests (A–G) plus the sync-status-visibility check PASS — field-level merge writes (§2.2), open-form buffering (§2.3), and the minimal pending-sync indicator (§4) all confirmed working under genuine two-device offline/reconnect conditions, including by the Product Owner testing without computer access for several tests (recorded in-session, written to the repo once reconnected). Three non-blocking findings registered, none blocking GO: Flight map-link staleness after editing location (medium-high, 6/10); a stuck "מנתח…" analyzing badge (a real regression introduced by this candidate's own open-form-buffering fix); a brief empty-trip flash on app load. **Promoted to Production, 2026-09-20.** Build `0.7.3-791b640c`, deploy `6aafd840cea964c83e644896`, byte-identical `qa`→Production promotion, rollback backup preserved (`production-promotion-backup-20260920-155614/`, prior state `0.7.2.1-19ff2b6b`). **`0.7.3` is closed** — the sync engine, field-level merge writes, open-form protection, and pending-sync indicator are all live for real users.
- **`0.7.3.1` correction, 2026-09-20 — the three findings above, scoped and closed.** Product Owner decision: group all three into one narrow correction, mirroring `0.7.2.1`'s pattern, rather than split further. **Note on process:** this correction's own kickoff prompt was issued without this assistant's involvement — no `PROMPT_FOR_CLAUDE_CODE_0_7_3_1_CORRECTION_V1.md` exists in this assistant's own authored-handoff record, unlike `0.7.2.1` and every prior correction. Root causes found and fixed: Finding 1 — `mapSearchLocation()` always preferred the extraction-only `departureAirport` field over the user-editable `location` field; fixed so an explicit edit now wins. Finding 2 — a stale buffered remote snapshot (captured while `0.7.3`'s own open-form-buffering fix held a modal open) could be re-applied after a fresher direct update, silently reverting it; fixed by clearing any leftover buffer on a direct apply and skipping the buffer's flush while a local write is in flight. Finding 3 — a neutral loading state now shows until `boot()` resolves, instead of the empty-Trip call-to-action showing by default first. **Acceptance session GO (`docs/versions/V7/0.7.3.1/EXECUTABLE_TEST_SESSION_V1.md`, `V1`), 2026-09-20.** All three tests PASS. Test 3's initial retest showed a FAIL (empty-Trip flash on a brand-new session); the Development Agent found no code path allowing it and correctly diagnosed a Service-Worker cache-propagation artifact instead (the first post-deploy visit served by the old, pre-fix SW's cached shell — a deliberate frozen offline-launch invariant, not something to alter for a cosmetic fix); the Product Owner's own close-and-relaunch retest came back clean, confirming the diagnosis without any code change. **Promoted to Production, 2026-09-20.** Build `0.7.3.1-f83cbb80`, deploy `6aafe89469914077f4a8089a`, byte-identical promotion, rollback backup preserved (`production-promotion-backup-20260920-170603/`, prior state `0.7.3-791b640c`) — low-risk, no data model or security rules changed. **`0.7.3.1` is closed.**

**`0.7.4` — Independent content-model gaps (no sharing/sync dependency; useful for the real trip regardless of sequencing above):**
- **`V6-F82` — insurance reverse-lookup shows only the first linked insurance.** Moved here from `0.7.2` (`V97`) — better fit, independent of sharing/ownership work. An item linked from more than one Insurance item's `relatedItemIds` should surface every covering insurance on its own detail page's reverse-lookup ("🛡️ מבוטח באמצעות..."), not just the first one found. Low-severity, low-frequency display bug (`0.6.7.4` acceptance session Round 5; assigned to Alpha 0.7 per `V89`; slotted to `0.7.2` per `V91`, moved to `0.7.4` per `V97`). Likely fix is `insuranceCoveringItem` (`src/v5-app.js`) returning every matching insurance item instead of just the first — not yet confirmed by code inspection. A related enhancement idea — an icon marking items that already carry linked insurance — was raised in the same discussion and explicitly deferred to Beta, not this candidate.
- **`V7-F02` — Task filter has no "open tasks only" option.** Moved here from `0.7.2` (`V97`) — better fit, independent of sharing/ownership work. Registered during `0.7.1`'s own acceptance session (`V94`, 2026-09-10): closed/completed tasks remain visible under every combination of the existing priority/due-date filters, with no way to view only open tasks. Fix: add a status filter (or equivalent toggle) to `filterAndSortTasks`, combining with priority/due-date the same way those two already combine (AND, not OR); closed tasks remain reachable via an explicit "show all"/"include closed" choice, never silently hidden without an on-screen indication filtering is active.
- **One or more Destination Countries, as an ordered list.** Roadmap gap identified after Alpha 0.3; binding Alpha 0.7 Trip Settings scope (`MVP_DELIVERY_HISTORY_V1.md`).
- **First-class Transport items** for transfers, rail, ferry/boat, rental vehicles and other local transport — Transport already exists as a Trip Center category and Timeline/Today concept but lacks a first-class item model (`V33`/`MVP_DELIVERY_HISTORY_V1.md`).
- **Participant management — confirmed missing from the live app entirely; this is a from-scratch build, not a UI relocation.** `MVP_DELIVERY_HISTORY_V1.md`'s `V24` (2026-08-06) declared Trip Settings the canonical entry point for participant management and explicitly removed it from Trip Center, as binding Alpha 0.7 scope — but this was missing from the sixteen-item scope formalized in `V90`. Added here per Product Owner decision, `V92`/`V93` (2026-09-10). **Code-inspection sanity check, `V93`:** `settingsView()` (`src/v5-app.js`) has no participant UI at all (only trip name/dates, danger zone, about section); no `state.participants` array or participant data model exists in `src/operational-data.js` or `src/v5-app.js` (the only fields named "participants" are an unrelated free-text Item field and a static games-catalog field — neither the Alpha 0.3 domain model of name/role/phone/email/notes/avatar/active-status). Trip Center's participant UI appears to have been removed at some point without Trip Settings' replacement ever shipping. Whether any participant data persists server/storage-side beneath the missing UI has not been checked and should be confirmed during this item's own implementation scoping, not assumed either way. Acceptance criteria: Trip Settings exposes add/edit/deactivate participant actions with the full Alpha 0.3 field set (name, role/family relationship, optional phone/email/notes/avatar, active/inactive status); participants are assignable to items/documents/tickets/packing per the relationship model `MVP_DELIVERY_HISTORY_V1.md`'s Alpha 0.3 section describes; no standalone Trip Center participant UI is (re)introduced.

Three findings registered: the arrow-direction bug (Resolved/Closed same session, build `0.7.4-fe61afe4`); a leftover duplicate free-text "משתתפים" participants field still showing on item edit forms alongside `0.7.4`'s own participant-assignment control (Minor, scoped to a fast `0.7.4.1` correction); an enhancement request — auto-recognize and auto-link an attached document to its correct participant (Test 7, Product Owner's own words: "the system should recognize for which participant is it and link it to him/her") — deliberately left unscoped, registered as `V7-F09` below. Recommendations approved as-is: a unified `invitations/{email}` collection (`active`/`revoked`, two states not three) replacing `config/appInvitations`; an `editorHistory` audit subcollection alongside the untouched `editorEmails` array; quarantine enforced via an `isInvited()` rules helper that blocks Trips/Storage/Smart Import in one write, resolving open question 3 (does revocation strip Editor access elsewhere) for free; the 30-day check computed on demand, no scheduled job; a new `admin-delete-account.mjs` Netlify Function — this project's first Firebase Admin SDK credential — with explicit account-naming, server-side re-verification of the 30-day window, and owned-Trips-only deletion scope; two admin UI surfaces; Editor-level Smart Import authorization via a minimal `registerOrVerifyTrip` change plus retiring the env var in favor of the new collection. Two decisions: deletion stays in scope for this candidate, not pre-emptively split out; single hand-seeded admin account confirmed, no self-service promotion UI. `PROMPT_FOR_CLAUDE_CODE_0_7_5_IMPLEMENTATION_KICKOFF_V1.md` authored.
- **Implemented and committed, 2026-09-21 (`68c050c`, `V111`) — not yet deployed to `qa`.** This assistant independently verified the commit against `DESIGN_V1.md` before recording it: the file list and `admin-delete-account.mjs`'s actual code both confirmed matching. **One verification question flagged, not silently resolved either way:** this assistant's own attempt to run `tests/v7-5-sharing-admin.mjs` did not complete (hung with no output); isolated to `firebase-admin/auth`/`firestore`/`storage` each hanging on bare import in this assistant's sandbox specifically, while `firebase-admin/app` alone imports fine — plausibly an artifact of this assistant's own network-restricted verification environment rather than a real defect, but unconfirmed. `PROMPT_FOR_CLAUDE_CODE_0_7_5_TEST_SUITE_CLARIFICATION_V1.md` authored, asking for a fresh, reported test run before the quality-gate claim is treated as confirmed. **Two manual Product Owner actions remain outstanding regardless** (Firebase Console invitation seeding with `isAdmin:true`; generating and setting `FIREBASE_ADMIN_SERVICE_ACCOUNT`) — neither this assistant nor the Development Agent can perform them. Rules deliberately not yet published, to avoid locking out existing users before the `invitations` collection exists.
- **Deployed to `qa`, live and healthy, 2026-09-21 (`0.7.5-8508e0cc`, `V112`) — after two real bugs found and fixed during the deploy itself.** This assistant checked the repo commit history directly and found a fix (`6c2b656`) not mentioned in the relayed summary. Bug 1, not a code defect: `VITE_FIREBASE_API_KEY` had Netlify's secret-value flag set, masking the real key from any build outside Netlify's own systems — fixed by deleting and re-adding the variable without that flag. Bug 2, a real defect found live: `admin-delete-account.mjs` returned `502` on every invocation because `firebase-admin/auth` transitively requires a package (`jwks-rsa`) with its own unfixed upstream ESM/CommonJS interop bug, crashing only under Netlify's bundled Function runtime — fixed by replacing that one import with a direct REST call to Google's Identity Toolkit API, plus a regression-guard test. This resolves the verification question this assistant raised at `V111` — the sandbox hang it hit independently was very likely this same broken chain. Admin function now confirmed responding `401` to an unauthenticated request; sign-in confirmed working.

**Three more items added `V145`, 2026-09-28, all found during `0.7.9.2`'s acceptance session (Test 5), all non-blocking:**

- **The Trip's documents-bin list has no way to re-link a removed (unlinked) document back to an item.** Once a document is unlinked via `0.7.9.2`'s new remove action, the only place it's still visible is this list, with no affordance to attach it to an item again. Not yet scoped.
- **The documents-bin list gives no indication of which item a document is or was linked to.** Makes it hard to tell, from that view alone, whether a listed document is still attached to something or was unlinked. Not yet scoped.
- **The documents-bin list shows a non-functional "הסרה" (remove) button on each row.** Clicking it does nothing. Two open dispositions, not yet decided: remove the dead button entirely (a small fix, candidate for folding into Beta 0.8), or wire it to mean a full removal from the Trip — which would need its own linked-item warning, the same shape as `0.7.9.2`'s per-item ticket-coverage warning. ## 5. Beta Plan

# Beta 0.8 — Visual and Interaction System

**The visual/interaction design spec is complete and locked (`V143`, 2026-09-28)** — `FAMILYTRIPS_UI_IMPLEMENTATION_DESIGN_SPEC_V7.md`, dated 2026-08-25. This was never previously referenced in this section; found and corrected when this assistant re-checked the design review's status rather than assuming it from an earlier verbal summary. The spec is a real, disciplined token system — exact hex values, type scale, spacing/radius/shadow tokens, a named icon library (Tabler, outline), RTL/bidi rules, empty/loading/offline states, pressed/focus states, an explicit light-mode-only statement for dark mode, and a full seven-state Smart Import status-pill system. Every gap raised in `UI_SPEC_V3_REVIEW_2026-08-18.md` was closed by `V4` (same day) and the spec continued to `V7`. Beta 0.8 is not blocked on any design decision — the remaining work is implementing this already-finished spec across the app, plus the small carried-forward refinement items below.

Scope, confirmed by the Product Owner at `V143`: implement `FAMILYTRIPS_UI_IMPLEMENTATION_DESIGN_SPEC_V7.md` across the app, plus these items folded in as a natural fit (all small, all genuinely visual/interaction): `V6-F14` (Hotel review notes field scoped down), `V6-F16` (unified "Add document" source-type selector), the Flight route-location label wording note from `V42`, a one-click "accept new value" action for merge-conflict warnings (from `0.6.6` QA acceptance, `V54`/`V56`), `V6-F80` (needs-review "not correct" affordance, already explicitly targeted at Beta), `V7-F15` (unify the two ticket-related sections on an item's detail view), `V7-F16` (undo on a mistaken "confirm as ticket coverage"), and `CR-005` (no confirmation message when an Editor is removed from a Trip). Not yet formally scoped into a `FAMILY_TRIPS_BETA_0_8_SCOPE_V1.md` — that's this assistant's next step now that `0.7.9.2` is closed and live in Production (`V145`, 2026-09-28).

## 10. Process-rule pointers

Two process rules that were originally recorded directly in this delivery plan have a single canonical home now: `VERSION_PACKAGE_AUTHORING_STANDARD_V25.md`, under its "Responsibility split" and "Controlled-document revision rule" sections respectively. The original wording, preserved for the record, is in `MVP_DELIVERY_HISTORY_V1.md`.
